# Holocron — Architecture, Routes & Hooks Reference

> Updated 2026-07-14. Read from codebase; update when structure changes.
> For full design token and color reference see **[docs/design-system.md](design-system.md)**.

---

## Route Map (`src/app/`)

| Route | File | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `/` | `page.tsx` | Landing / join page |
| `/create` | `page.tsx` | Character creator wizard — reads `?campaign=` query param |
| `/character/[id]` | `page.tsx` | Character sheet (tabbed view) — shell that mounts `PlayerHUDDesktop` |
| `/character/[id]/talents` | `talents/page.tsx` | Full-screen talent surface — vertical rail of destinations (one per owned spec + "Signature Abilities" + one per owned Force power, plus a "+ New Force Power" browse entry), main content area shows exactly one at a time (`contentView: 'tree' \| 'sig' \| 'force'` local state, Prompt 7b, extended to `'force'` in Prompt F3) rather than the tree with the sig section always scrolled below it; reads `?gm=1` and `?spec=` query params; replaced `HudTalentTreeModal` (retired). Switching destinations does not change `activeSpecKey`/`activeForcePowerKey` — each destination's content stays tied to whichever spec/power was last selected. Data comes from `useTalentSurfaceData`, not `useCharacterData` (Prompt 7a) — a lean, route-scoped hook so this route doesn't refetch the full character sheet; Force power data (`allForcePowers`, `buildForcePowerTree`, `forceRating`, `handlePurchaseForceAbility`, `powerBrowseList`) was added to that hook in Prompt F3. **Tree-first visual pass:** the ~300px mega header was replaced by a two-row **command bar** (~86px single-line / ~96–101px when the skills row wraps). Row 1 = career line + spec title (`.megaTitle`, same gradient/display treatment, now `--text-h3` instead of the 40→150px clamp) · separator · XP instrument (value over an "XP AVAIL" label) · Exit. Row 2 is its own full-width line carrying the complete career-skills list, and is deliberately **never** clamped, truncated or ellipsised — sharing a flex row with anything else is exactly what truncated skills in the reviewed mockup. Verified against the longest real string in the dataset (Technician + Retired Clone Trooper, 13 skills / 180 chars): 2 lines at 1280/1440, 1 line at 1920, zero truncation, no horizontal page scroll. The **ghost watermark moved out of the header into the tree stage**, behind the plaques; since `TalentTree`'s card is opaque, its background is now `var(--tree-card-bg, <HUD.bg>)` and only this route's stage sets `--tree-card-bg: transparent` — every other mount (preview modal, creator) leaves the variable unset and keeps the original opaque surface, so the indirection costs them nothing. The **rail is promoted to a console** at 218px (was 208px): display-font entries, active entry marked by a glowing gold edge bar (`.railEntryActive::before`) over an accent-tinted bed, START badge kept, ◈ on Signature Abilities and ✦ on Force entries, both "+ NEW" affordances as dashed ghosts (`.railGhostBtn`). **Plaque geometry scaled with it, and two hardcoded numbers had to move with it.** `.plaque` has no height of its own — it is `position:absolute; inset:10px` inside its grid cell, so the resting plaque is always `rowHeight − 20`; `gridTemplateRows` went `repeat(5, 160px)` → `repeat(5, 178px)` (140px → **158px** resting, live-confirmed) with `columnGap: 36` / `rowGap: 44` giving the ~56px/~64px visual separation (visual gap = grid gap + 2×inset). Header text stepped to `--text-body-sm`, body stays `--text-label`. Connector bars and the energy-trace chip **self-correct** — both measure live rects under `useConnectorLayout`'s `ResizeObserver` (verified: 20 bars, 4 active trace bars, hover-expand 158→208px after the resize). The two that do **not** self-correct, both in `TalentSurface.tsx` and both re-measured rather than scaled arithmetically: the bottom-row hover reserve `marginBottom` 160 → **220** (worst real bottom-row talent is cell 4-1, un-clamped at `scale(1.05)` = 363px against a 158px resting cell = 205px overshoot, was 148.1px) and the loading skeleton `minHeight` 908 → **1192** (measured tree-card box; keeps the skeleton→tree swap free of layout shift). 3 tiers fit without scrolling at 1440×900, 5 at 2560×1440. **Line clamp — fixed, and the clamp must stay on the inner span.** `.body`'s clamp was inert for the life of the component. The cause was **not** Lightning CSS (an earlier note here blamed it; the emitted chunk carries the declaration verbatim — that diagnosis was wrong). The real cause: `.body`'s only direct child is `RichText`'s single wrapping `<span>`, and `-webkit-box` lays out *children* as boxes — with exactly one child box there is nothing for `-webkit-line-clamp` to cut at, so the declaration computes (Chromium blockifies it to `display: flow-root`) but never bounds the height. Live-measured pre-fix on the Scout tree: Bolster `scrollHeight 194` vs `clientHeight 106`, hard-clipped mid-word. Short descriptions simply never reached the ceiling, which is why some plaques looked fine and others cut mid-word — **nothing was clamping anywhere**. Fix: `.body` becomes a plain `display: block; overflow: hidden` box (keeping `flex: 1`, so plaque geometry is unchanged) and the clamp moves to `.body > span` at **4 lines**. Standard `line-clamp` was tested and does **not** work here (the box just grew to fit all content), so the `-webkit-` form is required. The hover release also had to move: `.plaque:hover .body > span { display: block; -webkit-line-clamp: unset }` — `display: block` is what actually drops the `-webkit-box` line-boxing; `-webkit-line-clamp: unset` alone would not. Verified across the talents route (20 plaques, all states), the Force power tree, and the SpecSelectorList preview: max 4 lines everywhere, no text escaping any plaque, plain-text and inline-markup descriptions clamping identically. Native clamping truncates at *character* granularity, so the ellipsis can land inside a word (`…Decreas…`) — accepted deliberately, consistent with every other clamp in the app; word-boundary truncation would require per-plaque JS measurement. **`.fc-fx-desc` (globals.css) had the identical defect** — measured `scrollHeight 270` vs `clientHeight 32` — and was fixed the same way, including its `.fc-fx-row.is-expanded` release. `.fc-pow-desc.is-clamped` shares the structure and is very likely inert too, but its content fit its box in every case reachable, so it is unproven and left alone; `.ptt-name` wraps plain text and is expected to work, unverified. Mega-header (career line → title → ghost watermark → meta row) plays a once-per-route-load GSAP stagger reveal on first paint (Prompt 9, guarded by a `useRef` flag keyed off `shellReady`, not re-fired by later re-renders) — the four refs were **re-pointed** to the command bar's career line / title / skills row and the stage's watermark, with beat count, duration (0.3s), stagger (0.09s) and reduced-motion gating all unchanged; live-sampled at 14 partial frames with the original career→title→skills ordering intact. Because the watermark now lives in the stage it is absent under `contentView === 'force'`, so the timeline degrades to three beats there — already handled by its pre-existing `.filter(el => !!el)`, not a bug; the tree's own tier-by-tier entrance (`runTreeEntrance`, `buildTalentTree.ts`) is a separate concern owned by `TalentTree.tsx` and re-fires on every rail spec-switch — `ForcePowerTree.tsx` does NOT have an equivalent entrance (out of scope, confirmed in Prompt F4's audit; it does have its own hover energy-trace and purchase ceremony, see that component's own entry). `BuySpecButton`'s `onBuy` awaits `handleBuySpecialization` and, on success, renders `<PurchaseCeremony>` announcing the spec + any newly-granted career skills (see that component's own entry). |
| `/gm` | `page.tsx` | GM dashboard — reads `?campaign=` query param (~3,000+ lines — GOD COMPONENT) |
| `/gm/mapforge` | `page.tsx` | Map Forge — AI image generate → edit flow for campaign maps |
| `/table` | `page.tsx` | Table Display — full-screen map + initiative strip for in-person sessions; reads `?campaign=` query param; no auth gate; opened from the ⛶ Table Display arc in `MapToolsRadial` |
| `/wireframe` | `page.tsx` | Dev prototype page — UI component showcase, not linked in production |
| `/api/fal` | `route.ts` | POST → fal.ai gpt-image-2 image generation (server-side proxy) |
| `/api/fal-edit` | `route.ts` | POST → fal.ai gpt-image-2 multi-image edit / inpainting |
| `/api/anthropic` | `route.ts` | POST → Anthropic API proxy (keeps `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` server-side) |
| `/api/release-session` | `route.ts` | POST → deletes `character_sessions` row on player disconnect |

> **Removed routes** (previously documented, now gone):
> `/campaign/[id]`, `/player/[id]`, `/character/[id]/creator`, `/character/[id]/pdf`,
> `/group/[id]`, `/staging/[id]`, `/gm/combat` — all functionality absorbed into `/gm` and `/create`.

---

## Custom Hooks (`src/hooks/`)

### `useCharacterData(characterId)`
**Owner of all character state.** The most important hook.

- Fetches and owns: character, skills, talents, specializations, force abilities, weapons, armor, gear, critical injuries
- Exposes computed stats: `forceRating`, `encumbranceCurrent`, `encumbranceThreshold`, `encumbranceStats`
  - **Encumbrance (Prompt 2 of The Archive, migration 125)**: single source of truth is now `computeEncumbranceStats()` in `src/lib/derivedStats.ts` — a pure function (no Supabase, no React) called from here AND from `LootAwardModal.tsx` (`MobileHudLayout.tsx`/`MobileSessionCompanion.tsx`, named here at the time, were both deleted by the Mobile Shell Rebuild task — mobile has no encumbrance-consuming call site again until a later shell prompt wires one). Replaces three inline `useMemo`s that used to live in this hook (`encumbranceCurrent`, `encumbranceBonus`, `encumbranceBonusSources`) — the per-item attribution `encumbranceBonusSources` gave is superseded by `encumbranceStats.capacitySources`, with suppressed/reason fidelity that array never had. Returns `{ base, wornCapacity, load, threshold, cliff, perItem, capacitySources, loadSources }` — `base = 5 + Brawn` computed live (not `characters.encumbrance_threshold`, which is only ever set once at character creation and can drift stale if Brawn is later raised — found one live case, Grevi Unduli, stored at 7 vs live-computed 8; the formula change intentionally corrects this). `threshold = base + wornCapacity`, `cliff = threshold + Brawn` (RAW: encumbered ≥ Brawn ⇒ no free maneuver, 2 strain/maneuver). Two worn-rules exclusivity checks, keyed on the new `ref_armor`/`ref_gear.worn_anchor` column: **Rule A** — equipped armor's own −3 encumbrance reduction (floor 0) applies only to the first equipped suit per `worn_anchor` (deterministic tie-break: ascending by item `id`, since no `equipped_at` timestamp exists in the schema); a second suit on the same anchor pays full cost, `reason: 'anchor_occupied_armor'`. **Rule B** — `encumbrance_bonus` counts only for the first equipped item (armor or gear) per `worn_anchor`; a duplicate grants +0, `reason: 'anchor_occupied_capacity'`, and still pays its own encumbrance. Both rules WARN, never block (item stays equipped); an armor item can be suppressed by both rules at once (verified via a synthetic two-Katarn-suits test). Items with no `worn_anchor` keep the old no-exclusivity behavior. **`is_dropped` fix**: `derivedStats.ts`'s pre-existing implementation already excluded `is_dropped` rows; the hook's inline version didn't — found as drift during the Task 1 audit. In practice this was unreachable (both the initial fetch in `characterDataPrefetch.ts` and every drop handler already filter/remove dropped rows before either function ever saw them), so the unified function's defensive check is parity/safety, not an observed live bug fix — documented separately per the prompt's instruction, not folded silently into "consolidated encumbrance."
- `handleSetEquipState`/`handleToggleWeaponEquipped`/`handleToggleEquippedById` (equip/carry/stow write path) now read-fresh-then-write, queued per row via a `Map<id, Promise>` chain (`enqueueRowWrite`) rather than deriving next-state from local React state and firing a last-write-wins update — rapid toggling or hover-preview on the same row now serializes instead of racing. Local state still updates optimistically, but only after the DB round trip, not before.
  - `forceRating` = `ref_careers.force_rating` (0 for every respec career as of migration 094 — careers no longer grant Force Rating) + owned `FORCERAT` talent ranks + (`characters.force_rating_purchased ? 1 : 0`). Owning a Force-sensitive specialization no longer contributes directly — see `isEligibleForForceRating` in `src/lib/forceEligibility.ts`.
  - `careerForceRatingBase` is also exposed (career contribution only, excluding FORCERAT/purchase) — passed to `computeDerivedStats` so `force_rating_conditional` talents (e.g. WITCHCRAFT) gate correctly against "does the career already grant a free Force Rating" rather than against the combined total, letting Witchcraft's bonus stack with the FORCERAT talent and the deliberate purchase instead of being zeroed by them. Example: a Mystic (career FR 0 under reSpec) with both the Witchcraft talent and one FORCERAT rank has Force Rating 2 — each contributes independently.
  - `derivedForceRating` (internal) re-runs `computeDerivedStats` to fold in talent modifier bonuses and write-backs `characters.force_rating` (the display cache) whenever it drifts — lives here rather than in a single UI component so both desktop and mobile keep the cache fresh for GM-side consumers that read the raw column.
- Exposes HUD transforms: `speciesAbilities`, `hudSkills` (`HudSkill[]`), `hudTalents` (`HudTalent[]`), `hudWeapons` (`WpnDisplay[]`), `hudArmor` (`ArmDisplay[]`), `hudGear` (`GearRow[]`)
- Exposes all mutation handlers: `handleSetEquipState`, `handleUpdateCharacter`, `handleAddWeapon`, etc.
- `handlePurchaseForceRating()` — deliberate 10 XP purchase of Force Rating 1 (sets `force_rating_purchased`); refuses if already purchased, XP < 10, or `!isEligibleForForceRating(...)` (includes the droid/clone lockout). `handlePurchaseForceAbility` now also refuses (at the handler level) when `forceRating < 1`. `handleBuySpecialization` refuses buying a Force-sensitive spec for droid/clone characters, and sets `pendingForceRatingOffer` (exposed with its setter) after a Force-sensitive spec purchase completes when the character is newly eligible at Force Rating 0.
- Realtime: Subscribes to `characters`, `character_weapons`, `character_armor`, `character_gear`, `character_skills`, `character_talents`, `character_critical_injuries`
- HUD display types (`HudSkill`, `HudTalent`, `WpnDisplay`, `ArmDisplay`, `GearRow`) live in `src/lib/types.ts`; panel files re-export them for backward compat

### `useGroupAssets(campaignId)`
- Fetches `group_assets` table — vehicles, starships, safe houses, strategic assets, NPCs, other
- Used by GroupSheet, GM dashboard, and PlayerHUDDesktop (for stow locations)
- Realtime: subscribes to `group_assets` channel

### `useGroupStorage(assetId)`
- Fetches stowed inventory for a single `group_assets` record marked `is_group_storage = true`
- Queries `character_weapons`, `character_armor`, `character_gear` in parallel filtered by `stow_location_id = assetId + equip_state='stowed' + NOT is_dropped`
- Returns: `{ items: GroupStorageItem[], loading: boolean, taking: Set<string>, takeItem }`
- `taking` is a `Set<string>` of item IDs currently being taken (per-item spinner)
- `takeItem(itemId, itemType, takerId, qty?)` → calls `take_group_storage_item` RPC; throws `Error` on failure
- Realtime: subscribes to INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on all three item tables filtered by `stow_location_id`
- Pass `null` to disable fetching (used to pause when storage sheet is closed)

### `useCampaignPlayers(campaignId)`
- Fetches all players and their characters for a campaign
- Used by GM dashboard to populate initiative and combat panels
- Realtime: subscribes to `players` channel

### `useEncounterState(campaignId)`
- Tracks active encounter: `is_active`, `round`, `current_turn`, `tokens`, `initiative_order`
- Reads/writes `encounters` + `encounter_tokens` tables
- Realtime: subscribes to `encounters` channel
- Used by CombatPanel (GM side) and PlayerHUDDesktop (player side for combat status)

### `useQuartermaster(supabase, campaignId)`
- Owns all Quartermaster state for a campaign
- State: `qm: Quartermaster | null`, `qmItems: QuartermasterItem[]`, `buyRows: QmBuyRow[]`, `loading`
- Exposes: `toggleOpen`, `upsertItem`, `removeItem`, `getQmEntry`, `buyItem`, `sellItem`
- Realtime: subscribes to `quartermaster` (filtered by `campaign_id`) and `quartermaster_items` channels
- Used by `GroupSheet` (player QM button) and `QuartermasterModal` (full buy/sell UI)

### `useDestinyPool(campaignId, characterId, characterName, supabase)`
- Owns all Destiny Pool state extracted from `PlayerHUDDesktop`
- State: `destinyPool`, `pendingSpend`, `destinyPoolRecord`, `destinyRollRequest`, `destinySpendOpen`, `destinyGmFlash`, `destinyConsidering`
- Exposes setters for state that the parent component drives externally: `setDestinyRollRequest`, `setDestinySpendOpen`, `setDestinyGmFlash`, `setDestinyConsidering`
- Exposes `handleSpendDestiny(idx)` — two-tap confirm flow, persists to `campaigns.settings.destiny_pool`
- Realtime: subscribes to `campaigns` (postgres_changes), `campaign-events-*` (broadcast), `destiny-pool-player-*` (postgres_changes), `destiny-*` (broadcast)

### `useGmDestinyPool({ campaignId, campaign, characters, sendToChar })`
- GM-side Destiny Pool state extracted from `gm/page.tsx` to fix Single Responsibility violation
- Creates its own Supabase client internally
- State: `destinyPool`, `destinyPoolRecord`, `destinyGenerateOpen`, `manualAdjustOpen`, `gmSpendConfirm`, `manualLight`, `manualDark`, `manualBusy`
- Exposes all setters including `setDestinyPool` and `setDestinyPoolRecord` (needed by the campaign loader and the DestinyGeneratePanel callback)
- Exposes handlers: `handleGmSpendDark`, `handleApplyManual`, `flipDestinyToken`, `addDestinyToken`, `removeDestinyToken`
- Realtime: subscribes to `destiny-pool-gm-*` (postgres_changes on `destiny_pool` table)

### `useSessionMode(campaignId)`
- Tracks the current session mode (`'exploration' | 'combat'`) and round number for the campaign
- State: `mode`, `round`, `transitionPending`, `prevMode`
- Realtime: subscribes to `campaigns` channel to detect mode transitions

### `useSessionRollState(campaignId)`
- Manages session Duty/Obligation roll state from `session_roll_state` table
- State: `duty_roll`, `duty_triggered`, `obligation_roll`, `obligation_triggered`, etc.
- Provides computed bonus: per-character wound threshold bonus from Duty activation

### `useRollFeed(campaignId)`
- Live feed of dice roll results from `roll_log` table for all characters in a campaign
- State: `entries: RollEntry[]` — each entry has pool composition + result symbols
- Realtime: subscribes to INSERT on `roll_log` filtered by `campaign_id`

### `useCharacterSigAbilities(characterId, careerKey)`
- Signature Abilities Phase 1: owns `ref_sig_abilities`/`ref_sig_ability_nodes` lookup + this character's `character_sig_ability_nodes` purchases
- Returns: `{ availableSigAbilities: SigAbility[], lockedAbilities: Record<string, LockedSigAbility>, purchasedNodes: CharacterSigAbilityNode[], hasUnlockedTier5: boolean, lockInAbility(sigAbilityKey, specSlot), purchaseNode(sigAbilityKey, node), loading }`
- `lockedAbilities` is keyed by spec slot (`specialization_key`) — a character can lock a different signature ability under each in-career specialization it owns
- `hasUnlockedTier5`: true if `character_talents.xp_cost = 25` exists under a specialization in `ref_careers.specialization_keys` for `careerKey`. **Note:** for the `respec` dataset, `ref_specializations.career_key` is always `NULL` (migration 064 seeds it that way) — in-career association must go through `ref_careers.specialization_keys`, not a join on `ref_specializations.career_key`. (`useCharacterData.ts`'s `handleBuySpecialization` still does the `ref_specializations.career_key` join and is therefore always treating specs as out-of-career for respec — pre-existing, unrelated to this feature, not fixed here.)
- `lockInAbility`/`purchaseNode` are optimistic (local state updates before the Supabase write) and both deduct XP via a read-then-write on `characters.xp_available` (same non-atomic pattern `useCharacterData.ts` uses elsewhere)
- Realtime: subscribes to INSERT on `character_sig_ability_nodes` filtered by `character_id`
- Wired into both `useCharacterData` (exposed as `sigAbilities`, `lockedSigAbilities`, `purchasedSigNodes`, `hasUnlockedTier5`, `lockInAbility`, `purchaseSigNode`, consumed by `PlayerHUDDesktop`) and `useTalentSurfaceData` (same shape, plus `sigLoading`, consumed directly by the `/character/[id]/talents` route) — the talents route no longer goes through `useCharacterData` at all (Prompt 7a)
- Internal fetch (Prompt 7a): the `ref_sig_ability_nodes` query and the `hasUnlockedTier5` count query run in parallel (`Promise.all`) rather than sequentially — they have no dependency on each other, only on the initial 4-query batch (`ref_sig_abilities`/`character_sig_ability_nodes`/`character_specializations`/`ref_careers`), which was a sequential-by-accident hop, not a genuine one
- `careerKey` only starts this hook's fetch once truthy — previously always arrived from `useCharacterData`'s full 26-query batch (`character?.career_key`); on the talents route it now arrives from `useTalentSurfaceData`'s much smaller shell batch instead, so sig queries fire much sooner in wall-clock time without any change to this hook's own code
- Prompt 7c: its `ref_careers` lookup (previously a narrow `.select('specialization_keys').eq('key', careerKey).maybeSingle()` query) now reads from `refDataCache.ts`'s cached `ref_careers` rows instead of its own network request — a warm cache serves this with zero additional queries

### `useTalentSurfaceData(characterId)`
- Lean, route-scoped data hook for `/character/[id]/talents` only (Prompt 7a) — replaces `useCharacterData` on that route. Fetches only what `TalentSurface` and the route's header/rail render: `characters`, `character_specializations` (the "shell" batch, plus cached ref data) and `character_talents` (the "tree" batch, plus cached `ref_talents`) — both batches fire in parallel, each exposing its own readiness flag (`shellReady`, `treeReady`) instead of one blocking `loading` boolean, so the route can paint the header/rail as soon as the shell resolves while the tree area shows its own local skeleton. Signature-ability data comes from `useCharacterSigAbilities`, unchanged.
- Does NOT fetch weapons/armor/gear/crits/species/force-abilities/item-descriptors/attachments/qualities/obligation-or-duty-types/character_skills — none of it renders on this route (was ~68% of `useCharacterData`'s payload on this route)
- Prompt 7c: `ref_specializations`/`ref_careers`/`ref_skills`/`ref_talents` now come from `refDataCache.ts`'s shared, dataset-keyed cache instead of this hook's own queries — on a warm cache (e.g. arriving from the sheet, which populates the same cache) this hook fires zero ref queries; only `characters`/`character_specializations`/`character_talents` remain as real per-navigation requests. The `ref_specializations` retired-spec fallback merge (`missingSpecKeys`) stays as this hook's own character-specific post-processing on top of the cached rows — it is not, and cannot be, part of the shared cache.
- Mutation handlers (`handlePurchaseTalent`, `handleRemoveTalent`, `handleResolveDedication`, `handleCancelDedication`, `handleBuySpecialization`) do not duplicate purchase/XP logic — they call the same standalone functions (`purchaseTalent`, `removeTalent`, `resolveDedication`, `cancelDedication`, `buySpecialization`, `applyTalentModifiers`) that `useCharacterData.ts` now exports and calls itself, each hook supplying its own state/setters via a `deps` bag. The XP-race-safe fresh-read-before-write pattern lives in exactly one place either way.
- `useCharacterData.ts` itself is otherwise unchanged in behaviour — its own `loadCharacter` fetch (used by `PlayerHUDDesktop`/`MobileHudLayout`/everything else) still fetches the same tables in the same shape; only its ref_skills/ref_talents/ref_specializations/ref_careers legs are now cache-backed (Prompt 7c) instead of firing their own queries every mount.

### `useDerivedStats(input)`
- Computes derived character stats: soak, encumbrance thresholds, wound/strain thresholds, XP spent, force rating
- Input: character + talents + armor + weapons + ref data + optional `moralitySystem` (`'vanilla' | 'force_presence'`, defaults `'vanilla'`)
- Extracted computation logic; complements `useCharacterData`
- Force Presence threshold bonuses (Prompt B): when `moralitySystem === 'force_presence'`, `character.dark_points >= 7` adds `+2` wound / `-2` strain, `light_points >= 7` adds `+2` strain (both simultaneously is impossible — `light+dark<=10` DB CHECK). Deliberately NOT folded into `mods.woundThresholdBonus`/`strainThresholdBonus` (`computeDerivedStats`, `derivedStats.ts`) — those accumulate talent bonuses that are separately, permanently written back to `character.wound_threshold`/`strain_threshold` by `applyTalentModifiers`, and `effectiveStats.woundThreshold`/`strainThreshold` read those DB columns directly rather than `mods` — so anything added only to `mods` would never reach `effectiveStats` at all. Force Presence bonuses instead get their own additive term, applied directly at `effectiveStats` assembly, with their own appended tooltip source entries — purely derived, never written back, unlike talent bonuses. `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` computes `derivedStats` once and passes the same `effectiveStats` to both `HudStatusStrip` and `HudForceTab`, so the two can't disagree. Mobile (`MobileHudLayout.tsx`/`MobileSessionCompanion.tsx`, both since deleted by the Mobile Shell Rebuild task) didn't thread `moralitySystem` at the time this was written — defaults to `'vanilla'`, no behavior change, out of scope for this prompt; the new `MobileShell.tsx` doesn't thread it either, unchanged status.

### `useMapTokens(mapId, options?)`
- Owns `map_tokens` table state for the active combat map
- State: `tokens: MapToken[]`; each token has position, participant type, label, wound pct, visibility
- Realtime: subscribes to INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `map_tokens` (table has `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL` so DELETE filter on `map_id` works correctly for external deletes by `slot_key`)
- `options.visibleOnly` (Prompt 11) — player-facing callers only (`PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx`, `/table/page.tsx`). Adds `.eq('is_visible', true)` to the initial fetch and drops/excludes hidden rows in the realtime INSERT/UPDATE handler, so hidden-token data no longer sits resident in the player client's React state (the old pattern fetched every row, hidden included, and only hid it via a client-side `.filter()` after the fact — inspectable in React state/devtools the whole time). This does **not** fully close the gap: `map_tokens` RLS is `USING (true)` (`032_maps.sql`), so Realtime itself still evaluates that permissive policy and could broadcast one hidden-row UPDATE payload in transit before this filters it client-side. Closing that needs an RLS policy change (a migration — out of scope for Prompt 11, flagged for follow-up). GM call sites (`GmMapView.tsx`, `GmShell.tsx`, `StagingLeftRail.tsx`) omit the option and keep seeing every token, hidden included, same as before.

### `useActiveMap(campaignId)`
- Loads and subscribes to the active map (`is_active = true`) for a campaign from `maps` table
- State: `activeMap: ActiveMap | null` — includes grid config, image URL, token scale, `planet_id`
- Realtime: subscribes to `maps` channel filtered by `campaign_id`

### `useMapPlanets(campaignId)`
- Owns `map_planets` table state + realtime for a campaign — used to folder-group maps by planet
- State: `planets: MapPlanet[]`; exposes setter for optimistic mutations
- Realtime: handles INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE

### `useStowLocations(campaignId)`
- Loads stowable `group_assets` (vehicles, starships, safe houses) for item stow location UI
- Returns: `{ stowableAssets, baseOfOperationsName }`

### `useGmData(campaignId)`
- **Primary GM data hook** — owns campaign + characters load and common GM reference data
- Fetches: `campaigns`, `characters` (for campaign), `ref_morality`, `character_critical_injuries` (active, full rows)
- State: `campaign`, `setCampaign`, `characters`, `setCharacters`, `activeChars`, `forceNotifications`, etc.
- Returns the `UseGmDataReturn` interface; includes `charCrits: Record<string, CharacterCriticalInjury[]>` and `charActiveCritCounts: Record<string, number>`

### `useGmBroadcast(characters)`
- GM → player Supabase broadcast channel management
- Maintains per-character channels keyed by `character_id` (never destroys on re-render to avoid race)
- Exposes: `notify(charId, type, message)`, `sendToChar(charId, payload)`, `broadcastAll(payload, characters)`
- **Also exports two standalone (non-hook) `pending_actions` writers**, deliberately co-located with `sendToChar` because a GM action fires both: the broadcast is the instant delivery path, the queue row is the durable one.
  - `createPendingAction({ campaignId, characterId, actionType, payload?, sourceRef?, isBlocking? })` → `{ action, duplicate, error }`. Absorbs the `23505` raised by the partial unique index and returns the already-outstanding row with `duplicate: true` — re-firing a live request (GM pressing "Request Rolls" twice, or a per-card retry after a global request) is a no-op, not an error. Omitting `sourceRef` disables deduplication, since the index treats NULLs as distinct.
  - `cancelPendingAction(id)` — sets `status = 'cancelled'`, releasing the partial unique index so the same `source_ref` can be requested again. The durable counterpart to the existing `destiny-roll-cancel` broadcast, which only clears client state.
  - `cancelPendingActionsByType(campaignId, actionType, sourceRef?)` — campaign-wide sweep of every outstanding row of one type, optionally narrowed to one setup session. Exists for invalidation paths with no row ids in scope; `endEncounter` is the motivating case.
  - **Initiative is wired (see `InitiativeSetupModal` below). `DestinyGeneratePanel` is not** — that adoption is still pending.

### `useMarketMerchant(campaignId)`
- GM-side load/save/subscribe for the campaign's single active Market merchant (`market_merchants`, migrations 127/129). Standing realtime convention: catch-up `SELECT` (`.eq('is_active', true)`), then `.channel().on('postgres_changes', {filter: campaign_id=eq.<id>}, () => refetch())`, `removeChannel` cleanup. On ANY change event in-campaign it simply re-runs the catch-up `SELECT` rather than patching local state — simpler and robust given writes are already serialized (below), at the cost of one extra round trip per change.
- Writes are read-fresh-then-write, serialized through a per-hook `Promise` chain (`queueWrite`) — not a `useState` busy flag, since React state isn't a synchronous mutex. Each queued write (`toggleReveal`, `revealTier`, `cycleTier`, `setLinePrice`, `deleteLine`, `setOpenToPlayers`, `rollStock`, `restoreSnapshot`) waits for the previous one to fully land, re-`SELECT`s the row, mutates only its own concern, then `UPDATE`s — Playwright-verified: 10 same-tick synchronous clicks on 10 different rows' Reveal buttons all persist, zero lost updates (`revealedOut` counter increments monotonically 1→10 across the queued writes).
- `is_active` is never written directly by this hook — only via the `set_active_merchant` RPC, and only once ever per campaign in practice: `rollStock`/`restoreSnapshot` `UPDATE` the existing active row in place if one exists, or `INSERT` + immediately activate via the RPC if none exists yet. This keeps "one merchant row per campaign, forever" true without ever needing a second RPC call.
- `downloadSnapshot()`/`parseSnapshotFile()`/`restoreSnapshot()` wrap `marketSnapshot.ts`'s serialise/parse — this hook is the only place that module's contract is actually written to/read from a live merchant row (Prompt 1 built the contract with no UI consumer yet).
- Consumed by `GmMarketPanel.tsx` only. `GmMarketPanel`'s Open-to-Players toggle (Prompt 3) additionally owns `handleToggleOpenToPlayers` — the only place this panel creates or cancels `pending_actions` rows: opening loops `activeChars` (a new required prop, threaded from `GmShell`) calling `createPendingAction({actionType:'vendor_offer', sourceRef: merchant.id, isBlocking:false})` per character (deduped by the existing partial unique index if re-fired) plus `broadcastAll` (also newly threaded from `GmShell`) for the instant path, matching `useGmBroadcast.ts`'s own "fire both" convention; closing calls `cancelPendingActionsByType(campaignId, 'vendor_offer', merchant.id)` — one call cancels every player's row at once since they share `source_ref`.

### `usePlayerMarket(campaignId)`
- Read-only player subscription to the campaign's active Market merchant (The Archive Market Prompt 3). Returns `PlayerMarketView | null` — null whenever there's nothing to show (no active merchant, or `is_active`/`is_open_to_players` collapsed false), so the caller never has to inspect either flag itself. **Deliberately does not follow the standing `postgres_changes` convention** — see `market_merchants`'s Data Layer entry above for why (raw CDC would put unrevealed stock on the wire regardless of client code). Catch-up is the `get_player_market_view` RPC (migration 130); live updates arrive via `.channel('market:'+campaignId).on('broadcast', {event:'market_update'}, ...)`, fed by a DB trigger, never `postgres_changes` on this table. Network-proof (Playwright, WS-frame capture): unrevealed item names never appear in any HTTP response or WS frame this hook's channel receives.
- Consumed by `MarketStorefront.tsx` only.

### `usePendingActions(characterId, supabase)`
- Player-side view of this character's unresolved `pending_actions` rows (migration 117) — the durable substrate behind the planned Notifications drawer
- Returns: `{ actions: PendingAction[], count, blockingCount, resolve(id, resultPayload?) }`, ordered blocking-first then oldest-first
- Initial `SELECT` on `status = 'pending'` is the catch-up that broadcast-only delivery cannot do: today every decision popup arrives solely via `usePlayerBroadcast`'s `gm-action` channel, so a socket that is asleep or reconnecting silently drops the request and the player sees nothing (the suspected cause of the macOS Safari reports)
- Realtime: subscribes to INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `pending_actions` filtered by `character_id` (table is `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL`, so the filter holds on UPDATE/DELETE too); rows leaving `'pending'` drop out of the list. Subscription/cleanup shape follows `useCriticalInjuryRequest.ts`
- `resolve` is optimistic, writes `status = 'resolved'` + `resolved_at` + `result_payload`, and guards on `.eq('status','pending')`. `result_payload` carries the **actual result**, not merely a resolution flag — initiative values currently live only in `roll_log` (append-only, display) and in `InitiativeSetupModal`'s transient React state, so a GM reload loses them today
- Exports `PendingAction`, `PendingActionType`, `PendingActionStatus`
- Consumed by `InitiativeRollModal.tsx` (resolves its own row on submit) and by `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx`, which feeds the Alerts button and the pending-action drawer (see `HudNotificationsDrawer` below).

### `useGmAwards(campaignId, characters, sendToChar)`
- Owns XP/credits award flow and loot award management
- Reads/writes the `gm_award_log` table (replaced the old `useLootAwards`). *(Corrected 2026-08-08: this previously read "`loot_awards` table" — no such table exists in the database and there are zero references to that name anywhere in `src/`.)*
- State: XP amount, credits amount, award mode, pending/delivered loot awards

### `useGmLoot(campaignId, ...)`
- Owns loot generation and vendor purchase UI state for GM loot panel
- State: `lootType`, search filters, generated item list, vendor offer state
- Fetches from `ref_weapons`, `ref_armor`, `ref_gear` for loot rolling

### `useGmSession(campaignId, ...)`
- Owns combat session state: session mode transition, staging encounter, adversary/vehicle instances
- State: `sessionMode`, `combatRound`, `sessionBusy`, `stagingEncounter`, `stagingInitRoster`, `stagingVehicleRoster`, etc.
- Coordinates mode transitions between exploration and combat; triggers encounter creation in Supabase
- **Encounters are per-map and permanent (migration 115).** `stagingEncounter` is loaded by `(campaign_id, map_id)` on the active map, not by `is_active`, so switching maps swaps decks and the deck exists with or without combat. `is_active` now means only "combat is live on this row": `handleStagingCombatStart` sets it (updating the map's existing deck rather than inserting a row, merging the setup modal's edited rosters over the deck's by `instanceId` so entries left out of initiative survive as non-combatants), and `endEncounter` clears it plus `round`/`current_slot_index`/per-slot `acted`+`current`. `endEncounter` no longer deactivates the row or nulls `map_tokens.slot_key` campaign-wide — that pair was what emptied every map's deck and permanently unlinked surviving tokens from their instances when combat ended. Initiative slots carry no state between fights; Begin Combat regenerates them wholesale.
- `stagingAddToEncounter` (off-map add) creates the map's deck on demand via `ensureEncounterForMap` and toasts on success/failure, instead of silently early-returning when no combat had ever been started
- `stagingEncounter`/`setStagingEncounter` is the sole canonical `combat_encounters` subscription for the whole GM view (the old `useEncounterData` hook it might have overlapped with was dead code and has been deleted)
- Exposes `markEncounterPending`/`clearEncounterPending` — tracked in a `pendingKeysRef` set of `instanceId:stat` keys with an in-flight debounced write from `useEncounterCombatControls`
- Realtime merge is field-aware: incoming `combat_encounters` rows are staleness-checked against the last-applied `updated_at` (rejects out-of-order echoes), then merged per-instance — any adversary/vehicle instance with a pending key keeps its local (optimistic) version instead of the incoming row's, while everything else (initiative slots, other instances) takes the incoming row
- `beginCombat`, `changeRound`, `stagingLibrary`, `stagingLibraryLoaded`, `loadStagingLibrary` were removed (confirmed zero callers)
- `openStagingCombatModal` builds two parallel rosters from `map_tokens` for the "Begin Combat" flow: `stagingInitRoster` (adversary tokens, `participant_type==='adversary' && token_shape!=='rectangle'`) and `stagingVehicleRoster` (vehicle tokens, same `participant_type` but `token_shape==='rectangle'` — vehicles have no distinct `participant_type`). Adversary instances now carry `AdversaryInstance.alignment` (optional field on `src/lib/adversaries.ts`, threaded through `adversaryToInstance()`'s 3rd param), populated from the map token's stored `alignment` here — this is what lets `InitiativeSetupModal` show a genuine "Friendly NPC"/"Enemy" tag instead of defaulting everyone to enemy. `handleStagingCombatStart` wires `map_tokens.slot_key` for both adversary and vehicle tokens that ended up with a slot. `stagingAddVehicleToEncounter` (incremental single-vehicle mid-combat append) was removed as dead code — it had zero call sites and doesn't fit the batch client-side slot-building `InitiativeSetupModal` uses.
- `InitiativeSetupModal` (`src/components/dm/InitiativeSetupModal.tsx`) — **full-screen three-column GM panel** for "Begin Combat"/"Recheck Initiative" (was a stacked 4-step 760px modal; the stacked order list forced heavy scrolling, which drove the rework). Rendered via `Modal`'s `fullScreen` prop at `Z.hudSupreme` — `GmTopBar`/`GmLeftRail` sit on `--z-hud-combat` (9001), so the default modal layer would let the GM chrome overlap column 1 and the deck/FAB cover the Lock button. Header carries the Cool/Vigilance segmented control, which now sets the **default** skill for the whole encounter. Column 1 = one card per PC (roll-status chip, per-row skill selector, dice-pool pips, editable Succ/Adv, per-card `↻` that re-requests from that character only via `sendToChar`, alongside the global "Request Rolls"); values arriving from a player's broadcast render in the success colour with `data-live="true"` and revert to plain GM styling the moment the GM types over them. Column 2 = adversaries + vehicles. Column 3 = the compact sticky order rail + Lock Order & Start.
  - **Per-row skill assignment.** `ref_skills.type` is the category field — `stCombat` is exactly `BRAWL/GUNN/LTSABER/MELEE/RANGHVY/RANGLT` — so every skill selector (GM and player) filters `type !== 'stCombat'` rather than carrying a hardcoded exclusion list. The modal fetches the whole `ref_skills` catalog plus every PC's full `character_skills` row set (the old query filtered to `skill_key IN ('COOL','VIGIL')`); the `charSkillRanks` prop survives as a Cool/Vigilance seed. Adversary pools resolve `skillRanks` by **display name** (that's how `AdversaryInstance.skillRanks` is keyed) and the characteristic via `CHARACTERISTIC_ABBR`. The GM's per-row pick feeds that row's roll and rides along on the `initiative-request` broadcast as `skillKey`; a player's own pick arrives back on `initiative-result` as `skillKey`/`skillName`, clears any standing GM override for that character (the player's action is newer) and is flagged on the card. No persistence — all transient modal state, no migration.
  - **Visibility + exclusion.** Only adversaries whose live `map_tokens` row exists and has `is_visible` are listed; hidden and off-map (token-less) entries are merged into one `◌ N hidden token(s) — reveal on map to include` note and produce no slot. Tokens carry no instance id (`participant_id` is always null), so the bridge is `map_tokens.slot_key` → the deck's persisted `initiative_slots[].adversaryInstanceId`/`vehicleInstanceId`, with label matching as fallback; `tokens`/`encounterSlots` are passed in from `GmShell`. The same bridge fixes disposition: effective alignment is GM override → `AdversaryInstance.alignment` → token alignment → deck slot alignment → `'enemy'`, so the Recheck-initiative call site (whose roster predates the instance field) no longer silently shows every friendly NPC as Enemy. Per-card `✕` excludes (de-emphasised, restorable via `↥`, no slot); "Roll All Visible" skips excluded rows. Vehicles never roll — hand-entered Succ/Adv and the "own initiative slot" toggle only, no roll button and no skill selector.
  - **Pending-action queue wiring (migration 117).** Every player roll request now has two deliveries: the existing `sendToChar` broadcast (fast path) *and* a `pending_actions` row (durable path). Neither replaces the other — the broadcast is instant for a connected player, the row is what survives a suspended socket, which is the whole reason the queue exists.
    - **`source_ref` = a setup-session id**: a `useRef(randomUUID())` minted once per mount. `GmShell` renders the modal only while `initiativeSetupOpen` is true, so it is stable across one setup session and distinct across close-and-reopen — deliberate, since one encounter can legitimately need initiative twice. **Not** `stagingEncounter.id`, which is identical across both of those and absent on the recheck path. It is never null: a null `source_ref` would silently disable the partial unique index (NULLs are distinct in Postgres), which is the entire dedupe mechanism.
    - Both fire sites (`handleRequestRolls`'s loop and the per-card `↻`) route through `requestRollFor`, so the row creation is one code path. A repeat request collapses onto the existing row via `uq_pending_actions_open`; `createPendingAction` absorbs the `23505` and the GM sees nothing.
    - The modal subscribes to `pending_actions` (filtered on `campaign_id`, narrowed to its own `source_ref` client-side, since Realtime filters take one column). A row arriving `resolved` with a `result_payload` is folded into `pcResults`/`pcLive`/`pcPlayerSkill` exactly as the broadcast handler would have — this is what recovers a submission whose broadcast was dropped.
    - **The zero/zero ambiguity is fixed.** `pending: r.successes === 0 && r.advantages === 0` could not distinguish a genuine 0-success/0-advantage roll from an unanswered request. `pcIsPending(charId, r)` now reads the row's status first (`resolved` → rolled, `pending` → pending) and falls back to the old heuristic only when no row exists for that character in this session (a GM-rolled adversary, a vehicle, a duplicate, or a request predating the queue). Both consumers were updated — the `⏳ Pending`/`✓ Rolled` chip and `resolveBoardItem`'s rail score. Note `sortInitiative` never consumed the `pending` flag (it sorts on `successes`/`advantages`), and `allSlots`' own `pending` field has no reader; they were left as-is.
    - **Mount sweep.** On every mount the modal cancels *all* `pending` initiative rows for the campaign (`cancelPendingActionsByType(campaignId, 'initiative')` — no `sourceRef`) and broadcasts `initiative-cancel` to every character in the `characters` prop. This closes the GM-reload gap: a reload mints a new `setupSessionIdRef` and can no longer see the previous session's rows, which would otherwise sit `pending` until combat ended with a player still holding an orphaned roll modal. Campaign-wide by necessity — at mount there is no live session to distinguish a dead one from. Guarded by a `sweepStartedRef` so StrictMode's double-invoke and any re-render cannot re-fire it, and both request controls are disabled until `sweepDone`, so a request can never be created before the sweep that would cancel it lands. Resolved rows are untouched (the helper filters `status = 'pending'`).
      - **Explicitly not recovery.** Submitted rolls from the dead session are *not* adopted: no session id is persisted, `pcResults` still seeds to all-zero, and the GM re-requests, reading any prior values off the roll feed. A decision, not an oversight.
    - **`initiative-cancel`** — a new per-character message on the existing `gm-notify-<characterId>` channel, paired with `cancelPendingActionsByType`. Fired from four places (the mount sweep above, plus): modal close (`handleClose`, wrapping both the `✕` and `Modal`'s `onClose`), Lock Order & Start (`handleStart`), and `endEncounter` (`useGmSession.ts`). The first two scope by setup-session `source_ref`; `endEncounter` runs long after the modal is gone and sweeps by campaign. Before this, all three paths left an orphaned player roll modal open, broadcasting into a channel whose only subscriber had unmounted.
    - Inclusion rule: only entries in the `characters` prop produce a row. Adversaries are GM-rolled locally, vehicles are hand-entered, duplicates share the original's values, Reserve and excluded produce no slot — none of them can reach `requestRollFor`.
    - The **Recheck Initiative** instance is deliberately left unwired: `setRecheckInitiativeOpen(true)` is never called anywhere, so that modal is currently unreachable.
  - The order rail is a dnd-kit (`@dnd-kit/core`/`sortable`/`utilities` — first use in the codebase) sortable **dense single-line rail** — drag handle, position number, role colour dot, name, compact `2s·1a` score — with Duplicate (⧉, independently-draggable copy sharing the original's live roll values, no re-roll, badged `×N · Nth Action`) and Bench (✕, moves to a single collapsed Reserve row with a count badge) hover actions. Reserve is local/transient state, reset on every modal remount, with its own dnd-kit sortable list and a Restore (↥) action. The board auto-mirrors the roll-driven `sortInitiative()` order (`allSlots`) until the GM's first manual drag/duplicate/bench/restore (`boardTouched` flag), after which it's GM-authoritative; `handleStart` builds `finalSlots` from the live board order (not `allSlots` directly), so benched participants never reach `initiative_slots` and duplicates each get their own slot keyed by a local `boardId` (not any DB id). Role colours: gold=active/position-1, `var(--hud-accent-purple)`=PC, `var(--state-failure)`=enemy NPC, `var(--state-advantage)`=friendly NPC, `HUD.gold`+"VEH"=vehicle (matches `CombatFeedPanel`'s `TypeBadge`). Renders inline within the ambient theme (no theme forcing) — matches `InitiativeStrip`/`CombatFeedPanel`, both of which render inside GmShell's forced `gm-imperial` theme.
- `MapToolsRadial` (`src/app/gm/MapToolsRadial.tsx`) has a 4th arc, "Table Display" — a direct action (no popup panel) that opens `/table?campaign=<id>` in a new tab; restores the `⛶ Table Display` entry point lost when `GmMapPanel`'s Map section was gutted to token-only controls.

### `useGmCharacterActions(campaignId, characters, refMorality, sendToChar)`
- GM-side character action handlers: crit injury request dispatch, morality setup, fall/redemption flow
- State: `critReqOpenFor`, `moralitySetup`, `fallRedemptionOpen`, etc.
- Writes to `critical_injury_requests`, `characters` (morality fields), `character_critical_injuries`
- Params include `charCrits`/`setCharCrits` (from `useGmData`) for optimistic pip updates
- Exposes `healCritInjury(injuryId)` — heals an injury by ID, optimistically removes pip, sends player toast
- **Force Presence GM actions (Prompt C)**: `awardConflict(charId)`/`awardTranquility(charId)` — fresh-read-then-write +1 to `characters.session_conflict`/`session_tranquility` (deliberately NOT `adjustMorality`'s blind-write pattern, which reads local state with no re-fetch), then `sendToChar(charId, {type: 'conflict-awarded' | 'tranquility-awarded'})` — the DB write and the broadcast are independent, a failed broadcast doesn't affect the write. `gmFlipBalancePoint(charId, fromState, toState)` — the GM's backup correction path for an arbitrary roster character's Balance Points, reusing `forceUtils.ts`'s `computeBalancePointFlip`/`fetchFreshBalancePoints` (shared with the player's own flip in `useCharacterData.ts`, extracted this prompt since `ForcePresenceCard.tsx` exports no reusable sub-logic of its own). `consolidatePreview`/`computeConsolidatePreview()`/`dismissConsolidatePreview()`/`confirmConsolidate()`/`consolidateBusy` — the Consolidate flow: preview is local-only state (a GM-only readout, never sent to players), computed per force-sensitive roster character (`session_conflict > tranquility` → "flip to Dark", `tranquility > conflict` → "flip to Light or Dark→Neutral", tied/zero → "No change"); only `confirmConsolidate()` writes, zeroing `session_conflict`/`session_tranquility` for the whole previewed roster — it never touches `light_points`/`dark_points`. Award buttons use a promise-chain queue (`queueRef` in `ForcePresenceGmPanel.tsx`'s `RosterRow`), not a `useState`-based busy guard — confirmed live that a `useState` guard isn't synchronous enough to prevent two rapid clicks from both slipping past it and racing each other's fresh-read (same lesson `useEncounterCombatControls.ts`'s `encounterRef` already learned about relying on React state re-renders for correctness under rapid clicks).

### `useCombatParticipants(campaignId)`
- Owns `combat_participants` table state keyed by `character_id`
- State: `combatParticipants: Record<string, CombatParticipantRow>`
- Realtime: subscribes to `combat_participants` channel filtered by `campaign_id`; handles INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE
- Exports the `CombatParticipantRow` interface (moved from `CombatPanel`)
- Returns: `{ combatParticipants, setCombatParticipants }`

### `useEncounterCombatControls({ encounter, setEncounter, saveEncounter, supabase, campaignId, tokens, updateTokenWoundPct, markPending, clearPending, options? })`
- Shared write layer for all Encounter Deck card controls (`EncounterDeck.tsx`) — successor to the five combat adjustment functions previously inlined in the now-deleted `EncounterAdversaryPanel`/`EncounterVehiclePanel`
- Exposes: `adjustAdversaryWounds`, `adjustAdversaryStrain`, `adjustGroupSize`, `adjustHullTrauma`, `adjustSystemStrain`
- Optimistic: every adjustment reads the live value via a synchronous `encounterRef` mirror (updated in plain JS on every local optimistic change, kept in sync with externally-driven `setEncounter` calls via an effect) rather than the closed-over instance or a `setEncounter(prev => ...)` read-trick — this is what fixed the original click-lag bug where two rapid clicks sharing the same stale `adv`/`vehicle` reference would both compute from the same pre-click baseline and lose a delta
- Debounces the actual `combat_encounters` write per `instanceId:stat` key (350ms trailing, via `scheduleWrite`) instead of writing on every click; syncs `map_tokens.wound_pct` via `useMapTokens`'s `updateTokenWoundPct` in parallel with (not sequentially after) the encounter write; inserts defeat/disabled entries to `combat_log` for wounds/hull-trauma crossings
- `markPending`/`clearPending` (passed through from `useGmSession`'s `markEncounterPending`/`clearEncounterPending`) flag which `instanceId:stat` keys have an in-flight debounced write, so `useGmSession`'s realtime merge doesn't let a stale echo clobber a fresher optimistic edit
- Reads/writes the encounter via a synchronous `encounterRef` mirror (not a setState read-trick) so a superseded debounced write is always reliably cancelled, even under a rapid click burst that queues multiple React updates before a re-render — this was the root cause of an earlier known transient stale-value-revert bug, now fixed and live-verified (5+ runs including a 10+-click burst).

### `useCombatLog(encounterId)`
- Owns `combat_log` table state for a single encounter
- State: `entries: CombatLogEntry[]`
- Realtime: initial load filtered by `encounter_id`, then subscribes to INSERT on `combat_log`
- Exports `CombatLogEntry` and `CombatLogAlignment` types (moved from `CombatLog`)
- Returns: `{ entries }`

### `usePendingDamage(campaignId)`
- Owns pending damage state and realtime subscription for the `pending_damage` table
- State: `pendingDamages: PendingDamage[]`, `editedDamages: Record<string, number>`
- Exposes both setters so `CombatPanel` handlers can manipulate state directly
- Realtime: subscribes to INSERT/UPDATE on `pending_damage` filtered by `campaign_id`

### `useAdversaryTokenImages()`
- Loads `adversary_token_images` table once on mount; returns map + setter for optimistic updates
- Returns: `{ tokenImages: Record<string, string>, setTokenImages }`

### `useVehicleTokenImages()`
- Loads `vehicle_token_images` table once on mount; returns map + setter for optimistic updates
- Returns: `{ tokenImages: Record<string, string>, setTokenImages }`

### `useRefWeapons()`
- Loads all `ref_weapons` once on mount, keyed by lowercased name
- Returns: `Record<string, WeaponRef>` — used for quick damage lookups in combat

### `useCharacterPortraits(characterIds)`
- Fetches `portrait_url` for the given character IDs; re-fetches when the ID set changes
- Returns: `Record<string, string>` (character_id → URL)

### `useCharacterSkills(characterIds)`
- Fetches `character_skills` for multiple character IDs in one query
- Returns: `Record<string, CharacterSkill[]>` keyed by `character_id`

### `useEquippedWeapons(characterIds)`
- Fetches equipped `character_weapons` for multiple character IDs
- Returns: `Record<string, CharacterWeapon[]>` keyed by `character_id`

### `useCriticalInjuryRequest(characterId, supabase)`
- Player-side: subscribes to pending `critical_injury_requests` for a character
- State: `pendingCritRequest: CriticalInjuryRequest | null`
- Drives the crit injury roll modal on the player HUD

### `useCharacterConflicts(characterId, supabase)`
- Loads `character_conflicts` for Force-user morality tracking
- State: `conflicts: ConflictEntry[]` — each entry has `description` (type label), `narrative?`, `session_label`, `is_resolved`, `player_acknowledged?`, `created_at`
- Derived: `pendingConflicts: ConflictEntry[]` — subset where `player_acknowledged === false`, sorted oldest-first; drives the player notification queue
- Returns: `{ conflicts, setConflicts, pendingConflicts }`

### `useGmCampaignConflicts(campaignId, forceSensitiveCharIds)`
- Loads all unresolved (`is_resolved = false`) `character_conflicts` for a set of force-sensitive character IDs
- State: `conflicts: GmConflictRow[]` — each row has `id`, `character_id`, `description`, `narrative?`, `session_label?`, `created_at`
- Realtime: subscribes to INSERT on `character_conflicts` filtered by `campaign_id`; prepends new rows live
- Returns empty array immediately when `forceSensitiveCharIds` is empty (no query fired)
- Called from `GmShell` (lifted from `GmToolsPanel`); reshaped into `Record<string, GmConflictRow[]>` for `GmPartyPanel`
- Returns: `{ conflicts }`

### `usePlayerBroadcast(options)`
- Player-side: subscribes to GM broadcast channels for this character
- Handles: destiny flash, vendor offer, crit injury request, dialog/toast messages
- **Force Presence (Prompt C)**: `'conflict-awarded'`/`'tranquility-awarded'` payload types call new optional callback props `onConflictAwarded`/`onTranquilityAwarded` — matching `'destiny-gm-spent'`'s exact callback-prop shape (not a hook-local state var, since the overlay needs no payload data — visual-only, fired once per GM award press). `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` wires these to two plain `useState<boolean>` flags (`conflictFlash`/`tranquilityFlash`) rendered by `HudModalsOverlay.tsx` as `<PresenceFlash variant="conflict"|"tranquility">`.
- Callbacks wired from options: `onDestinyRollRequest`, `onVendorOffer`, `onCritRequest`, etc.

### `useForcePowers(characterId, supabase)`
- Loads and structures force power trees for a character from `character_force_abilities`
- Returns tree data consumed by `ForcePowerTree` component and `allForcePowers` (`ForcePowerDisplay[]`) consumed by `ForcePanel`
- `buildForcePowerTree`'s per-node `canPurchase` (Pass 2) resolves span-covered adjacency via `buildForceCellKeyMap` (`ForcePowerTree.tsx`, Prompt F1/F3) — `ForcePowerTree.tsx`'s own `buildForceEnergyGraph` (Prompt F4) reuses that exact same map to build the energy-trace graph, not a second resolution mechanism
- Connector-bar edge builder had two bugs, fixed: a self-loop (RIGHT-direction edge used `col+1` instead of `col+span`) and missing edges (an overly-broad `span > 0` gate incorrectly applied to `dir.down` reads, not just `dir.right`); a dedup pass (`buildForceCellKeyMap` + `Set`) now strips any residual self-loop/duplicate edge after `connections` is built
- `allForcePowers` also sets `basicAbilityKey` (`ability_tree.rows[0].abilities[0] ?? null`) — the structural identifier for a power's base node, replacing `ForceCheckOverlay`'s old `endsWith('BASIC')` key heuristic; see the `ForceCheckOverlay` section for the audit behind it
- `allForcePowers` matches a character's owned upgrade to its tree position via `(power_key, tree_row, tree_col)`, not by ability key — ability keys get renamed across reSpec snapshot reseeds (e.g. migration 102), but a purchase's tree position is permanent

### `useBonusSkillKeys(skillModifiers, talents, refTalentMap, speciesAbilities)`
- Memoized `Set<string>` of skill keys that have bonus dice (boosts or setback removal)
- Computed from talent modifiers and species ability `affected_skills`

### `useIsMobile(breakpoint?)`
- Responsive breakpoint detection via `matchMedia`; defaults to 768px
- Returns: `boolean`
- Used by `src/app/character/[id]/page.tsx` to branch between `PlayerHUDDesktop` (≥768px) and `MobileShell` (<768px) — breakpoint value (768) unchanged by the Mobile Shell Rebuild task; still the only consumer project-wide

### `useTicker(text, isOpen, delayMs?)`
- Drives the character-scramble "ticker" animation for panel headers
- Exports `TickerChar` type: `{ key: string; display: string; settled: boolean }`
- When `isOpen` flips true: after `delayMs` (default 60ms), reveals chars left-to-right with 28ms stagger; each scramblable char cycles through 4 random glyphs at 35ms before landing
- Spaces and non-alphanumeric chars are never scrambled — they appear immediately at their stagger position with `settled: true`
- When `isOpen` flips false: immediately resets all chars to their final settled values and cancels all pending timeouts
- All timeouts cleaned up on unmount
- Returns: `{ chars: TickerChar[] }`

## Data Layer

### Supabase Tables (30+ tables, key ones)

**Characters & Skills**
- `characters` — base stat block, XP, credits, backstory
- `character_skills` — per-character skill rank overrides
- `character_talents` — purchased talents with tree position + xp_cost
- `character_specializations` — purchased specs, in purchase order
- `character_force_abilities` — purchased Force power upgrades
- `character_critical_injuries` — tracked injuries
- `character_sig_ability_nodes` — Signature Abilities Phase 1: purchased sig-ability tree nodes (base + upgrades). `spec_slot` (migration 090) records which `specialization_key` the ability was locked in against, since a character can lock a different signature ability per in-career spec. No FK on `sig_ability_key` (dataset-keyed, matches migration 062's convention). No RLS (matches every other ref/character-progress table added alongside it).
- `character_conflicts` — Force-user morality conflict log; `description` = type label; `narrative` = optional body; `player_acknowledged` = false until player dismisses notification
- `character_sessions` — active session keys (with player's chosen `ui_theme`: 'binary-sunset'|'operative'|'kyber'); cleared on disconnect via `/api/release-session`
- `critical_injury_requests` — GM-initiated requests for crit injury rolls (status: pending/applied)

**Inventory**
- `character_weapons` — equipped state + stow location
- `character_armor` — equipped state + stow location
- `character_gear` — equipped state + stow location + quantity
- `ref_armor.restricted` / `ref_gear.restricted` (migration 128, boolean, default false) — `ref_weapons` has always had this column; `scripts/parse-oggdude.ts`'s `parseArmor()`/`parseGear()` never read `<Restricted>` at all, a seeder gap (not a source-data gap), found while building The Archive Market feature (legality/tier rules need "is this restricted" on every item table, not just weapons). Fixed in the seeder itself (both functions now call a shared `isRestricted()` helper) so a future reseed won't regress. Backfilled from `oggdude/DataCustom/Armor.xml`/`Gear.xml` (items are OggDude-canonical per this file's Dataset Architecture section — NOT `respec project data/`, which is a non-canonical copy for these two tables and disagrees on `<Restricted>` counts). Verified counts: 43/112 armor rows, 135/610 gear rows. One XML gear row (`DATABRBO`, "Blackops Data Breaker") has `<Restricted>true</Restricted>` written twice inside one block — a source-file typo, confirmed by direct inspection — which makes a naive text-grep read 136 instead of the correct de-duplicated 135 distinct restricted gear items; `isRestricted()` handles the resulting fast-xml-parser array value (`v.some(isRestricted)`) so this row isn't silently dropped either. All extracted restricted keys matched 1:1 against existing `ref_armor`/`ref_gear` rows by key — no alias table needed (unlike migration 122's category backfill).

**Campaign & Group**
- `campaigns` — name, gm_pin, settings JSONB, base_of_operations_name
- `campaign_settings` — single-row config table. `active_dataset TEXT DEFAULT 'oggdude'` controls which reference dataset (OggDude vs reSpec) is active across the app.
- `players` — display_name, is_gm, campaign_id
- `group_assets` — vehicles, starships, safe houses, NPCs, strategic assets. `is_group_storage BOOLEAN DEFAULT false` (migration 081) flags an asset as a shared storage pool visible to players via "View Storage" UI.
- `quartermaster` — One row per campaign. `is_open` controls player access. `sell_pct` is the buy-back rate (default 25%).
- `quartermaster_items` — Items stocked in the QM. `item_key` + `item_type` identify the ref table row (`ref_weapons`, `ref_armor`, `ref_gear`).
- `encounters` — is_active, round, current_turn, initiative_order JSONB
- `encounter_tokens` — slot_key, character_id, adversary_id, vehicle_id, position
- `gm_award_log` — GM XP/credits/loot awards (written by `useGmAwards`). A `loot_awards` table was documented here historically but has never existed in the database.
- `destiny_pool` (via campaign_settings) — light/dark pip counts
- `session_roll_state` — per-campaign Duty/Obligation roll results for a session
- `pending_damage` — queued damage applications from combat checks
- `pending_actions` (migration 117) — durable queue of GM/system actions awaiting a **player** decision, the counterpart to `pending_damage`'s player-action-awaiting-GM direction. Columns: `campaign_id`, `character_id`, `action_type` (CHECK: `initiative | destiny_generate | critical_injury | conflict_ack | vendor_offer | loot_reveal | gm_dialog | force_rating_offer`), `status` (CHECK: `pending | resolved | cancelled`), `is_blocking`, `payload` jsonb, `result_payload` jsonb, `source_ref`, `created_at`, `resolved_at`. Partial unique index `uq_pending_actions_open (character_id, action_type, source_ref) WHERE status = 'pending'` is the idempotency guard. Indexed on `(character_id, status)` for the player hook and `(campaign_id, status)` for future GM-side visibility. RLS enabled with permissive `SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE USING (true)` policies matching `003_rls_policies.sql`/`028` — there is no GM/player auth split, so an `auth.uid()` policy would lock out every row. `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL` + in the `supabase_realtime` publication. Read by `usePendingActions`, written by `createPendingAction`/`cancelPendingAction`/`cancelPendingActionsByType` in `useGmBroadcast.ts`. **Initiative is the first wired action type** — `source_ref` is `InitiativeSetupModal`'s per-mount setup-session id (never `stagingEncounter.id`, and never null), and `result_payload` carries `{successes, advantages, skillKey, skillName}`, which is the only persistent record of a submitted roll until Lock Order & Start writes it into `combat_encounters.initiative_slots`. **`critical_injury_requests` remains authoritative for injury state** — `action_type = 'critical_injury'` rows carry that request's id in `source_ref` and are a pointer plus presentation layer only; nothing was migrated out of it.

**Market**
- `market_merchants` (migration 127; `seed` column added by migration 129) — the single active GM-generated merchant per campaign, for the Market rail panel (`GmMarketPanel.tsx`, Prompt 2). Ephemeral: no history table — long-term storage is a downloadable JSON snapshot (`src/lib/marketSnapshot.ts`), not this table. In this design a campaign has at most one merchant ROW ever — the first "Roll Stock" INSERTs + activates via the RPC; every subsequent "Roll Stock" regenerates that same row's fields in place, so `is_active` is written exactly once per campaign, only by the RPC, never by a direct `.update()`. Columns: `campaign_id`, `name`, `location_modifier` (int), `archetype`, `scale`, `legality`, `is_active`, `is_open_to_players` (GM-controlled player visibility), `stock` jsonb (array of `{item_table, ref_key, name, base_rarity, modified_rarity, price, restricted, tier: 'open'|'back_room'|'under_counter', revealed}`), `seed` (int, nullable — the `marketGenerator.ts` mulberry32 seed used to roll `stock`, so the exact same roll can be reproduced given the same catalogue+params; intentionally NOT part of `marketSnapshot.ts`'s exported JSON contract, which Prompt 2 was scoped to consume, not change), `created_by`, `created_at`/`updated_at`. Price is display-only and GM-editable — nothing in this system computes, deducts, or validates credits, and nothing writes to any `character_*` table. One-active-per-campaign enforced the same way as `maps.is_active` (migration 097): partial unique index `idx_market_merchants_one_active (campaign_id) WHERE is_active = true` plus a `SECURITY DEFINER` function `set_active_merchant(campaign_id, merchant_id)` wrapping clear-then-set in one transaction, so Realtime subscribers never see a committed zero-active gap. RLS: permissive `USING (true)` on all four operations (no GM/player auth split in this app), matching `pending_actions`. `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL` + in the `supabase_realtime` publication. Live-verified via anon-key REST: insert/read under RLS, the RPC atomically flipping the active row between two merchants, and a direct bypass `PATCH` correctly rejected with `23505` by the partial unique index. Also live-verified through the real UI (Playwright): rapid-click safety (10 synchronous same-tick clicks on 10 distinct rows' Reveal buttons → all 10 persisted, zero lost updates through `useMarketMerchant.ts`'s serialized write queue) and a full snapshot round-trip (download → mutate the live merchant → restore from the pre-mutation file → re-download → byte-identical).
  - **Player projection (migration 130, The Archive Market Prompt 3).** `stock` mixes public (open/revealed) and secret (unrevealed back-room/under-counter) lines in one jsonb column, and this table's RLS is permissive (`USING(true)`) like every other table in the app — RLS is row-level and can't redact keys inside a JSON blob. A player subscribing to raw `postgres_changes` on `market_merchants` would receive the entire row, secrets included, in the WebSocket frame itself, regardless of what client code chooses to read — so players never do that. Two SQL functions instead: `market_player_projection(m market_merchants) RETURNS jsonb` computes the safe shape once (`visible_lines` = every line with `tier='open'` OR `revealed=true`, regardless of which tier it's currently assigned; `locked_tiers` = `back_room`/`under_counter`, ALWAYS both present with a `difficulty_rarity` computed from every line assigned to that tier whether revealed or not — the array is never filtered down, so an empty locked tier and a stocked one produce byte-identical shapes); `get_player_market_view(p_campaign_id)` (RPC, catch-up — a plain HTTPS call via PostgREST, never part of the realtime WAL stream) and a `trg_market_merchants_broadcast` trigger (`AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE`, calls `realtime.send(market_player_projection(NEW), 'market_update', 'market:'||campaign_id, private:=false)`) both call this one function, so catch-up and live updates can never drift apart. The trigger fires on every write regardless of which client code performed it (reveal, tier cycle, roll, restore, open/close), so no application code needs to know a player might be listening — `useMarketMerchant.ts`/`GmMarketPanel.tsx`'s reveal handlers are untouched. Network-proof (Playwright, anon-key REST, and a raw WebSocket-frame capture): an unrevealed item's name is absent from every HTTP RPC response and every WS frame the player's browser receives, verified against a merchant with a stocked back room. Live-verified end-to-end: a GM reveal (single row or whole tier) reaches an already-open player storefront with no reload (a few seconds' realtime propagation, not instant); an empty and a stocked locked tier render identically to a player.

**Maps**
- `maps` — campaign map tiles: image_url, grid config, token_scale, is_active, is_visible_to_players, planet_id
- `map_tokens` — token positions on the active map: x/y, participant_type, wound_pct, is_visible, token_size
- `map_planets` — planet folder groupings for maps; used to organise map library by location

**Reference Data (read-only)**
- `ref_species` — species definitions
- `ref_careers` — composite PK `(key, dataset_source)`. Added `dataset_source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'oggdude'` and `is_retired BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false` in migration 062.
- `ref_specializations` — composite PK `(key, dataset_source)`. Same additions as `ref_careers` (migration 062).
- `ref_talents` — composite PK `(key, dataset_source)`. Same additions as `ref_careers` (migration 062).
- `ref_skills`, `ref_weapons`, `ref_armor`, `ref_gear` — all four now carry a `categories TEXT[]` column (migration 122 added it to `ref_armor`/`ref_gear`, matching `ref_weapons.categories`'s pre-existing shape exactly). Populated via `scripts/backfill-item-categories.ts` (idempotent, `--dry-run` supported) from `respec project data/Armor.xml`/`Gear.xml` — Armor's category data is shaped like Weapons (`<Categories><Category>`), Gear's lives in a single `<Type>` element instead. `ARMOR_KEY_ALIASES` in that script maps 8 DB keys whose spelling diverges from the XML `<Key>` for the same item (e.g. DB `SITHPAIN` -> XML `SITHPAINHARNESS`; found by cross-checking `Name` after a dry-run reported them as unmatched on both sides) — same alias-map pattern as migrations 108-110 for specializations.
- `item_icon_overrides` — campaign-scoped GM-pinned image per catalogue item (migration 123). See migration table for shape/RLS.
- `ref_force_powers`
- `ref_force_abilities` — composite PK `(key, dataset_source)`. Same additions as `ref_careers` (migration 062).
- `ref_critical_injuries`, `ref_item_attachments`
- `ref_item_descriptors`, `ref_weapon_qualities`
- `ref_obligations`, `ref_duties`, `ref_morality`
- `ref_adversaries`, `ref_vehicles`, `ref_starships`
- `ref_sig_abilities` — Signature Abilities Phase 1 (migration 088). Composite PK `(key, dataset_source)`, `is_retired`, `career_key` (no FK — dataset-keyed, matches migration 062's convention).
- `ref_sig_ability_nodes` — tree nodes for each `ref_sig_abilities` row: `row_index` 0 = base node (`col_span=4`, spans the full width — OggDude's source XML encodes this by repeating the same node key across all 4 ability slots rather than an explicit span field), `row_index >= 1` = upgrade nodes (4 per row, `col_span=1`). `connect_up/down/left/right` booleans drive the same adjacency-gated purchasability + connector-line pattern as `TalentTree.tsx`/`buildTalentTree.ts`, adapted locally in `TalentSurface.tsx` (formerly `HudTalentTreeModal.tsx`, retired).

> **Dropped FK constraints (migration 062):** The following FK constraints were removed when the four ref tables above changed from `key TEXT PRIMARY KEY` to composite `(key, dataset_source)` PKs:
> `character_talents_talent_key_fkey`, `character_specializations_specialization_key_fkey`, `characters_career_key_fkey`, `ref_specializations_career_key_fkey`.

**Combat Reference**
- `combat_encounters` — one persistent encounter deck per `(campaign_id, map_id)` (unique index, `map_id` added in migration 115); holds the roster (`adversaries`/`vehicles` JSONB), `initiative_slots`, and combat status. `is_active` = combat is live on this row, NOT row existence. Separate from the `encounters` initiative tracker.
- `combat_participants` — per-character combat state (wounds, strain, weapon display)
- `combat_log` — timestamped combat event entries for a single encounter
- `adversary_token_images` — custom portrait/token images for adversary entries
- `vehicle_token_images` — custom portrait/token images for vehicle entries
- `custom_talents` — GM-created talents (migration 049)

### Supabase Realtime
Every hook that manages live data opens its own channel. Pattern:
```
supabase.channel(`channel-name-${id}`)
  .on('postgres_changes', { event: '*', schema: 'public', table: 'table_name', filter: `col=eq.${id}` }, handler)
  .subscribe()
```
Cleanup via `useEffect` return → `supabase.removeChannel(channel)`.

### Storage
- Bucket `portraits` — character portrait images (uploaded via `/api/fal` or direct upload)
- Bucket `maps` — campaign map tiles (referenced from `maps.image_url`)

## Component Hierarchy

### Tier 1 — Page Shells (route-level)
```
/character/[id]/page.tsx
  └── PlayerHUDDesktop          (3-row CSS grid: 64px rail · centre · right column)
        ├── HudTopBar           (row 1 — logo, portrait chip, character name, campaign/XP meta)
        ├── HudStatusStrip      (row 2 — wounds ±, strain ±, encumbrance bar, crit pips)
        ├── HudLeftRail         (row 3 left — 64px icon rail; quick action buttons open HudQuickDrawers; nav buttons open HudFullPanels; utility buttons for dice/adversaries; bottom-most Hand tuck toggle, player-only)
        ├── HudRightColumn      (row 3 right — full-height RollFeedPanel)
        ├── HudSessionTab       (row 3 centre — map canvas + initiative strip + quick drawers)
        │     ├── HudQuickDrawer (combat/force/skill — 260px, position:absolute, slide from left)
        │     └── InitiativeStrip (compact mode, position:absolute bottom:0)
        └── HudFullPanel (skills/talents/force-panel/inventory/lore/group — 82%, position:absolute, slide from left)

`/character/[id]/page.tsx` (mobile branch — `useIsMobile` < 768px)
  └── `MobileShell`             (full-height flex column — owns all data hooks; mount point, replaces the deleted `MobileHudLayout`)
        ├── `MobileHeader`      (fixed top — avatar/name/identity line, Map + Notifications icon buttons, 3-cell Wounds/Strain/Soak vitals row)
        ├── [body — flex:1, one `.m-body-page` scroll container per destination, toggled via `hidden` so each keeps its own scroll position]
        │     ├── `MobileAbilitiesDestination` (real, Prompt 2/7 — search-first Skills/Talents/Force/Trees surface, see below)
        │     └── destination placeholder content (Sheet/Gear/Party — real content lands in later prompts)
        └── `MobileNav`         (fixed bottom — Sheet/Abilities/Gear/Party, centre Roll button raised above the bar)

#### Mobile Shell Rebuild (this task) — hard cutover, Prompt 1/7

The entire prior mobile branch (`MobileHudLayout`, `MobileRunner`, `MobileBottomNav`, `MobileIdentityBar`, `MobileVitalsStrip`, `MobileDestinySpendSheet`, `MobileStowLocationSheet`, `MobileTabBar`, `MobileSessionCompanion` and every file under `screens/`/`tabs/`, plus `shared/BottomSheet.tsx`) was **deleted, not extended** — no mobile sessions were running, so no migration path was needed. `src/components/mobile/` now holds only: `MobileShell.tsx`, `MobileHeader.tsx`, `MobileNav.tsx`, `MobileBottomSheet.tsx` (kept in place as the sheet primitive for prompts 2-7's check panels — portaled via `createPortal`, token-compliant; `shared/BottomSheet.tsx` was the redundant non-portaled alternative and was deleted instead), and `overlays/DiceRollerSheet.tsx` (kept — desktop's `FloatingDiceRollerFAB.tsx` still imports it; relocating it out of `mobile/` is separate housekeeping, out of this task's scope). The known Force-roll `logRoll` bug (Force rolls from the old `MobileDiceScreen` never logging) is resolved by that file's deletion, not a fix.

- `MobileShell.tsx` — owns the same data hooks the old layout did (`useCharacterData`, `useDerivedStats`), plus `usePendingActions` (new here — unread-notification dot). Resolves the header's "epithet/species/rank" line as `[specialization or career name, species name]` via `refCareers`/`refSpeciesAll`/`refSpecMap` lookups already returned by `useCharacterData` — `Character` has no literal `epithet` or rank field, so specialization (falling back to career) stands in for it. Destination selection (`MobileDestination` — `'sheet' | 'abilities' | 'gear' | 'party'`) is local `useState`, so it persists for the life of the mount; each destination renders inside its own `.m-body-page` div toggled via the `hidden` attribute rather than being unmounted, so switching away and back preserves that destination's scroll position with no new dependency.
```
