```
- `MobileHeader.tsx` — avatar (portrait or initials), name, identity line, Map + Notifications icon buttons (48×48, both inert this prompt), and a 3-cell Wounds/Strain/Soak vitals row. All three vital cells are real, enabled `<button>`s at identical size (`min-height: 48px`) with a no-op `onClick` rather than `disabled` — `disabled` would dim the cell (existing `:disabled{opacity:.4}` convention) and misreport "unavailable" instead of "not wired up yet." Wounds/Strain show a `current/max` label plus a 3px fill bar (`Math.round(pct*100)}%` width — the one documented inline-style exception, a live per-render percentage with no CSS-class equivalent); Soak shows a bare value, no bar.
- `MobileNav.tsx` — four destinations as equal-width flex items (`Sheet`/`Abilities`/`Gear`/`Party`) plus a sibling Roll button `position: absolute; left: 50%` on the nav bar itself — landing exactly on the flex midpoint (the boundary between Abilities and Gear, the 2nd/3rd of 4 equal columns) without a grid-span hack. Roll button is 64×64px, inert this prompt.
- No top runner exists anywhere in the new shell — `MobileRunner.tsx` is deleted outright, not folded into `MobileNav`.
- **Mobile legibility tokens (new, additive-only)**: `state-tokens.css`'s `[data-mobile-shell]` block defines `--hud-mobile-{bg,panel,surface,border,border-hi,text,text-dim,text-faint,gold,accent,accent-hi}` — a fixed, higher-contrast Ember Tatooine variant that does **not** respond to `[data-theme]`/`ThemeSwitcher` (mobile always renders this palette regardless of the player's desktop theme choice). Exported to JS as `HUD_MOBILE` in `tokens.ts`, alongside two other new additive exports: `MOBILE_TAP_MIN = 48` (fixed geometry constant, not a spacing-scale value) and `VITAL` (`{wounds, strain}`, wrapping the pre-existing but previously-unexported `--hud-vital-wounds`/`--hud-vital-strain` CSS vars). No existing token name or CSS var value was changed — confirmed via `git diff` showing zero removed/altered lines in either file, addition-only. Computed contrast (WCAG relative-luminance formula): body text `--hud-mobile-text` (`#F5EDE3`) on `--hud-mobile-bg` (`#1C1512`) ≈ **15.5:1**; secondary text `--hud-mobile-text-dim` (`#C9B8A8`) on the same background ≈ **9.4:1**; both against the raised `--hud-mobile-surface`/`--hud-mobile-panel` background (`#2C201A`) ≈ **13.6:1** / **8.2:1**. All exceed the 7:1 / 4.5:1 floors with margin. `DICE_COLOR`/`DICE_META`/`SYM`/`SYM_COLOR` (the sealed dice-identity namespace) are untouched — this shell doesn't render dice yet, and the mobile palette work never touches that namespace regardless.
- New stylesheet `src/styles/mobile-shell.css` (imported by `globals.css` after `animations.css`), all classes `.m-`-prefixed to guarantee zero collision with any desktop class. Smallest font-size token used anywhere in it is `var(--text-label)` (11px floor) — `--text-overline`/`--text-caption` (which dip to 9px/10px at small viewports) are deliberately never used in this file.

#### Abilities Destination — Prompt 2/7

Reference-only surface (search + full talent/signature-ability tree view); zero XP spend, zero write path — the Purchase button in the detail sheet is `disabled` with a `TODO(prompt 2b)` comment and is never wired to a handler. Skill/Force-power taps are no-op `TODO(prompt 3)` stubs (their check panels don't exist yet). Reuses every piece of tree logic verbatim from its existing location rather than duplicating it: `buildCharacterTalentTree`/`adaptSigAbilityNodes` (`src/lib/buildTalentTree.ts`, both already pure/extracted — no desktop component embeds this logic) and `useConnectorLayout` (`src/hooks/useConnectorLayout.ts`, the same measured-DOM connector-bar hook `TalentTree.tsx`/`TalentSurface.tsx` share). No new Supabase query anywhere — every field comes from `useCharacterData`'s existing return plus one new `useForcePowers()` call fed from fields it already returns.

- `MobileAbilitiesDestination.tsx` (Part A — search surface): sticky search input + a horizontally-scrolling filter chip row (All/Skills/Talents/Force/Trees, each showing a live count that reacts to both the search query and the active chip), results grouped by type with an uppercase group label (a group renders only when it has ≥1 result). Row data sources: **Skills** — every `HudSkill` (all skills, untrained included, matching `HudSkillQuickList`'s own convention), right-hand element is small `DICE_META`-coloured dice glyphs from `getSkillPool(charVal, rank)`. **Talents** — `hudTalents` (owned-only, matching `HudTalentsScreen`'s historical convention), right-hand element is a `×N` rank indicator when `refTalentMap[key].is_ranked`. **Force** — `allForcePowers.filter(fp => fp.purchasedCount > 0)` from the new `useForcePowers()` call (same owned-only filter `HudForceTab.tsx` already applies), right-hand element is the character's currently *available* Force dice (`getAvailableForceDice(forceRating, character.force_rating_committed)`) — not a per-power stat, since none exists; the same figure appears on every Force row by design. **Trees** — one row per owned specialization (`charSpecs`) plus one per locked-in signature ability (`Object.values(lockedSigAbilities)`, `buildTreeTabs()` in `MobileAbilityTree.tsx`), chevron only, tapping opens Part B scoped to that tree. Type-bar colours are `HUD_MOBILE.{gold,accent,force,sig}` (two new mobile-only tokens added this prompt, see below) — never raw hex. Tapping a talent row opens the Part C detail sheet directly (it's already owned, so its state is always `'owned'` from a search-row tap — only tapping a node inside the Part B tree can surface `'available'`/`'locked'`).
- `MobileAbilityTree.tsx` (Part B — tree view): a normalizer (`NormNode`) unifies `TalentTreeNode` (5-row spec trees) and `AbilityTreeNode` (3-row signature-ability trees, row 0 spanning all 4 columns via `grid-column: 1 / -1`) into one renderable shape so one grid renderer serves both — **signature ability trees are 3 rows, not 5, and the grid's `grid-template-rows` is sized per-tree from real data, never hardcoded to 5.** Tab strip (`buildTreeTabs()`) supports any number of specializations and locked-in signature abilities with no hardcoded limit — live-verified against Zid Hag (3 owned specializations, the only 3+-spec character in the live DB), switching cleanly between all three (`4/20`, `4/20`, `7/20` XP-node counts, screenshot-verified). No character in the live DB has a locked-in signature ability yet (`character_sig_ability_nodes` has zero `row_index = 0` rows) — the sig-tab code path (`buildTreeTabs` appending a `'sig'`-kind tab whenever `lockedSigAbilities` is non-empty, same `{name, purchased, total}` tab shape) is verified by code trace only, not a live click-through. Connections render via `useConnectorLayout` exactly as the desktop trees do — measured DOM bars in a `position:absolute` `.m-tree-connectors` layer, `.is-active` when both endpoints are owned; live-verified against Zid Hag's Blockade Runner tree (17 real bars measured, e.g. `0-2>1-2` reporting `active:true` because both "Galaxy Mapper" and "Grit" are owned). **Rank pips render filled-owned-only with no maximum ceiling** — matches `TalentTree.tsx`'s own documented convention (`ownedRank` count, no per-talent max-rank data exists anywhere in the schema to draw a ceiling against; verified via that file's own comment). The 5×4 grid (`gap:14px`, node `min-height:88px`) measures **496px** tall for a full spec tree (5×88 + 4×14, exactly as specced) against a **~499.7px** available `.m-tree-scroll` height on a real 390×844 viewport (Zid Hag, Blockade Runner) — a ~10px genuine overflow from the legend row beneath the grid, so `.m-tree-scroll` scrolls vertically as the spec explicitly permits when a tree "genuinely exceeds the available height," not because of an oversized/wrong layout. Node labels are the one documented exception to the file's 11px floor — 10px (`.m-tree-node-name`, `-webkit-line-clamp:3`), commented inline, since a full name is always reachable in the detail sheet.
- `MobileTalentDetailSheet.tsx` (Part C): hosted in the extended `MobileBottomSheet` (see below) with a fixed footer (Close + disabled Purchase). Its "what unlocks this / what it opens" line (`buildUnlockText()` in `MobileAbilityTree.tsx`) is derived **only** from the tree's real `TalentTreeConnection[]` edges touching that node's cell key (checked in both directions, since an edge is recorded once — right/down, plus the signature tree's base→row-1 "up" edge — but is functionally bidirectional for adjacency purposes) — never a hand-written per-node string.
- `MobileBottomSheet.tsx` extended (not replaced): new optional `footer?: ReactNode` prop, rendered outside the scrollable `.m-sheet-body` in a `flex-shrink:0` `.m-sheet-footer` region, so Part C's footer never scrolls with the description. Converted from ad-hoc inline styles to `.m-sheet-*` classes in `mobile-shell.css` in the same pass — `position` now lives entirely in those classes, never inline, per the standing rule that an inline `position` on a drawer/overlay wrapper silently overrides its class and breaks containment. Also now sets `data-mobile-shell=""` on its own portaled root (it portals to `document.body`, outside `MobileShell`'s DOM subtree, so without this the `[data-mobile-shell]`-scoped `--hud-mobile-*` custom properties wouldn't inherit into it at all).
- **Two new mobile-only colour tokens** (additive, same pattern as Prompt 1's `HUD_MOBILE` set): `--hud-mobile-force` (purple) and `--hud-mobile-sig` (teal), added to `state-tokens.css`'s `[data-mobile-shell]` block and exported as `HUD_MOBILE.force`/`HUD_MOBILE.sig` in `tokens.ts`. Identity/accent colours only (type bars, tab borders) — no text contrast requirement applies to them, and neither touches the sealed `DICE_COLOR`/`DICE_META` namespace.

#### Combat Check — Prompt 3a/7

Full-screen flow (`position:fixed` portal to `document.body`, `z-index:500`), launched from `MobileNav`'s Roll button, wired at `MobileShell.tsx` (owns a `combatCheckOpen` boolean and one new hook call, `useEncounterState(campaignId)`, feeding `encounter?.id`). Reuses every calculation verbatim from its existing pure-function location — `getSkillPool`, `getRangedDifficulty`, `getDualWieldPool`, `applyModifiers`, `upgradeAbility`, `upgradeDifficulty`, `totalPoolDice`, `formatResultSummary` (all `dice-engine.ts`/`combatCheckUtils.ts`), `checkCriticalEligibility` (`criticalUtils.ts`) — zero pool/difficulty/upgrade/dual-wield/crit maths reimplemented anywhere in the new files. Live-verified (Playwright, Guy Rando — 2 equipped "Blaster Pistol" rows, one melee vibrodagger): a ranged check, a dual-wield check (matched skill penalty), and a melee check all produced correct pools and rolled successfully with zero console errors.

- **Two flagged deviations from a literal desktop mirror, both required by Step 0's own findings**: (1) `findDualWieldCandidates` is desktop's module-private, unexported function inside `WeaponSelectStep.tsx` (a do-not-modify file) — `MobileWeaponStep.tsx` carries its own copy, composing only the already-pure `canDualWield`/`isRangedSkill`, no new eligibility rule invented. (2) This prompt's own FLOW spec has no target-selection step for melee (`Weapon → Pool → Result`, not `Weapon → Target → Pool`) — melee difficulty is therefore entirely player-set starting at 0 (no opponent-resistance lookup, unlike desktop's `getMeleeDifficulty`-against-`encounterEnemies` flow), and `pending_damage` is consequently never written from this panel (the same `selectedTargets.length > 0` gate desktop uses is simply never satisfied here, not a different write path).
- `MobileCombatCheck.tsx` — orchestrator and the only file that writes. Same write sequence as `CombatCheckOverlay.tsx`, verified table-by-table against a live campaign (Guy Rando, campaign `6ba1c2c4-…`): (1) `combat_participants` update (`active_weapon_name/key`, `secondary_weapon_name/key`, `.eq(campaign_id).eq(character_id)`) on weapon select, dual-wield confirm, close, and new-attack — confirmed via direct SELECT after a roll. (2) `roll_log` insert via the shared `logRoll()` (`lib/logRoll.ts`) — never reimplemented, called with the identical field shape `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx`'s `handleRoll` uses; confirmed live (`roll_type:'combat', weapon_name:'Blaster Pistol', range_band:'medium'` row present immediately after a roll) — this is the exact class of write the old mobile dice screen used to silently skip for Force rolls, and it does not recur here. (3) `combat_log` insert, same columns/order as desktop, `encounter_id` resolved from the `useEncounterState` prop with the identical `combat_encounters` `.single()` SELECT fallback desktop uses when no encounter is pre-seeded; confirmed live (`result_summary:"Blaster Pistol at Medium — 1 Success · 2 Advantage"`). (4) `character_weapons` equip writes (`handleEquipWeapon`, same table/columns) — the carried-weapon "Equipping costs a Maneuver" confirm flow is preserved in `MobileWeaponStep.tsx`, not dropped for scope.
- **Range-step delta vs desktop's `difficultyAdd`**: mobile's Range step (`MobileRangeStep.tsx`) shows a signed delta stepper on the SAME `adjustments.difficultyAdd` field the Pool step's Difficulty deck control edits — not a second field. Selecting a different band resets `difficultyAdd` to 0 (every other adjustment field is untouched); live-verified (added 1 Boost on the Pool step, navigated back, changed Short→Medium, returned — Boost still present, 1 added die). This reset-on-band-change is a **deliberate mobile-only UX addition, not a maths difference** — desktop never resets `difficultyAdd` on band change at all, but for any single given band+delta+adjustments combination, both platforms compute the identical pool through the identical `applyModifiers` call.
- **A real bug, found and fixed via live testing**: `handleSelectWeapon` originally auto-advanced to the next step immediately on weapon selection — which unmounts the Weapon step (mobile renders only the current step, unlike desktop's always-mounted accordion) before the player can reach the dual-wield toggle that "lives on this step, not the range step" per spec. Fixed by staying on the Weapon step after selection; advancing is now only via the explicit footer Next/Back, matching the step rail's own semantics.
- `MobileWeaponStep.tsx` (Step 1): equipped + carried weapons + the synthetic unarmed sentinel (`id:'__unarmed__'`, same construction as desktop's `WeaponSelectStep.tsx`), All/Ranged/Melee filter chips, quality chips, and the inline dual-wield panel (toggle → off-hand picker → side-by-side cards with swap and the plain-language penalty line — no separate review step, confirmed live: `"Combined check uses the lower skill rank and the lower characteristic of the two weapons. Difficulty +1 (same skill)."`). **Quality chips are a mobile-local component, not `QualityBadge`** — Step 0 confirmed `QualityBadge` unconditionally wraps its content in `Tooltip.tsx`'s hover-only trigger (`onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave` only, no tap path), and nothing about `QualityBadge` itself is otherwise reusable around that constraint since it exposes no way to swap the trigger. `MobileWeaponStep.tsx` instead reads `refWeaponQualityMap` directly and tap-toggles an inline `.m-quality-desc` description (`RichText`-wrapped) below the chip row — live-verified against the Blaster Pistol's Stun Setting quality, full description rendered on tap. `STAGGER` excluded via the same `NON_QUALITY_KEYS` set convention `WeaponSelectStep.tsx` established; `Array.isArray` guards the qualities field (`BARADIUMCHRG`-style `{}` case).
- `MobileRangeStep.tsx` (Step 2, ranged only): 5-cell range grid via `getRangedDifficulty` per band, bands beyond the weapon's max range disabled-but-visible, band-specific penalty notes shown on the cell. Below the grid, the derivation bar + delta stepper described above.
- `MobilePoolStep.tsx` + `MobileModifierDeck.tsx` (Step 3): the pool readout marks each die as from-the-sheet (plain `DiceFace`) vs player-added (a small accent dot overlay) by diffing the final `applyModifiers` output against the pre-modifier base pool — presentational only, computed from the same numbers the roll uses, never a separate calculation. **`MobileModifierDeck.tsx` is the reusable component prompt 3b hosts again** — see its own file-header comment for the full contract. Props: `{ adjustments, onChange, extras, onExtrasChange, basePool, difficultyFloor?, expanded, onExpandedChange, onReset }`. `extras: {abilityAdd, proficiencyAdd}` exists because `ManualAdjustments` (`dice-engine.ts`, out of scope to edit) has no field for a raw Ability/Proficiency die add — the spec's "Rarely needed: Ability, Proficiency" steppers are implemented as a flat post-`applyModifiers` addition the caller applies (`finalPool.ability += extras.abilityAdd`), the same category of operation `boostAdd`/`forceAdd` already are inside `applyModifiers`, never routed through it. The upgrade steppers' result lines (`"N upgrade(s) converted M ability → M proficiency"`) are computed by calling `upgradeAbility`/`upgradeDifficulty` a second time against the base pool for display purposes — the same pure functions `applyModifiers` itself calls, never a value read off the stepper count. The expander is a controlled prop (`expanded`/`onExpandedChange`) owned by `MobileCombatCheck.tsx`, so "stays open for the duration of the check" holds regardless of what re-renders inside the deck.
- `MobileCombatResult.tsx`: banner, net-symbol pills, rolled `DiceFace`s with a symbol-icon overlay, and the critical block (`checkCriticalEligibility`-gated, same eligibility text desktop's `RollResultStep.tsx` shows) — laid out fresh for mobile rather than reusing that step's JSX (desktop tokens/layout throughout). Damage is shown as an unqualified "before soak" estimate since this prompt has no target, not a soak-adjusted final number.
- Contrast: no new text colours this prompt — reuses `HUD_MOBILE.text`/`textDim` (≈15.5:1 / ≈9.4:1, see Prompt 1's entry) throughout. Steppers are 44px buttons inside a 58px `.m-deck-row`/`.m-range-derivation` row (`MobileModifierDeck.tsx`, `MobileRangeStep.tsx`) — the one documented sub-48px exception, matching the established mobile pattern; every other interactive element (weapon rows, quality chips, dual-wield switch, range cells, footer buttons) is ≥48px in its smallest dimension.

#### Skill & Force Check Panels — Prompt 3b/7

`MobileRollChooser.tsx` — a `MobileBottomSheet` with three buttons (Combat/Skill/Force), replacing the direct Roll→Combat wiring from Prompt 3a. Force is `disabled` unless `isForceUserSensitive(character, forceRating)` (`lib/forceUtils.ts`, reused unmodified). `MobileShell.tsx` owns the chooser's open state plus `skillCheck`/`forceCheck` `{open, initialKey}` state — `MobileAbilitiesDestination`'s Skill/Force row taps (previously `TODO(prompt 3)` no-ops) now call `onOpenSkillCheck(key)`/`onOpenForceCheck(key)`, which open the matching panel pre-selected. Zero new Supabase queries anywhere in this prompt — every field (`forceRating`, `character.force_rating_committed`, `allForcePowers`, `encounter`) was already loaded by `MobileShell.tsx` in Prompts 1-3a.

**Skill panel** (`MobileSkillCheck.tsx`): a skill list (no `initialSkillKey`) or straight to the check screen (from an Abilities tap). Difficulty ladder (`DIFF_LABELS`, 6 rungs) + signed delta stepper reuse the exact `baseDifficulty`/`difficultyAdd` shape `HudSkillQuickList.tsx` already established (Prompt "Skill Difficulty Delta" — selecting a rung resets the delta to 0, the rung never re-selects itself when the delta pushes the effective value onto a different rung's number) — live-verified: selecting Hard then stepping the delta +1 shows `"Hard +1 → 4 difficulty"` with the ladder's selected cell still reading `"Hard"`, not jumping to Daunting. Pool math is `getSkillPool` + `applyModifiers` directly (not `buildSkillCheckPool`, which lacks a `challengeAdd`/extras surface for the deck) — same two pure functions, no reimplementation. Hosts `MobileModifierDeck`/`MobilePoolStep` and `MobileCombatResult` **completely unchanged** — `MobileCombatResult`'s `crit`/`secondary`/`netDamageEstimate` props are all nullable, so the skill panel passes `crit={null}` etc rather than forking a second result component. Write path replicates `HudSkillQuickList.handleRollClick` exactly: `onRoll?.(result, label, pool)` has no `meta`, so the shared `logRoll()` call carries no `roll_type`/`weapon_name` fields — confirmed live (`roll_type: null` on the resulting `roll_log` row, matching current desktop behavior byte for byte; a separate, much older `roll_log` row with `roll_type:'skill'` predates this and the current `HudSkillQuickList.tsx` — traced to the now-deleted pre-rebuild mobile dice screen, not a live discrepancy). No `combat_log`, no `pending_damage` — confirmed by omission (this path never touches either table).
- `MobileModifierDeck` is hosted with zero changes and zero combat-specific prop — confirmed by its own prop interface (`ModifierDeckProps`, `MobileModifierDeck.tsx`), which has no weapon/target/dual-wield field to begin with.

**Force panel** (`MobileForceCheck.tsx`) — three stages, no numeric stage variable, exactly mirroring `ForceCheckOverlay.tsx`'s own `stage1Done = !!selPower` / `stage2Done = !!forceRoll` gating (pip allocation UI only renders once `stage2Done`). **No modifier deck here** — this is a deliberate parity decision with desktop, which has no such control on a Force check; extra dice go through the general dice roller instead, not this panel.
- **Stage 1 (Power)**: lists `allForcePowers.filter(p => p.purchasedCount > 0)`, same filter as desktop's `purchased`. Labels the Force Rating figure explicitly as **dice**, not pips (`"Your Force Rating gives you N Force die/dice — these are the dice you are about to roll, not pips"`) — `available = getAvailableForceDice(forceRating, committedForce)`, live-verified against a character with `force_rating_committed = 1`: `available` correctly read 1, not the raw rating.
- **Stage 2 (Roll)**: `rollForceDice(available)` — square die glyphs (light `✦`/dark `✧` per die), no modifier controls anywhere on this stage.
- **Stage 3 (Allocate)**: `ForcePoint{kind,idx,dieIndex}`/`pointId`/`points`/`placedIds`/`lightSpentIdx`/`darkSpentIdx` and every handler (`handleRowTap`, `handleChipTap`, `handleTokenTap`) are a verbatim copy of `ForceCheckOverlay.tsx`'s own allocation model — `idx` is a per-kind sequence number across the whole roll (not per-die; `dieIndex` only matters for desktop's flight animation, which this panel doesn't reproduce). Upgrades are `selPower.abilities.filter(a => a.purchasedRanks > 0)`, basic (row-0) ability handled identically (allocatable, excluded from the write payload's `activated_upgrades`). **No cap anywhere** — `setAlloc` unconditionally pushes onto the target upgrade's array; `pip_cost` has exactly one use site in the whole file (`fp_cost: u.pip_cost` inside the notification payload's `activatedUpgrades` map), confirmed by grep, matching desktop's own single use site exactly. Live-verified: assigning a pip, tapping it back out of the upgrade (returns to the bank, `is-used` clears), light and dark pips both landing on one upgrade row's chip list.
- **Writes — `handleChannelForce`, verbatim from `ForceCheckOverlay.tsx`, including its known-odd `roll_log.result` mapping**: `netSuccess: lightSpent` (allocated light count), `netAdvantage: forceRoll.totalDark` (the TOTAL dark pips *rolled*, not `darkSpent`/allocated — a real inconsistency in the original, reproduced exactly per the Step 0 instruction not to "fix" it), `triumph: darkSpent`, `despair: 0`. Live-confirmed on a real roll (1 dark pip rolled, then returned to the bank before committing, so `darkSpent = 0`): the resulting `roll_log` row still shows `netAdvantage: 1` (from `totalDark`) alongside `triumph: 0`/`dark_pips_used: 0` — the exact quirk, reproduced byte-for-byte. `combat_log` insert confirmed live (`roll_type:'force power', weapon_name:'Commune', result:{totalFP:0, totalDark:1, totalLight:0, darkPipsUsed:0}`). `force_notifications` insert is correctly gated on `darkSpent > 0` (never fired in the verification run, since the test returned its only dark pip before committing) — same gate as desktop, not exercised differently. **No character mutation anywhere** — grepped the whole file: zero `supabase.from('characters').update(...)` calls; strain (`strain_cost: isDathomiri(character) ? 0 : darkSpent`) is carried only as a payload field on `force_notifications`/`roll_log.roll_meta`, exactly matching desktop's advisory-only behavior. `isDathomiri()` (`lib/dathomiriUtils.ts`) is reused as-is, a pure function of `character.species_key` already loaded — no new query.
- Pip assignment is local React state only (no server round-trip per tap) — every handler uses the functional `setState` form, which React serializes correctly on its own, so there is no race window a promise-chain queue would close. The one genuinely async action, the Commit write, is guarded by a `busy` boolean exactly as desktop's own `handleChannelForce` is guarded — a single in-flight write, not a per-row queue, since there is only one write action in this flow (unlike a market row's independent per-row read-modify-write).
- Force light/dark pip colours reuse `SYM_COLOR.lightPip`/`SYM_COLOR.darkPip` (`tokens.ts`, sealed dice-identity namespace) directly — no new mobile token, no raw hex.

#### Gear Destination — Prompt 4a/7

Encumbrance ledger, filtered item list, item detail sheet, and item state changes (equip/carry/stow-with-location, drop). Every write reuses `useCharacterData.ts`'s existing exported mutators verbatim — `handleSetEquipState`, `handleRemoveWeapon`, `handleRemoveEquipment` — passed straight through `MobileShell.tsx` → `MobileGearDestination.tsx` → `MobileItemDetailSheet.tsx` with zero new write logic anywhere in this prompt's files. This gets the promise-chain-per-row queue (`enqueueRowWrite`, a `Map<id, Promise>` chain already inside the hook) and the read-fresh-then-write pattern for free — nothing to reimplement or re-verify, since it's the exact same function desktop calls.

- **Two corrections to the prompt's own premises, both confirmed by Step 0 against live schema/code and resolved rather than improvised**: (1) there is no equip-slot exclusivity/swap/block system — `EquipSlot`'s own type comment states anchors are "deliberately non-exclusive... any item may attach to any anchor, any number of items may share one anchor," so a worn item's anchor is shown as plain text with no swap/block behavior on equip. The only real anchor exclusivity is a completely different, encumbrance-only mechanism (`ref_armor`/`ref_gear.worn_anchor`, inside `computeEncumbranceStats` — never blocks equipping, only silently denies one item's own cost-reduction/threshold-bonus per anchor). (2) There is no partial-quantity stow/drop write path anywhere in `useCharacterData.ts` (`character_gear.quantity` is a single column with zero split/increment logic) — stow and drop both act on the whole row/whole stack, exactly matching `handleSetEquipState`/`handleRemoveWeapon`/`handleRemoveEquipment`, never inventing a quantity-split schema change with no desktop counterpart.
- `MobileGearDestination.tsx` — normalizes `weapons`/`armor`/`gear` (raw arrays from `useCharacterData`, already loaded by earlier prompts) plus their ref maps into one `GearItem[]` list. Icon URLs are reused from `hudWeapons`/`hudArmor`/`hudGear`'s already-resolved `iconUrl` field (itself backed by `itemIconResolver.ts` inside the hook) via an id-keyed lookup built once in `MobileShell.tsx` — `itemIconResolver.ts` is never called a second time from mobile code. Encumbrance ledger (Part A): `current`/`threshold`/`cliff` read directly from `useCharacterData`'s existing `encumbranceStats` (`computeEncumbranceStats`, `derivedStats.ts` — pure, already computed, not reimplemented); the fill track's over-threshold segment and the cliff marker position are both derived from those same numbers. The derivation breakdown ("Base 5", "Brawn +N", one row per `capacitySources` entry) and the over-threshold penalty text ("Add [setback:N]…", "No free maneuver…") are the same comparisons `EncumbranceBar.tsx` (`src/components/character/`) already uses (`over = current - threshold`, `losesManeuver = over >= brawn`) — reused wording, not new copy. Live-verified against a real character (Derek Caabesk): ledger read `10/12`, derivation rows `Base 5 / Brawn +2 / Utility Belt +1 / Backpack +4 / Threshold 12` — matching `computeEncumbranceStats`'s formula exactly. Segment filter chips (All/Equipped/Carried/Stowed) with live counts, grouped list with a per-group encumbrance subtotal (sum of `perItem[id].cost` for that group, so a stowed group's subtotal is always 0) — live-verified counts `Equipped 5 · Carried 13 · Stowed 1` against 19 real items.
- `MobileItemDetailSheet.tsx` (Part C) — plate (icon, type+state eyebrow, name), a stat grid keyed by item type (weapon: DMG/Crit/Range/Enc; armor: Soak/Defense/Enc/Hard Points; gear: Qty/Enc), weapon quality chips via `MobileQualityChips` (see below — reused, not rebuilt), a detail-row list (carried-as + anchor, encumbrance — showing both worn and carried figures when they differ via `item.enc` vs `item.baseEnc`, rarity, value), an attachments line (`item.attachmentsCount` / `hardPoints`, or "No hard points. This item cannot take attachments." when `hardPoints` is 0 — verbatim from `item-detail-panel.tsx`'s own copy) with an explicit "Attachment management isn't available on mobile yet — view only" placeholder, an anchor-exclusivity note (`reasonNote()`, verbatim text copied from `item-detail-panel.tsx`'s own function of the same name, driven by the real `EncumbranceSuppressReason` from `encumbranceStats.perItem[id]`) and the description/effect text wrapped in `RichText`. Footer: one state button per real `EquipState` (`equipped`/`carrying`/`stowed` — confirmed the only three values the type has; no state is ever unreachable per Step 0, so no "why you can't" copy was needed — every button is always enabled). Drop lives as a separate, deliberately less-prominent `.m-drop-link` control inside the scrollable body, not a fourth footer button next to the routine state buttons.
- **`MobileQualityChips` extracted and exported from `MobileWeaponStep.tsx`** (Prompt 3a's file) specifically so this prompt could reuse the exact same tap-accessible chip component rather than building a third quality-chip implementation — `displayableQualities()` was exported alongside it. `MobileWeaponStep.tsx`'s own weapon-row rendering was updated to call the extracted component instead of its old inline JSX; behavior is unchanged (confirmed via `tsc`/build). `QualityBadge` (desktop) remains unreusable for the same reason established in Prompt 3a's Step 0 — it unconditionally wraps its content in `Tooltip.tsx`'s hover-only trigger.
- **Stow flow** (`MobileStowSheet.tsx`, new): tapping the Stowed footer button opens this sheet, listing real locations from `useStowLocations(campaignId)` (Base of Operations + `group_assets` filtered to vehicle/starship/safe_house) — live-verified against the same campaign used throughout these prompts: `"Hope (Home One-Class MC80 Cruiser)"` (Base) and `"Black Bantha" UT-60D U-wing Troop Transport` (starship). One flow, not two chained sheets — tapping a location immediately calls `onSetEquipState(id, type, 'stowed', location)`. Also hosts Drop (a `mode` prop) — quantity is shown as informational text only (`"This drops the entire stack of N"` when `qty > 1`) rather than an editable split, per the corrected premise above, followed by an explicit Cancel/Confirm Drop step before the destructive call fires.
- Live write verification, side by side with desktop (identical — same function called, not a lookalike): `handleSetEquipState(id, type, state, location?)` → `character_{weapons,armor,gear}` `update({equip_state, is_equipped, stow_location_*, equip_slot})` after a fresh `.select('id')` re-read, queued per-row; `handleRemoveWeapon`/`handleRemoveEquipment` → same tables, `update({is_dropped:true, dropped_at, dropped_by})`. Live-confirmed: tapping a state button updated the on-screen eyebrow and the ledger within the same round-trip desktop itself needs (two sequential Supabase calls — the fresh-read then the update — so the UI reflects the change once both resolve, not synchronously on tap; this is desktop's own latency profile, not a mobile-specific delay). Stowing an item dropped the ledger from `10/12` to `6/12` in the same live session, confirming the ledger recomputes immediately once `armor`/`weapons`/`gear` state updates.
- Contrast: no new text colours this prompt — reuses `HUD_MOBILE.text`/`textDim` throughout (see Prompt 1's computed ratios, unchanged). `.m-item-row`/`.m-location-row` are ≥56-64px; state buttons, drop link, and detail-row targets are all ≥48px.

#### Party Destination & Notifications — Prompt 5/7

Party holds what's shared (destiny pool, roll feed); Notifications is a separate full-height surface reached from the header icon, holding `pending_actions` that need a player response. `usePendingActions` is instantiated exactly **once**, in `MobileShell.tsx` (confirmed by grep — the only call site in the mobile tree), and its `actions`/`resolve`/`count` are all passed down from that one instance — the standing project rule (documented in `InitiativeRollModal.tsx`'s own comment: a second instance on the same `characterId` would open a second Realtime channel on the identical `pending-actions-<id>` topic and the two would collide). Two new hook calls in `MobileShell.tsx`, zero new tables: `useRollFeed(effectiveCampaignId)`, `useDestinyPool(effectiveCampaignId, characterId, character.name, supabase)`.

- **`MobilePartyDestination.tsx`** — destiny readout (`.m-destiny-bar`) above the feed. Light/dark points render as individual 48px tap-target pips from the real `destinyPool` array (`useDestinyPool`'s own state, synced over its existing `destiny-db-`/`destiny-pool-player-`/`destiny-`/`campaign-events-` channels — no new subscription added), each wired straight to `handleSpendDestiny(idx)` — the hook's own two-tap-confirm/read-fresh-write/broadcast sequence, not reimplemented. Live-verified against the real campaign: 6 pips (4 light, 2 dark) rendered and counted correctly; tapping a pip armed it (`is-armed`, matching the hook's `pendingSpend` index) exactly as desktop's two-tap confirm expects. **No GM-only control exists in `useDestinyPool` at all** (generation/award live in separate, out-of-scope GM hooks) — confirmed by reading the hook's full return shape, so nothing needed to be deliberately excluded; there was nothing GM-only to reach in the first place. Destiny pip identity colours (`#0EA5E9` light / `#A845F5` dark) are the same sealed hex `DestinyPoolDisplay.tsx` (desktop) uses — not a new mobile token, not reinterpreted.
- **Roll feed** — `[...rolls].reverse().filter(r => !r.hidden)`, the exact rule `RollFeedPanel.tsx` applies for a non-GM viewer (`!isGm ? ... : ...filter(r => !r.hidden)`); mobile is always the player's own device, so the filter is unconditional (no `isGm` branch needed at all). Cards are grouped by category the same way `classifyRoll()` (desktop) does — `force` / `initiative` / `system` / everything else — with compact single-line rows for `initiative`/`system` and a fuller card for skill/combat/force rolls, all noticeably smaller than desktop's own cards so more fit on screen at once. **Force entries read `roll_meta` fields directly** (`power_name`, `activated_upgrades`, `dark_pips_used`, `strain_cost`, `dice_results`) — the same fields desktop's `ForceCard` reads — never `result.netAdvantage`/`result.netSuccess`; the write-side hand-mapping quirk documented in Prompt 3b's Force write path (`netAdvantage` carrying `totalDark`) never surfaces on the read side on either platform, so there was nothing to reproduce or avoid here beyond matching the same field reads. Live-verified: a real Force roll (Bylethia Ford's "Commune", logged during Prompt 3b's own verification pass) rendered correctly as the first feed card, with its dice-result pips using `SYM_COLOR.lightPip`/`SYM_COLOR.darkPip` (sealed) exactly as `MobileForceCheck.tsx` already does. Net-result symbol pills for skill/combat rows reuse the same `SYM`-driven rendering pattern as `MobileCombatResult.tsx`'s `NetPill` (not literally imported — a second small local implementation of the same trivial symbol-lookup, since the prompt's "reuse, don't rebuild" instruction was scoped to the quality-chip and modifier-deck components specifically, not every small presentational helper).
- **`MobileNotificationsSheet.tsx`** — full-height portal (`position:fixed`, `z-index:500`, `createPortal` to `document.body`) exactly like `MobileCombatCheck.tsx`'s own root, so it is never trapped inside `.m-shell`'s or any ancestor's `backdrop-filter` containing block. Every one of the 8 real `action_type` values from the live CHECK constraint (`initiative`, `destiny_generate`, `critical_injury`, `conflict_ack`, `vendor_offer`, `loot_reveal`, `gm_dialog`, `force_rating_offer`) has an entry in the same `TYPE_META`/`describe()`/`titleFor()` maps `HudNotificationsDrawer.tsx` (desktop) uses — copied verbatim, not reworded. Per Step 0: **only `initiative` has real desktop resolve UI**; the other 6 (excluding `vendor_offer`) get desktop's own literal placeholder copy, *"This surface is not wired to the queue yet — resolve it from its own popup"* — an honest gap inherited from desktop, not invented for mobile. `vendor_offer` gets this prompt's own explicit placeholder (*"The storefront isn't available on mobile yet…"*), per the Prompt 4b deferral.
- **Initiative hosted with zero modification** — `InitiativeRollBody` (exported from `InitiativeRollModal.tsx`) is deliberately presentation-agnostic by its own doc comment ("Rendered in two places... One implementation, two hosts") and owns its complete promise-chain-queue (`queueRef`) + resolved-id guard already; `MobileNotificationsSheet.tsx` mounts it directly inside a notification card with no wrapper `Modal`, passing the single shared `resolvePendingAction`. **Live end-to-end verified with a real `pending_actions` row** (inserted for this verification, then resolved through the actual UI, not simulated): header dot appeared (`count:1`) → notification card rendered `"Initiative — Cool"` with the real dice-pool preview (Presence 1 · Rank 2, Y/G pips) → tapping "Roll Initiative" then "Submit" wrote through the same path desktop uses (`pending_actions.status → 'resolved'`, `result_payload: {skillKey:'COOL', skillName:'Cool', successes:1, advantages:0}`, confirmed via direct DB read) → header dot cleared and the card disappeared, both reactively, with no manual refresh.
- Contrast: no new text colours — reuses `HUD_MOBILE.text`/`textDim` throughout; the two destiny hex values are the one documented sealed exception (identity colour, not body text, no contrast requirement applies). Destiny pips and every notification/feed tap target are ≥48px.

#### Marketplace Storefront — Prompt 4b/7

**`MobileMarketStorefront.tsx`** — player-facing merchant browse view, reached by tapping "Open Storefront" on a `vendor_offer` notification card (`MobileNotificationsSheet.tsx`, replacing Prompt 5's deferral placeholder). Step 0 audit found **no purchase write path exists anywhere in the codebase** — desktop's own `MarketStorefront.tsx` and migration 127's doc comment both say so explicitly ("All transactions are manual... the player talks to the GM"). User chose to mirror desktop's inspect-only model rather than invent a first transactional buy mechanism; there is no buy button anywhere in this component, matching desktop exactly.

- **Security model, unchanged, just a second consumer**: `usePlayerMarket(campaignId)` (unmodified) is the only data source — catch-up via the `get_player_market_view` RPC, live updates via a `market:<campaignId>`/`market_update` broadcast, both backed by migration 130's `market_player_projection()`. Never subscribes to `postgres_changes` on `market_merchants`, never selects that table directly — verified live: an all-three-tiers merchant (`open` lines always visible regardless of `revealed`, an unrevealed `back_room`/`under_counter` line withheld entirely, a revealed `back_room` line correctly promoted to a normal visible row) rendered exactly the permitted set in the DOM, and captured WebSocket broadcast frames contained none of the still-unrevealed line names — confirmed by direct payload inspection (Playwright `page.on('websocket')` frame capture), not just checking rendered output.
- **Item detail reuses `ItemDetailPopup.tsx` directly** (`src/components/shared/ItemDetailPopup.tsx`), not `MobileItemDetailSheet.tsx` (Prompt 4a) — that component requires owned-item fields (`equipState`, `stowLocation`, `onSetEquipState`, encumbrance ledger) that a storefront ref-table line has no equivalent for, and the desktop Step-0 audit already confirmed `ItemDetailPopup` as the established, dataset-agnostic, inspect-only component (already shared 3 ways, the desktop player storefront's own first caller to omit its icon-override props). Weapon quality badges come from `ItemDetailPopup`'s own `QualityBadge` usage, not a new implementation. A small local `useCatalogueDetails()` hook (public `ref_weapons`/`ref_armor`/`ref_gear` columns only, no merchant secrets) is duplicated from desktop's own file-local hook of the same name, per that file's own precedent comment ("kept local since this is the only other place that needs it") — a third small duplicate of a trivial data fetch, not shared business logic.
- **z-index note**: `.m-market-root` (400) is deliberately below the other full-screen mobile overlays' usual 500 (`.m-notif-root`/`.m-combat-root`) — `ItemDetailPopup` portals separately at the sealed `Z.modal` (410), and needs to paint above this root to be visible at all. Mutually exclusive by construction: `MobileShell.tsx` closes notifications the instant the storefront opens (`onOpenStorefront` closes one, opens the other), so the two never need to out-rank each other.
- **Merchant-closes-mid-browse**: `usePlayerMarket` collapses to `null` the instant the GM sets `is_open_to_players=false` or deactivates the merchant (both broadcast-driven, no polling) — the component renders an explicit "Merchant unavailable" message rather than silently failing or leaving a stale storefront open. No delete path exists on `market_merchants` (insert-once, update-in-place), so that case doesn't need separate handling.
- Live-verified end to end (real campaign, an archived test character per project convention): opened from a seeded `vendor_offer` pending action, all 12 `open`/revealed lines rendered with correct price/rarity, both locked-tier rows rendered with their aggregate difficulty check text, tapping a line opened `ItemDetailPopup` with correct stats/qualities/description for both a gear and a weapon row.

#### Sheet Destination & Vital Adjusters — Prompt 6/7

Last placeholder destination replaced, and the header Wounds/Strain cells (inert since Prompt 1) wired up. Everything is read-only except wounds/strain adjustment, the one write path in this prompt.

- **Wounds/strain write path (Step 0, 3.1.1)**: desktop's `handleVitalAdjust` (`useCharacterData.ts`) has neither a fresh DB read nor a queue — `newValue = Math.max(0, character[field] + delta); setCharacter(...); await supabase.from('characters').update({[field]: newValue}).eq('id', character.id)`. **`MobileVitalAdjustSheet.tsx`** calls this same function (same table/columns/condition/floor, same instant `setCharacter` echo — not reimplemented) but wraps it in a promise-chain queue (`queueRef`) plus a genuine read-fresh correction: each queued step re-`SELECT`s the live column value immediately before writing, then passes `handleVitalAdjust` a *corrected* delta (`delta + (freshValue - localValue)`) so the algebra always lands on `freshValue + delta` regardless of how stale the local `character` prop is at that instant — closing the exact gap Step 0 found, without duplicating or modifying `handleVitalAdjust` itself. Live-verified with a real rapid-tap sequence (5× "+1" fired via raw DOM `dispatchEvent` to bypass Playwright's per-click actionability throttling): DB and header both landed on exactly `+5`, with the correction visibly compensating (`correctingDelta: 2`) on two steps where a `characters`-table realtime reload (`loadCharacter(true)`, fired unconditionally on every own write per `useCharacterData.ts:583` — `markSelf()` only suppresses the toast, not the reload) had raced ahead of the local optimistic state. No corruption, no lost updates, sequential.
- **Threshold behavior (3.1.2)**: no automatic incapacitation or critical-injury trigger exists anywhere in the codebase (confirmed by grep — zero hits for "incapacitat"); desktop's only past-threshold behavior is recoloring the bar and letting the value exceed threshold uncapped. Mobile matches exactly — `.m-vital-reading-value.is-over` recolors, an over-by note appears, nothing else fires automatically.
- No broadcast/notification on adjustment (3.1.3) — a plain table UPDATE, same as desktop, relying on the existing `characters` realtime subscription for other viewers.
- `WoundsStrainOverlay.tsx` (deleted Prompt 1) has zero remaining references anywhere (3.1.4, grep-confirmed) — no equivalent needed beyond `HudStatusStrip.tsx`'s inline ±buttons, which this sheet already exceeds functionally (queue + read-fresh desktop itself lacks).
- **`MobileSheetDestination.tsx`** — Identity (portrait/name/spec-or-career/species), XP (total/available/spent, display-only, spent computed as `xp_total - xp_available`), Characteristics (6-box grid, raw `character` fields), Derived Stats (`effectiveStats.soak/defenseMelee/defenseRanged/woundThreshold/strainThreshold` — consumed, never recomputed — plus Force Rating gated on `is_force_sensitive`), Critical Injuries (`crits`, each with a two-tap "Mark Healed" calling the existing `handleHealCrit` — confirmed player-facing on desktop despite a stale GM-facing code comment elsewhere, Step 0 3.3), Morality/Force Presence (branches on `moralitySystem` exactly as `ForcePanel.tsx` does), Lore (species/obligation/duty/motivation as collapsible `RichText` cards, closed by default so long content never renders as one unbroken scroll).
- **Morality (3.5) — Force Presence is editable, vanilla stays display-only.** Correcting a wrong call in the initial build: Force Presence balance was locked `pointerEvents:'none'` on the assumption it should stay read-only, but the GM confirmed it must be editable on mobile like desktop. **`MobileForcePresenceEditor.tsx`** (new, FIX prompt) replaces that lock — a mobile-local, touch-usable control, NOT a reuse of `ForcePresenceCard`: that component's pips are a fixed 18×18px and its flip popup positions two small text buttons at the pip's exact center via `getBoundingClientRect()` — both well under the 48px floor and, at ~21px pip spacing, colliding with neighbouring pips' popups. `ForcePresenceCard.tsx` itself was read, never modified. The new editor renders 10 real 48×48px pip buttons (measured, Playwright) that inline-expand to 48px-tall Light/Neutral/Dark choice buttons directly below the row (no floating popup, no positioning math). It writes through the exact same desktop mutator, `handleFlipBalancePoint` (`useCharacterData.ts`) — same table (`characters`), same columns (`light_points`/`dark_points`), same `.eq('id', character.id)` condition, same `computeBalancePointFlip` validation — not reimplemented. Desktop's version already does read-fresh internally (`fetchFreshBalancePoints`); the only gap (Step 0, FIX-prompt 3.3: desktop has no queue at all) is closed with the same promise-chain-queue + latest-props-ref pattern as the Prompt 6 vitals adjuster. Live-verified: a real flip (neutral→light) landed in the DB immediately; 5 rapid dispatched taps on the same pip (which stays visually "neutral" until light points fill from the top pip down, matching desktop's own fill order) each correctly added +1, ending at exactly `initial + 5` with zero lost updates. **Vanilla morality was confirmed GM-only** (`handleMoralityChange`'s only caller, `MoralityCard.tsx`'s ± buttons, is gated `isGmMode` — a player never sees them on desktop either) — the mobile vanilla-morality display card is correctly left read-only, unchanged from the original build.
- **Z-index**: no new overlay root added. The vital adjuster reuses the existing `MobileBottomSheet` (`.m-sheet-panel`, z-index 410); the Sheet destination itself is inline page content inside `.m-body-page`, not a portal. Existing mobile roots for reference: `.m-sheet-panel` 410, `.m-combat-root`/`.m-notif-root` 500, `.m-market-root` 400 (Prompt 4b, deliberately below the sealed `ItemDetailPopup` at `Z.modal` 410) — none touched or conflicted with here.
- Contrast/tokens: zero new colors — reuses `HUD_MOBILE.text`/`textDim`/`textFaint`, `var(--state-failure)`, `var(--hud-mobile-accent)` throughout, all already vetted in prior mobile prompts. One documented inline-style exception (the `pointerEvents:'none'` lock described here was later removed by the Force Presence editable-on-mobile fix, above): a live morality-percentage bar width (`Math.min(100, Math.max(0, character.morality_value))%`, same "no CSS-class equivalent for a live percentage" pattern as `.m-ledger-track-fill` from Prompt 4a).

#### Sheet Destination — visual redesign (in-place, no behaviour change)

`MobileSheetDestination.tsx` restyled in place (same file, same props contract plus one addition — see below); no new component files, no data-source or write-path changes. XP display **changed from total/available/spent to available-only**, per this task's own explicit instruction overriding the design mockup it was built from.

- **Identity banner — superseded, see the FIX below.** The original redesign duplicated the shell header (portrait, name, species/career tags) in a second block directly beneath it; that block is gone. The mockup's campaign-name eyebrow and character epithet were, even in the original version, **omitted rather than faked** — no data source exists for either (no campaign-name fetch anywhere in mobile, and `epithet` isn't a real `Character` field — see Prompt 1's own architecture note on this same point) and OUT-OF-SCOPE forbids adding a new data source for a visual-only task.
- **Characteristics** — six pointy-top hexagons (`clip-path: polygon(50% 0%,100% 25%,100% 75%,50% 100%,0% 75%,0% 25%)`), each a `CHAR_COLOR[key]` outer shape behind an inset dark inner fill (the "clipped outer shape over dark inner fill" border effect) plus a 16%-opacity colour wash. `CHAR_COLOR` (`tokens.ts`, the approved raw-hex exception) is threaded into CSS via an inline `--char` custom property per hex — single source of truth stays `tokens.ts`, no hex duplicated into the stylesheet. Mapping: brawn `#E03A1E`, agility `#D4903A`, intellect `#C8AA50`, cunning `#B07828`, willpower `#C82A10`, presence `#E07050`.
  - **Contrast, computed**: value text (`HUD_MOBILE.text`, `#F5EDE3`) against the composited wash — worst case (intellect, brightest of the six) is 8.87:1 at the wash's actual 16% opacity, every other characteristic higher; all clear the 7:1 body floor. The label text is a real, load-bearing exception: the **raw** `CHAR_COLOR` hex used directly as text measured as low as **2.86:1** (willpower) and **3.61:1** (brawn) against the dark surface — both fail even the 4.5:1 secondary floor outright. Fixed by lightening for text use only (`color-mix(in srgb, var(--char) 70%, white 30%)`) — computed worst case (willpower) **4.63:1**, every other characteristic 5.4–9.2:1. Border/glow/wash keep the pure `CHAR_COLOR` hex; only the label text is tinted.
- **Defences** — soak/melee/ranged trio, each cell with a top accent rule; soak's rule uses `--hud-mobile-accent`, the other two `--hud-mobile-border-hi`.
- **Wounds/strain meters** — `.m-vital-meter`, current-over-threshold with a fill bar (`--state-failure` for wounds, `--die-force` for strain) and a `›` chevron affordance. **New wiring, not a new write path**: these buttons call `onOpenVitalAdjust` (a new prop on `MobileSheetDestinationProps`, wired in `MobileShell.tsx` straight to the same `setVitalField` the header Wounds/Strain cells already use) — same `MobileVitalAdjustSheet`, same queue, same read-fresh logic, completely untouched; this only adds a second call site for the existing opener; the original file had no wound/strain current-value display or tap affordance in the Sheet body at all (only the header cells opened the adjuster) — the redesign's own requirement to make these "already-tappable" meters live here required threading that one prop through, nothing deeper.
- **Critical injuries** — left accent rule in `--state-failure`, unchanged two-tap "Mark Healed" logic (byte-identical `CritCard` state machine, only its CSS classes changed).
- **Force Presence** — `MobileForcePresenceEditor.tsx` restyled in place (per Step 0 3.4's own pre-confirmation: its pip markup, queue, and write path are fully separable from its presentation). Added, presentation-only, from the same already-flowing `lightPoints`/`darkPoints` props: a light/dark count readout above the pips, and a split balance bar beneath (`--fp-split` custom property, same "live percentage, no CSS-class equivalent" documented-inline-style pattern used elsewhere). Pips restyled (light: pale + `box-shadow` glow; dark: near-black with a purple rim glow) at their **unchanged 48×48px** size — measured live post-restyle. **Interaction, queue (`queueRef`), and write path (`onFlipBalancePoint` → `handleFlipBalancePoint`) are byte-identical to before** — confirmed by diff and by a live flip (neutral→light) landing in the DB immediately after the restyle. **Contrast finding and fix**: the raw `--hud-accent-purple` token used directly as the "Dark" count/label text and the chooser's "Dark" button label measured only **3.3–3.9:1** on this panel's background — under the 4.5:1 floor. Fixed the same way as the hex labels, with a dedicated `DARK_VIOLET_TEXT` constant (`color-mix(in srgb, var(--hud-accent-purple) 70%, white 30%)`, computed **6.42:1**) used only for text; pip fill/border/aura/balance-marker keep the pure token. The same fix was needed for the identity banner's `Force N` tag (also raw `--hud-accent-purple` as text, same measured range) — same tint applied there too.
- **Lore** — accordions gained a small icon glyph (`.m-lore-icon`) alongside the existing title/summary/chevron; collapsed-by-default behaviour and `RichText` rendering are unchanged (same `CollapsibleSection` open/close state machine).
- **Section headings** — accent dot + wide-tracked uppercase label + a rule fading to the right (`.m-sheet-section-label::before`/`::after`), replacing the old plain label.
- **Motion** — numbers count up on entrance (`CountUp`, a small local subcomponent: the *rendered* value is always the real, final number — reduced motion needs zero special-casing since that's already what a static/no-JS render shows; motion-enabled mode tweens a local display value from 0 up on first mount only), sections stagger in via a `useEntranceRef` helper that drives `transform`/`filter: opacity()` directly through GSAP `onUpdate` (never GSAP's own `opacity` property, per the "never gate visibility via the opacity CSS property" rule), and the wound/strain bars fill from 0 to their real target width the same way. All of it is gated by `usePrefersReducedMotion()` (first mobile component to use it) in each effect's own dependency array, and every entrance effect is a `gsap.from`-shaped tween *toward* markup that was already correct at rest — so turning motion off doesn't require a separate "final state" code path, the static JSX already is the final state.
- **Embers — kept.** A small (10-particle) decorative drift layer (`EmberLayer`), driven by GSAP's own ticker rather than a custom `requestAnimationFrame` loop, renders nothing at all under reduced motion (the component isn't mounted). Measured live at 390×844 with the layer running plus the entrance stagger in flight: **~61fps** over a 1-second `requestAnimationFrame` sampling window (Playwright) — no measurable cost, kept per the task's own default.

#### Sheet Destination — FIX: banner clipping/duplication, hex scale, empty Lore

**Root cause of the clipping (not guessed — found via computed styles):** the redesign's root element reused `.m-abilities` (`display:flex; flex-direction:column; height:100%`, built for the Abilities destination's sticky-search-bar-over-scrolling-list layout) with no `overflow-y` of its own. Sheet's content is far taller than one screen; with no overflow escape valve, the flex algorithm's default `flex-shrink:1` compressed every section proportionally to force the whole page into that 100% height budget, and `.m-sheet-banner`'s own `overflow:hidden` then clipped whatever got crushed below its content size. Confirmed live: `.m-sheet-banner` measured **32px tall with a 72px-tall child inside it** (`getComputedStyle`/`offsetHeight` via Playwright). Fixed by giving the destination its own root, **`.m-sheet-page`** (plain block, no flex/height constraint) — `.m-body-page` (the actual, pre-existing scroll owner, already `overflow-y:auto`) now does the scrolling the same way every other destination already relies on it to. No padding was added to push content clear; the underlying cause (borrowed flex-shrink layout) is what changed.

**Duplication removed**: the banner no longer renders portrait, name, or species/spec-career tags — `MobileHeader.tsx` already shows all of that (`m-avatar`, `m-identity-name`, `m-identity-line` = spec-or-career · species). The banner is now a slim context strip: glow + base rule, plus a Force-rating tag when `is_force_sensitive` (the one thing the header does *not* show). For a non-Force-sensitive character it's just the glow/rule at ~21px tall — intentional, matches the FIX's own instruction to keep the glow/rule "so the section still reads as designed" even with nothing else to show.

**Hex scale**: with `.m-sheet-page` now supplying a single `var(--space-4)` gutter (removed the redundant per-section horizontal padding on the banner/XP row that would have doubled up), measured hex cells are **116×126px** at 390px width — inside the requested ~100–115px band (1px over on width; not re-tuned further, still comfortably ≥48px tap target and readable). Characteristics **and** Defences (and, in practice, the wound/strain meters and the top of Force Presence) are all visible without scrolling at 390×844 — verified live.

**Lore empty-state (D)**: diagnosed against the GM's own character (Bylethia Ford) directly — her DB row has `species_key='DATHOMIRIAN'`, `duty_obligation_configured=true` with both `obligation_type`/`duty_type` set. Live-tested against the real running app: **all three accordions (Species, Obligation, Duty) render correctly** once the page finishes loading. The data path was never broken for her. **Why the earlier "single lore item renders cleanly, verified live" claim didn't catch this**: that check used a different test character (one with only `species_key` set, everything else null) and confirmed the *non-empty* case renders — it never exercised the state where all four lore conditions (`speciesName`, obligation, duty, motivation) are simultaneously false, because no real seeded character happens to have that combination. That's a real gap in the earlier prompt's coverage, not a false claim about what it did test. The **code itself** had a latent, real defect independent of any specific character: if that all-four-empty state is ever reached, the `Lore` heading rendered with nothing beneath it, since each of the four items was independently gated with no shared fallback. Fixed with a `hasAnyLore` flag; when false, the heading is followed by a plain "Nothing recorded." line (reusing `.m-morality-not-configured`) instead of a void. Verified live by temporarily nulling `species_key`/`duty_obligation_configured`/`motivation_type`/`motivation_specific` on an archived test character (Hugo Sass, then restored) — `0` lore cards, "Nothing recorded." rendered.

**Critical Injuries and Force Presence were already correct** (D.5) — Critical Injuries omits its own section heading entirely when `crits.length === 0` (heading and cards are gated together, not independently, so there is no reachable state with the heading and no content); Force Presence's heading is followed by a ternary with no falsy branch (editor, "not configured" message, or the vanilla morality card always renders something) — verified by reading both, no code change needed for either.
- Verified live: sections with no data (no crits, non-force-sensitive character on a `force_presence`-system campaign still correctly shows the Force Presence panel since that's campaign-wide, not per-character; only one Lore accordion when only Species is configured) render with no layout gaps or broken spacing.

#### Map Destination — Prompt 7/7 (player, view-only)

Last of the seven mobile-rebuild prompts. Wires the header Map icon (inert since Prompt 1) to a full-screen, view-only port of the existing player map. This is a port, not a redesign — Step 0 found the desktop `MapCanvas.tsx` fully self-contained (props-driven, own Pixi bootstrap/camera/tokens, container-relative sizing via `ResizeObserver`, no desktop-layout coupling) and already touch-compatible for panning via Pixi v7's Pointer-Events-based EventSystem — so it is **hosted unmodified**, not forked, per the approved Step 0 conclusion.

- **`MobileMapDestination.tsx`** — full-screen portal (`.m-map-root`, z-index 500, same tier as `.m-notif-root`/`.m-combat-root`; the only child that needs to out-rank it, `PlayerTokenTooltip`, is sealed at `Z.tooltip` 9999 and always wins regardless — no conflict either direction). Hosts `MapCanvas` with `isGM={false}`, `currentCharacterId={null}` (never matches any token's `character_id`, so `canDrag` is false for every token — MapCanvas's own computation, not a new prop) and `onTokenMove={() => {}}` — zero write path, grep-confirmed no `insert`/`update`/`upsert`/`rpc`/`delete` anywhere in the file. Data comes through the same hooks desktop uses, unmodified: `useActiveMap(campaignId).visibleMap`, `useMapTokens(visibleMap?.id ?? null, { visibleOnly: true }).tokens` — filtering is server-side (query itself carries `.eq('is_visible', true)`; the realtime handler drops hidden rows before they reach state), confirmed clean at Step 0 and reconfirmed live here: a seeded hidden token's data appeared in neither the rendered DOM nor any of 36 captured realtime broadcast frames during a live Playwright pass.
- **Orientation**: `matchMedia('(orientation: landscape)')`-driven, no Screen Orientation API lock (deliberately, per the prompt's own instruction — unreliable on iOS Safari, fails silently under rotation lock). Portrait shows `.m-map-rotate` (rotate glyph + prompt, no map mounted); landscape renders the map directly; already-landscape-on-entry skips the prompt entirely (verified — no timing gap where a cramped map briefly renders before the check runs, since the initial state read is synchronous `window.innerWidth > window.innerHeight`).
- **Touch**: one-finger pan works with zero added code (Pixi's native Pointer Events, confirmed at Step 0). Pinch-to-zoom is new, added *around* `MapCanvas`, not inside it — a `usePinchZoom` hook translates a 2-finger pinch into synthetic native `WheelEvent`s dispatched at the DOM element MapCanvas already attaches its own `wheel` listener to (found by querying for the `<canvas>` MapCanvas appends and reading `.parentElement` — the only way to reach that element without a forwarded ref, since MapCanvas exposes none). Reuses MapCanvas's own zoom math (clamp, focal-point centring) exactly rather than reimplementing it. Double-tap-to-zoom was evaluated and omitted — desktop has no such gesture (wheel only), per the prompt's own instruction not to invent one.
- **Tap-to-inspect**: desktop's token info card is hover-only as built (`HudSessionTab.tsx` never wires `onTokenClick`, and a non-draggable token's `pointerdown` handler — the only path that could distinguish a tap from a drag — is never attached at all when `canDrag` is false, which is every token on mobile). A raw touch mapping of hover-start/hover-end would only flash the tooltip while a finger is down, per Step 0's own finding — not usable. Built a tap-to-show/auto-dismiss (3.5s) equivalent instead, reusing `onTokenHover`/`onTokenHoverEnd` (existing MapCanvas props) and the desktop `PlayerTokenTooltip` component verbatim, unmodified. Auto-dismiss (rather than tap-elsewhere-to-dismiss) was a deliberate choice: `MapCanvas` renders one `<canvas>` DOM element, so every tap — whether it hit a token or empty map — fires an identical native `click` with no way from outside to tell them apart without touching MapCanvas's own hit-testing. The `tooltipEntity` derivation (PC → live encounter slot → cached adversary → cached vehicle → name-only fallback) is ported from `HudSessionTab.tsx`'s own `useMemo` verbatim — not exported there, so not importable, and re-derived here field-for-field rather than invented, matching desktop exactly (verified live: tapping a token rendered the same name/wounds card desktop would show for the same token).
- **Cleanup**: `MapCanvas`'s own unmount effect (`app.destroy(true, { children: true, texture: true })`) is unchanged and does the real work; verified live via 5 rapid open/close cycles of the mobile map takeover — 0 canvases left in the DOM after each close, and a fresh `WebGL` context was still obtainable afterward (no context-limit exhaustion).
- **FIX — `useIsMobile` is now orientation-aware (short-side, not width).** The architectural gap noted when this prompt shipped — a width-only breakpoint (`max-width: 767px`) meant rotating a wide-enough phone to landscape crossed 768px and swapped the whole page from `MobileShell` to `PlayerHUDDesktop`, unmounting the map before it could ever be reached — is fixed. `useIsMobile.ts` now tests `min(width, height) < breakpoint` instead of `width < breakpoint`, expressed as a single matchMedia query: `(max-width: 767px), (max-height: 767px)` (a comma-separated media list is itself an OR, and `min(a,b) < X ⟺ a < X OR b < X`). Breakpoint value unchanged (768, per the FIX prompt's explicit instruction). One consumer project-wide (`src/app/character/[id]/page.tsx`, confirmed by grep — no other file makes its own width-based mobile/desktop determination); signature/default unchanged, so it needed no edit. SSR-safe as before (`useState(false)` initial, resolved client-side in `useEffect`) — first-render output is unchanged. Live-verified at all required viewports: phone portrait/landscape (390×844, 844×390, 375×667, 667×375) → mobile; desktop (1440×900) and tablet both orientations (820×1180, 1180×820) → desktop; a short wide window (1200×600) → mobile, the explicitly accepted consequence, not special-cased. Rotation verified end-to-end with a DOM-instance marker: the same `MobileShell` root element (not a fresh remount) survives a portrait→landscape resize, and the map becomes reachable through it with no unmount/remount step — no debounce/throttle was needed since `matchMedia`'s own `change` event only fires when the evaluated boolean actually flips, which can't thrash on intermediate resize values the way a raw pixel-tracking listener could, and it fires on orientation change for free (rotation changes width/height, which changes which query matches) without depending on either the `resize` or `orientationchange` event specifically.

#### XP Purchasing — Prompt 2b/7 (talent nodes, skill ranks, Force upgrades)

Enables the three in-play XP spends on mobile — talent tree nodes (including signature ability nodes), skill ranks, Force power upgrades. Immediate writes, no GM approval step. Characteristic purchases and new-specialization purchases are explicitly out of scope (not listed in the prompt; the latter is a separate purchase type, `buySpecialization`/`specPurchaseCost`, `useCharacterData.ts`, reported at Step 0 but not built here).

- **Validation/cost reuse, zero reimplementation**: talent nodes use `buildCharacterTalentTree`/`buildTalentTree` (`src/lib/buildTalentTree.ts`, pure, module-level exported — already imported by `MobileAbilityTree.tsx` since Prompt 2) for prerequisite/row-adjacency gating and cost; Force upgrades use `buildForcePowerTree` (`src/hooks/useForcePowers.ts`, memoized, already the source of `allForcePowers[].treeNodes` that Prompt 2 already consumed) for the same. The actual writes — `purchaseTalent` (module-level exported, `useCharacterData.ts:71`), `handlePurchaseForceAbility`, `handleBuySkill`, `lockInAbility`/`purchaseSigNode` (the latter two via `useCharacterSigAbilities.ts`, re-exported from `useCharacterData()`'s own return object) — are all called through the exact same `useCharacterData()`/`useForcePowers()` hook instances `MobileShell.tsx` already runs; none are duplicated or reimplemented.
- **Step 0 findings, real gaps in the reused desktop functions (not fixed — out of scope, `useCharacterData.ts`/`useCharacterSigAbilities.ts` are shared infra, not mobile files)**: `handleBuySkill` has no internal fresh-XP-read before persisting (unlike `purchaseTalent`/`purchaseForceAbility`, which do); `lockInAbility`/`purchaseNode` (signature abilities) have **no XP-sufficiency guard at all** — they will write a negative balance if called with insufficient XP. Mobile compensates entirely at its own layer: **`MobilePurchaseConfirmSheet.tsx`** re-`SELECT`s `characters.xp_available` fresh immediately before calling any of the five mutators, for every purchase type uniformly — this is the only fresh check that exists for skill/sig-ability purchases and a harmless, defensive second check for talent/Force purchases (which already fresh-read internally). None of the five write paths are transactional (`Promise.all` of separate insert/update/insert calls, confirmed at Step 0) — a failure after the affordability check passes but before/during the real write is reported to the player as "Purchase may not have completed — check with your GM" rather than assumed to have silently succeeded or silently failed.
- **`MobilePurchaseConfirmSheet.tsx`** (new) — the single confirmation step every purchase type routes through. A promise-chain queue (`queueRef`) plus a synchronous `busy` guard means a double-tap on Confirm can only ever fire the write once — live-verified: three rapid dispatched clicks on Confirm produced exactly one `xp_transactions` row and exactly one XP deduction. Its own Confirm control sits in the sheet body (near the top), not the footer — the footer holds only Cancel, so the natural thumb position right after the FIRST tap (on a type's own "Purchase" button, which sat near the bottom of the previous sheet) lands on the safe dismiss action, not a second confirming tap.
- **Talent/signature-ability nodes**: `MobileTalentDetailSheet.tsx`'s Purchase button (disabled with a TODO since Prompt 2) is live. `MobileAbilityTree.tsx`'s `handleNodeTap` now attaches a `purchaseTarget` (talent row/col/specKey, or sig-ability key/specSlot/node) to the tapped node's detail data; `MobileAbilitiesDestination.tsx` computes live `canBuy`/`disabledReason` from it every render (never baked in at tap time, so affordability always reflects current XP) and builds the actual `PendingPurchase.execute()` closure calling straight through to `handlePurchaseTalent`/`lockInAbility`/`purchaseSigNode`. **Signature ability base-node ("lock in") wiring is implemented and correct but has no reachable entry point in the current mobile UI**: `buildTreeTabs` only ever produces a sig tab for an ability already present in `lockedSigAbilities`, so a sig tree is only ever viewable (and its row-0 base node only ever rendered) after it's already locked in — discovering/browsing a not-yet-locked-in signature ability has no mobile surface at all yet, and wasn't in this prompt's Parts A/B/C. Purchasing UPGRADE nodes (row 1+) within an already-locked-in sig tree works today, live-verified via the general talent-node flow above (same tree UI, same confirm sheet).
- **Skill ranks**: reached from a new `+` button in the Skills search row (48×48px, `stopPropagation`'d so it doesn't hijack the row's existing tap-to-open-check-panel behaviour from Prompt 3b) opening **`MobileSkillDetailSheet.tsx`** (new) — current rank, next rank, cost (`nextRank*5 + (isCareer?0:5)`, the exact formula `handleBuySkill` enforces internally, duplicated here for **display only**, never as the purchase decision), current dice pool. Live-verified: purchasing Astrogation rank 0→1 updated the row's dice-pool display and rank text in place with no remount, and matched the DB exactly (`character_skills.rank`, `characters.xp_available`, one `xp_transactions` row).
- **Force power upgrades**: same `+`-button pattern on Force search rows opens **`MobileForceUpgradeSheet.tsx`** (new) — lists `power.treeNodes` (from `allForcePowers`, already computed) with cost/state per node, each with its own Purchase control gated on `canPurchase` (prerequisite), affordability, and Force Rating ≥ 1 (the same guard `handlePurchaseForceAbility` enforces internally) — each disabled state states its specific reason (prerequisite / Force Rating / XP).
- **Nested-button DOM bug found and fixed during verification**: the search-row's own `<button className="m-result-row">` broke the moment a second real `<button>` (the new buy-icon) was nested inside it — Chromium's HTML parser implicitly closes an open `<button>` when it encounters another one, silently restructuring the DOM out from under React's own tree (confirmed live: Playwright reported the inner button as permanently "not visible" despite a normal computed style/bounding box). Fixed by changing `.m-result-row` to a `<div role="button" tabIndex={0}>` with its own `onClick`/`onKeyDown` (Enter/Space) handling — not a mobile-shell-wide pattern change, just this one row.
- Contrast/tokens: zero new colors — reuses `HUD_MOBILE.accent`/`text`/`text-dim`, `var(--state-failure)` throughout, all already vetted. No inline styles beyond what's already established elsewhere in the mobile tree.

/gm/page.tsx                    (~3,000+ lines — GOD COMPONENT)
  ├── GmLeftRail                (52px fixed left rail; buttons for map/tools/party/market; utilities for dice/screen/library — `GmPanelId` = 'map'|'tools'|'party'|'library'|'market')
  ├── CombatPanel               (~3,647 lines — GOD COMPONENT)
  ├── GroupSheet                (~1,852 lines)
  ├── ItemDatabaseTab
  ├── DestinyPoolDisplay
  ├── GmMapView                 (token map + encounter controls)
  └── NPC/Vehicle editors

/create/page.tsx
  └── Character creator wizard (inline — no sub-component shell)

/gm/mapforge/page.tsx
  └── Map Forge (inline — generate → edit flow)
```

### Tier 2 — Feature Panels
Each panel is a self-contained component accepting pre-computed display data from the parent:
- `InventoryCardPanel` — master/detail inventory card system (left manifest rail + right item detail) for weapons, armor, gear with equip/stow/discard controls; composed of `item-thumb-grid` (now renders manifest ROWS, not a thumbnail grid — see Prompt 3a below), `item-detail-hero`, `item-quality-list`, `item-detail-panel`, and the shared `stow-location-modal` (exports `StowLocationModal` + `StowPill`); wired via `HudInventoryTab` (replaced the former single-file `InventoryPanel`). Split proportions: `grid-template-columns: minmax(430px, 1.15fr) minmax(360px, 0.85fr)` on the row below the Ledger hero, per `inventory-panel-spec-v3.html` (Prompt 3a). Top of panel is the **Ledger hero** (`LedgerHero`, defined in-file in `inventory-card-panel.tsx`, Prompt 2 of The Archive) — shows `load / threshold` + "room for N more"/"N over", a scale with threshold and cliff tick marks, three expandable bands (Base, Worn Capacity, Load) listing `EncumbranceStats.capacitySources`/`loadSources` with suppressed contributors struck through and their reason composed from `EncumbranceSuppressReason`, an ambient left-border + background tint plus RAW consequence copy when over threshold. Colours: `var(--green)` (capacity gain), `var(--state-threat)` (over-threshold warn), `var(--die-force)` (normal load fill) — no new tokens.
  - **Hover-preview rewire (The Archive Prompt 3, Task 1)**: `hoverId`/`simStats` state moved OUT of `LedgerHero` up into `InventoryCardPanel` (their nearest common parent) — no new context/provider, plain lifted state was the smallest change. `ItemThumbGrid` gained `onHoverStart`/`onHoverEnd` props on each row, wired to the same `handleHover` the Ledger's own band rows already used. A manifest-row hover now previews "what if I equip/stow THAT item" (the primary use case per `inventory-panel-spec-v3.html`); a band-row hover still previews "what if I dropped this contributor" — both call the identical `onSimulate(itemId, itemType)`. `simulateEncumbrance` (`PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx`) changed from always-force-stow to toggling each item to the OPPOSITE of its current equipped-ness (equipped → stowed, anything else → equipped), matching the mockup's toggle rule; still calls only `computeEncumbranceStats`, no parallel maths.
  - **Prompt 5 — Effect block heading removed, deliberately.** `item-detail-panel.tsx`'s `EffectBlock` no longer has an "Effect" label above `effect_text`. Prompt 4's audit (sampled ≥20 rows per table, `ref_weapons`/`ref_armor`/`ref_gear`) found the migration-124 `[P]`-boundary split misroutes flavour text into `effect_text` at roughly 55% on weapons (35% outright flavour), 30% on armour, 25% on gear, with no reliable textual signal (dice shortcodes, rule verbs, character-address framing) reliable enough to auto-correct it. A decision was made NOT to re-split the data and NOT to do manual review — instead the panel stops asserting a mechanical/flavour distinction the data can't support. The text still renders (via `RichText`, still null-safe — no block when `effect_text` is null), just unlabelled; the left gold border is kept as a structural cue only. Special Qualities (the `ItemQualityList`/qualities-jsonb path) is unaffected and remains the correct source for mechanical content like Stun Setting, Pierce, Accurate. **Do not restore an "Effect" (or similarly authoritative) heading here** without first either re-splitting the data with a validated method or otherwise addressing the underlying misroute rate — see Prompt 4's audit for the numbers.
  - **Prompt 8 — reading size correction.** Prompt 7's `FS.hero` (64->96px) overshot the mockup's `clamp(30px, 2.4vw, 42px)` target by roughly 3x, dominating the panel and leaving dead space above the bar. Corrected to `FS.h2` (22->40px, per its own doc comment in `tokens.ts`) — the closest existing step to the mockup's literal clamp, reused rather than adding a new one; resolves to 45px at a 1440px viewport (Playwright-measured), a small overshoot of the mockup's 42px ceiling from reusing an existing token instead of hand-tuning a new clamp to match exactly. Dead space above the bar is gone as a direct consequence of the smaller font's shorter line box — no separate spacing/margin change was needed.
