Everything else from Prompt 7 (over-threshold colouring, italic+dashed-underline simulated-reading treatment, numerator+denominator simulation reactivity, ghost treatment/movement/reduced-motion gating, `--hud-accent-purple` cliff colour) is untouched; scale geometry (fill width, both mark positions) verified identical to Prompt 7's measured figures on both an under- and over-threshold character.
  - **Prompt 7 — reading emphasis, ghost legibility, cliff colour.** (1) **Big reading (Task 1/1b)**: the load figure is now `FS.hero` (`var(--text-hero)`, the project's own reserved "big number" token — see its own doc comment in `tokens.ts`, reused rather than adding a size step) — **superseded by Prompt 8, see above, which corrected this to `FS.h2`** — threshold follows at `FS.label` as `/ N`, "room for N more" unchanged size. Position untouched, only typographic weight changed. The whole reading (numerator AND denominator AND room) now reads from `display` (`= simStats ?? stats`) instead of always `stats` — hovering a state button previews the SIMULATED resulting load/threshold, correctly recomputing the denominator too (equipping/stowing a threshold-granting item changes `wornCapacity`; verified live against Bexar's "BX Droid Modular Storage Unit" — hovering Stowed on it changes the reading from `13/11` to `13/8`, load unchanged, threshold correctly dropping by the lost +3 bonus). Simulated reading is visually distinguished with italic + a dashed underline — no explanatory copy added. Same `display` the ghost geometry already reads; no second computation. (2) **Ghost legibility (Task 2)**: rebuilt as two nested wrappers — `ghostRef` (position/width/show-hide crossfade, unchanged from Prompt 6) wrapping a new `ghostPulseRef` inner div carrying the stripe pattern. The stripe's "gap" colour changed from `transparent` (which let the gold fill show through underneath for a shrink, reading as texture) to `var(--hud-bg)` (opaque dark), so a shrink ghost now visibly CUTS OUT its segment from the fill instead of tinting it; a solid 2px direction-coloured edge (`--green` right edge for shrink, `--state-threat` left edge for grow) marks the boundary. Movement: `ghostPulseRef` gets a continuous `backgroundPositionX` stripe-travel tween plus a `sine.inOut` yoyo opacity pulse (via `filter: opacity(...)`, tweened independently of `ghostRef`'s own crossfade so the two never fight over the same property), both `repeat: -1`, both killed and reset to static under `usePrefersReducedMotion()` (Playwright-verified: identical computed `backgroundPositionX`/`filter` across a 600ms window under `reducedMotion:'reduce'`, actively changing under normal motion in the same test — positive control confirmed the harness itself, not just the gate). (3) **Cliff mark colour (Task 2b)**: `var(--hud-accent-purple)` (`#9060D0`, already used elsewhere in these rebuilt panel files, same resolved value in all three themes since only `:root`/Ember and `[data-theme="kyber"]` define it and neither `[data-theme="gm-imperial"]` overrides it) replaces `--state-threat` — Prompt 6 reasoned correctly about the rejected `--led-cliff` token but landed both marks on the same colour, making threshold and cliff indistinguishable without reading labels.
  - **Prompt 6 — bar treatment, hover trigger moved, Load band filtered.** `inventory-panel-spec-v3.html` reached this session for the first time this prompt (it never arrived during Prompt 2, hence the Ledger being built from prose then) — reviewed properly and matched section-by-section. **Bar treatment** (Task 1, visual only — Prompt 4's `scaleMax = Math.max(cliff+2, load+1, 1)` arithmetic and denominator untouched, Playwright-verified identical fill/mark percentages before and after on both an over- and under-threshold character): track grew from a 6px hairline to a 22px (`1.375rem`) bordered, inset-dark track; fill is now a gradient (gold ramp normally, gold→`--state-threat` when over) instead of a flat `--die-force`/`--state-threat` fill; both marks now extend above and below the track with a label underneath (`THRESHOLD` / `MANEUVER CLIFF`) and glow (`filter: drop-shadow(...)`) when the currently-simulated state would cross them. No new tokens — the mockup's proposed `--led-capacity`/`--led-cliff` sealed-namespace additions (rejected in Prompt 2) stay rejected; ghost/cliff colours reuse `--green`/`--state-threat`, already the panel's own "gain"/"danger" tokens. **Hover trigger moved** (Task 2, replacement not addition): the manifest-row hover trigger is gone entirely — `ItemThumbGrid`/`ManifestRow` no longer take `onHoverStart`/`onHoverEnd`/`onHoverItem` (grep-verified zero remaining references anywhere in `src/`, the only other hits in the codebase are unrelated combat/map-token hover code in `EncounterDeck.tsx`/`MapCanvas.tsx`). The trigger is now the state segmented control in `ItemDetailPanel`'s footer (`EquipButtons`) — hovering "Stowed"/"Carried"/"Equipped" simulates moving the *selected* item to that state, so the button being hovered states the simulation with no explanatory copy needed. `simulateEncumbrance` (`PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx`) and `onSimulate`/`handleHover`'s signatures grew a `targetState: EquipState` parameter — it now moves the item directly to the given state rather than inferring a toggle direction. The Ledger's own band-row hover (`SourceRow`) is unchanged in behaviour, just passes no explicit target so `handleHover`'s default (`'stowed'`) applies — "what if I dropped this contributor" stays unambiguous and intact. Ghost rendering was corrected alongside the move: the solid fill now always reflects the TRUE (non-simulated) load, and the ghost is a separate band spanning only the delta between true and simulated position — `grow` (simulated costs more) renders forward in a `--state-threat` hazard stripe, `shrink` (simulated costs less) renders back in a `--green` stripe; hovering a state the item is already in produces a `null` ghost direction naturally (simulated stats equal true stats), no special-casing needed. **Load band filter/sort** (Task 3): `LedgerHero`'s Load band now reads `display.loadSources.filter(s => s.value > 0).sort((a,b) => b.value - a.value)` at render time — `value` is `perItem`'s already-reduced effective cost (a worn suit's `-3` baked in), so no recomputation. An empty result (nothing carried) renders "Nothing carried." instead of an empty/absent container. Worn Capacity's band is untouched, including suppressed/zero-grant entries.
  - **Prompt 4 — scale headroom fix.** `LedgerHero`'s scale fill and both tick marks already shared one denominator (`scaleMax`) — there was never a fill/marks mismatch. The bug was `scaleMax`'s formula: `Math.max(stats.cliff * 1.15, display.load * 1.05, 1)` scaled headroom as a PERCENTAGE of cliff, which over-allocates space past the threshold once cliff exceeds it by even a modest Brawn (e.g. load 12/threshold 13/cliff 16 left ~30% of the bar as dead space past the threshold, making a numerically tight 1-point gap read as "plenty of room"). Replaced with `inventory-panel-spec-v3.html`'s own reference formula, `Math.max(stats.cliff + 2, display.load + 1, 1)` — small fixed headroom instead of a cliff-scaled percentage, so the threshold zone commands most of the bar regardless of how far out cliff sits. Verified via Playwright reading the actual DOM `style.width`/`style.left` of the fill and both marks (not visual estimation) against two live characters: Bexar (over-threshold, load 13/threshold 11/cliff 14) → fill 81.25%, threshold mark 68.75%, cliff mark 87.5%, matching `Math.max(16,14,1)=16` exactly; Guy Rando (under-threshold, load 12/threshold 13/cliff 16) → fill 66.67%, threshold mark 72.22%, cliff mark 88.89%, matching `Math.max(18,13,1)=18` exactly. No value in `derivedStats.ts` changed — this is a rendering-only fix.
  - **Market Prompt 1 — `readOnly` prop.** `ItemDetailPanelProps.readOnly?: boolean` (default `false`) added for the planned Market "inspect before you buy" view — deliberately a modification to this LIVE Inventory component, not a parallel component, so there is exactly one item detail view in the app. When `true`: the footer (equip/stow/discard) does not render; the Encumbrance tab is dropped from the tab list and its default active tab becomes `mods` instead of `enc`; `onSetWeaponState`/`onSetArmorState`/`onSetGearState` became optional (still called by the footer, which simply doesn't render under `readOnly`). Everything else (hero, identity, stat chips, effect, qualities, lore) is unchanged. Default `false` leaves every existing Inventory call site byte-identical — confirmed via `tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` clean and no other call site touched. **Not yet wired to Market** (no rail entry/panel exists yet — Prompt 2/3): a ref-catalogue row (no owning character instance) cannot satisfy `SelectedItem`'s `WpnDisplay`/`ArmDisplay`/`GearRow` shape as-is — `IdentityBlock` requires `state: EquipState` and `condition: ItemCondition`, which a ref row has no natural value for. `readOnly` alone hides the UI that would need those values' real semantics, but an adapter synthesizing placeholder `id`/`equipState`/`condition`/`stowLocation` from a ref row is still needed before this panel can render Market stock; not built in this prompt.
  - **Prompt 3a — layout repair round.** Prompt 3's own self-audit missed that Task 2 (manifest rows) and Task 3 (split proportions) from its own instructions were never actually built — confirmed by the user this was a scoping split on their end, not an error, but the gap was real and is fixed here. (1) **Scroll chain root cause**: `item-detail-panel.tsx`'s Prompt 3 rebuild grew the hero (80px→230px) and added a new identity block + effect block as fixed, unscrollable content above a too-small nested scroll region (`minHeight:'6rem'` around tab content only); once that fixed stack plus the tab bar plus the footer exceeded the panel's available height, the footer/tabs were silently clipped by the root's own `overflow:hidden` — no scrollbar appeared anywhere. Fixed by folding hero-through-tab-content into ONE `flex:1, overflowY:'auto', minHeight:0` region, with the state-control/Discard footer as a `flexShrink:0` sibling outside it — restores the pre-Prompt-3 single-scroll guarantee while still pinning the footer. (2) **Manifest rows**: `item-thumb-grid.tsx` rewritten in place — `ItemThumbGrid` now renders `ManifestRow`s (64×40 `ItemReadoutPlate` `size="row"`, unchanged; name on one un-truncated line; sub-line of equip-state/category/condition; an effective-encumbrance badge reading straight from `EncumbranceStats.perItem[id]` — gain in `var(--green)`, cost in `var(--die-force)`, a suppressed bonus as `+0` with its reason, no parallel maths) instead of the old 2-column thumbnail grid. `item-thumb.tsx` (`ItemThumb`) **deleted** — fully replaced, zero remaining consumers (grep-verified). (3) **Split proportions**: the manifest/detail row is now `grid-template-columns: minmax(430px, 1.15fr) minmax(360px, 0.85fr)`, replacing the old fixed `width:'9.25rem'` rail that caused name truncation. (4) **Ledger hero**: confirmed NOT displaced by the Prompt 3 rebuild — same unconditional position at the top of `InventoryCardPanel`, code path untouched; it was simply off-screen/unclear in the original bug report, not broken.
- **Item detail panel rebuild (The Archive Prompt 3, Task 2)** — `item-detail-panel.tsx` rebuilt in place per `item-detail-panel-v3.html`: hero art zone (`ItemDetailHero`, now art-only — name/type/status pills moved out into a separate identity block below it, matching the mockup's `.hero`/`.ident` split) → identity block (name, `Pill` row: equip-state/type/condition) → stat chips (real ref columns only — weapons DMG/CRIT/RANGE/SKILL/ENC/HP + qualities, armor SOAK/DEF/ENC/HP, gear QTY/ENC) → Effect block (`effect_text` via `RichText`, renders nothing — no placeholder — when null; verified against `SHOCKGL`, a real weapon whose `[P]` content is entirely a "Models Include" line so `effect_text` is `NULL`) → three tabs (Encumbrance/Mods/Lore, replacing the old single scrollable body). **Encumbrance tab** reads `EncumbranceStats.perItem[item.id]` (Task 1 of Prompt 2's function) — base encumbrance, worn reduction, threshold granted, a reserved Mods row, a suppressed-reason warning line when `perItem.reason` is set, and a net-effect row; this is the sole home for an item's ledger contribution — the `.contrib` box sketched in `inventory-panel-spec-v3.html`'s workspace pane was NOT also built, per the prompt's explicit instruction. **Mods tab** renders hard-point pips from `hard_points` (weapons/armor only — gear has no such column, confirmed against the schema, never fabricated) or an empty reserved-state string when 0; the Mods *system* (attachments) is not built. **Lore tab** renders `lore_text` via `RichText`. State segmented control + Discard/Remove pinned in a footer at the bottom, replacing the old inline `EquipButtons`/`DiscardFooter` flow. Descriptions render through `RichText` now, not `stripBBCode` — zero remaining `stripBBCode` references in the file (grep-verified). `item-condition-track.tsx` **deleted** — its condition display is now the identity block's condition `Pill`, and it had zero other consumers (grep-verified before deletion). `ItemReadoutPlate` gained a third `size: 'hero'` variant (`row`/`detail` behavior byte-identical, unchanged) that restores the REF designation (top-left) and category tags (bottom-right) the `'detail'` size's own doc comment said were dropped for colliding with artwork at 48-58px — the hero box is 200px+, large enough for both to render without collision; `refKey`/`categories` (previously declared but never destructured/used) are now read. `WpnDisplay`/`ArmDisplay`/`GearRow` (`types.ts`) and `RefWeapon`/`RefArmor`/`RefGear` gained `effectText`/`loreText` (camelCase display) and `effect_text`/`lore_text` (ref column) fields respectively, threaded through `useCharacterData.ts`'s `hudWeapons`/`hudArmor`/`hudGear` transforms — the first read path for these Prompt-1 columns anywhere in the app.
- `QuartermasterModal` (`src/components/QuartermasterModal.tsx`) — Player-facing portal modal for buying and selling equipment with the campaign QM. Two tabs: **Buy** (browse stocked items, filter/search, purchase deducts credits + decrements stock) and **Sell** (inventory list, sell at `sell_pct`% of market value via soft-drop pattern). Loads active character via `character_sessions` → `characters` join. Uses `useQuartermaster` hook for all data and operations.
- `SkillsPanel` — skill list with dice pool popover
- `TalentsPanel` (`src/components/wireframe/TalentsPanel.tsx`, Prompt B2) — now renders the ENTIRE desktop Talents full-panel (previously just a "quick reference" grid beneath a separate spec/XP bar + per-spec footer owned by `HudTalentsTab`). Layout: a 360px hero card (career name at display scale, one row per owned specialization with its own segmented progress bar and a `?spec=<key>` deep-link to the talents route, a `BuySpecButton` for a new spec, and the single "Open Holocron — Talent Trees" gateway button, combat-locked with a tooltip) + a systems card (relocated-unchanged passive-bonus summary, a proportional activation-distribution bar that doubles as a filter, filter chips with zero-count types omitted, and search — search now COMPOSES with the active filter instead of bypassing it, a deliberate behaviour change) + an owned-talents grid + a separate species-abilities section below it. Talent cards use the same notched-plaque/`gridColumn:'1 / -1'`-drawer idiom as `ForcePanel`'s power cards (one shared `expandedId` state, only one drawer open at a time across BOTH the talents and species sections). `HudTalentsTab.tsx` is now a pure data-passing wrapper with no UI of its own — same role `useTalentSurfaceData` plays for the talents route. Species abilities are separated OUT of the talents grid using `HudTalent.isSpeciesGranted` (set by `useCharacterData`'s `hudTalents` merge, untouched by this prompt) and rendered from `speciesAbilities` directly rather than the merge — more complete than the merge itself, since `skill_rank`-type abilities never reach `hudTalents` at all. Source specialization name(s) per talent (a talent owned via two specs lists both, never picks one) come from the raw `talents: CharacterTalent[]` array, since the merged `hudTalents` collapses same-key rows and drops `specialization_key`. `player-hud/TalentsPanel.tsx` is a separate, dead file — only its `HudTalent` type export is live (imported by both `HudTalentsTab.tsx` and this file); its own component is unused. Activation colours (Passive/Incidental/Maneuver/Action/OOT) are sealed fixed identity constants (`TALENT_ACTIVATION_COLOR`/`TALENT_ACTIVATION_META`, `tokens.ts`, same convention as `DICE_COLOR`/`DICE_META`) — theme-independent by design, distinct from the pre-existing theme-tokenized `ACTIVATION_COLOR`/`ACTIVATION_TOKEN` (`tokens.ts`) that `TalentTree.tsx` (the talents route) and `TalentsCard.tsx` (`CharacterHud.tsx`) still use unchanged. The wireframe gallery page (`src/app/wireframe/page.tsx`) still renders `<TalentsPanel />` with zero props — every prop on this component is optional with a safe empty default for that reason.
- `HudForceTab` — force power tree grid (moved to full panel in `HudLeftRail` as `force-panel`)
- `CriticalInjuriesPanel` — injury tracker
- `CombatPanel` — encounter initiative, token management, dice rolling
- `GroupSheet` — group asset management
- `GmMapView` — interactive token map with read-only stat-block hover tooltips and health bars (hover-only; no click-lock)

### Player HUD Sub-components (`src/components/player-hud/`)
- `HudTopBar` — grid row 1; renders the HOLOCRON logo, a portrait chip (avatar thumbnail), character name, and campaign/XP meta; accent colors use CSS vars (`--hud-accent-*`) rather than rgba literals
- `HudStatusStrip` — grid row 2, `gridColumn: 1 / -1`; full-width strip with Wounds ±, Strain ±, compact `EncumbranceBar`, `CriticalInjuryPips`; combat/force check buttons moved to `HudLeftRail`
- `HudLeftRail` — grid row 3 left (64px fixed); quick action buttons (⌖ Combat, ≋ Force [force-sensitive only], ⬠ Skill) open narrow drawers inside the map area; nav buttons (⚙ Skills, ★ Talents, ✦ Force Panel [force-sensitive only], ▣ Inventory, ✦ Lore, Group [faction image]) open full-width slide-in panels; utility buttons (⬡ Dice, ⊗ Adversaries [red]) below divider; Force and Group buttons use faction images (jedi.webp, rebel.png) with per-theme CSS filters. Bottom-most: a Hand tuck/raise toggle (`handTucked`/`onToggleHand` props, both optional — omitted together to hide the entry entirely, which `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` does for `isGmMode`, matching `HandOverlay`'s own player-only gate). Symbol flips `⌄`/`⌃` with state.
- `HudFullPanel` — generic slide-in panel wrapper; `position: absolute; width: 82%` inside the centre column div; used for six navigation panels (skills, talents, force-panel, inventory, lore, group); contains a header (symbol + title + close button) and a scrollable body. Optional `iconSrc?: string` prop renders an `<img>` in place of the header's `symbol` span (used by the force-panel call site for the mask-tinted jedi.webp emblem); falls back byte-identically to the old `symbol` span when omitted, which every other call site still does.
- `HudRightColumn` — grid row 3 right panel; contains full-height `RollFeedPanel` (Approach A feed layout with Design B cards for top-2 rolls, compact history rows, initiative notifications, system entries); action buttons moved to `HudStatusStrip`
- `HudLoreTab` — lore tab content; `CharacterAvatar` at top with portrait upload/delete support; accepts `onPortraitUpload` and `onPortraitDelete` props; portrait was moved here from `HudLeftColumn`
- `ForcePanel` / `HudForceTab` — restructured from the original two-tab (Powers/Committed) layout into a single-view "quick-glance hub": `ForceRatingHero` (hero-scale `FS.hero` numeral + ambient glow, dark-side pulse variant), a Presence swap point (below), a Force Powers grid of header-only `ForcePowerCard`s (notched clip-path, ellipsis-truncated name, no inline expand — clicking one opens a full-width `PowerDrawer` as a CSS Grid sibling (`gridColumn: '1 / -1'`), not nested inside the card), and a `CommittedDiceSection` (continuously-indexed sockets via `data-socket-index`, live "N of N Force dice available" readout from `getAvailableForceRating`, `forceUtils.ts`). `JediEmblem` renders the panel's jedi.webp icon via a `mask-image` tint to an exact token colour (distinct from `.hud-fi-jedi`'s filter-based tinting used elsewhere) — `HudFullPanel` gained an `iconSrc?` prop for this, falling back to its old `symbol` span when absent everywhere else.
  - **Presence swap point** (`ForcePanel.tsx`, the former "FUTURE SWAP POINT" comment): branches on `moralitySystem` prop — `'vanilla'`/undefined → `PresenceCard` (Morality value + Conflict, byte-identical to before), `'force_presence'` → `ForcePresenceCard` (`src/components/player-hud/ForcePresenceCard.tsx`, Prompt B), a fetch error (`moralitySystemError`) → a standalone error card, never a silent fallback to either system. The branch lives at this one mount point, not inside `PresenceCard`'s body.
  - `ForcePresenceCard` — player-facing Force Presence Balance Point track. 10 pips (dark fills from the left pole inward, light from the right, neutral in the middle — derived purely from `lightPoints`/`darkPoints` props, never stored per-pip), pole emblems via the same `mask-image` tint technique as `DestinyPoolDisplay.tsx`'s `DestinyIcon` (dark pole: `var(--hud-accent-purple)`, the existing Destiny/DestinyGMFlash violet — deliberately distinct from `ForcePanel.tsx`'s own `DARK_PURPLE`/`var(--state-activated)`, which is vanilla Morality's Fallen glow). Clicking a pip opens a chooser (portaled to `document.body`, matching `ForceDescriptionPanel`/`PurchaseCeremony`'s convention — `panelBase`'s `backdropFilter` makes every card its own containing block for `position:fixed` descendants) offering valid transitions (neutral↔light, neutral↔dark, light→dark direct; dark→light is NOT valid, a Dark pip offers Neutral only) — chosen via `useCharacterData.ts`'s `handleFlipBalancePoint` (fresh-fetch-then-write, same pattern as `fetchFreshCommitState`/`purchaseTalent`, no capacity guard beyond the client-side `light+dark<=10` check backed by the DB CHECK). GSAP throughout (ambient pip breathe/absorb + a sparse mote-drift layer, paused via `useHudPanelContext().isOpen` and killed/`clearProps`'d under `usePrefersReducedMotion`; a threshold-crossing ceremony — violet/cyan wash from the relevant pole — fires only on the transition edge, including for GM-driven writes arriving via the existing `characters` realtime subscription, not just local flips). One IMPLEMENTATION NOTE: the chooser's positioning anchor (`position:fixed`+`zIndex`) must never be the SAME element GSAP animates opacity/scale on — doing so was observed live to strip the anchor's `position`/`z-index` entirely after the entrance tween ran, dropping every option behind the dismiss-scrim. Fixed by GSAP only ever touching a plain inner wrapper nested inside the (untouched) positioned anchor. Exposes a single canonical `axisFor(lightPoints, darkPoints)` (clamped `[-1,1]`) consumed by `accentColorFor`, the mote-drift effect, and `PresenceSmoke` — never recomputed per-consumer. Dark pips carry a permanent hairline `color-mix(in srgb, HUD.textFaint 30%, transparent)` rim (Prompt B2 contrast fix) independent of the violet glow's current animated intensity, and the card's own background is `HUD.surfaceLo` plus a soft `HUD.surfaceHi` radial lift centred on the pip row (one tone lighter than `panelBase`'s default `HUD.panel`, so void-black Dark pips read at rest without relying on the smoke layer).
  - `PresenceSmoke` (`src/components/player-hud/PresenceSmoke.tsx`, Prompt B2) — ambient Three.js alignment-smoke layer mounted as `ForcePresenceCard`'s rearmost child, behind the mote layer/pips/tally. One `OrthographicCamera` + one 2-triangle `PlaneGeometry` + a single `ShaderMaterial` (3-octave value-noise fbm, no texture asset, no particle system) — the only Three.js usage anywhere in this codebase (`three` was a declared but previously-unused dependency; no prior house convention existed to match, so this establishes one: `ResizeObserver`-driven `renderer.setSize`, full `dispose()` of geometry/material/renderer on unmount, mirroring `MapCanvas.tsx`'s Pixi `app.destroy(true, {...})` disposal shape with Three's own API). Colour rides `ForcePresenceCard`'s shared `axis` value (off-white neutral → KOTOR red/blue, continuous `THREE.Color.lerp`) — a **deliberate KOTOR homage exception** to the app's violet dark-side token, called out in an inline comment at the colour constants; nothing else in the Force Presence feature's violet identity was touched. Colour/density are driven by GSAP tweens on plain numeric targets (not a second animation mechanism), synced to the same 300ms `--ease-smooth` window `ForcePresenceCard`'s border-color/state-word CSS transitions already use. Surges (denser + faster, rushing to full saturation) hook `ForcePresenceCard`'s existing `ceremony` state verbatim — passed as a `surge` prop, never a second edge-detector. Lifecycle: mount/unmount IS the pause mechanism (conditionally rendered only when `isOpen && !prefersReducedMotion`, exactly mirroring the mote layer's own `if (prefersReducedMotion || !isOpen) return` early-out) — closing the Force tab fully disposes the renderer rather than merely hiding a still-rendering canvas. Reduced motion renders no Three canvas at all; `ForcePresenceCard` swaps in a static CSS radial-gradient wash instead, reusing the same three colour constants (`KOTOR_RED_CSS`/`KOTOR_BLUE_CSS`/`NEUTRAL_SMOKE_CSS`, exported alongside the Three-side hex versions — one source of truth, not a duplicate literal set). Pixel ratio capped at a flat 1 (not device-scaled) after live frame-time measurement showed the ambient layer set (smoke + motes + pip glow) costing a measured ~8ms/frame in headless Chromium versus a reduced-motion baseline — attributed to headless software WebGL (SwiftShader) rather than shader cost given the shader's trivial complexity, but capped conservatively regardless since the softer, non-HiDPI result suits diffuse smoke anyway.
  - **Commit/Release write path** (first code path that *creates* a `ForceCommitment`, not just cancels one): `PowerDrawer` renders a `CommitControl` (stepper + "Commit" ghost button) on any owned upgrade row where `isForceCommitCapableForAbility(key, description)` is true (`forceUtils.ts` — regex-detects `commit` + `[FO]` co-occurrence in prose, since no dataset column encodes this, plus a 5-key hardcoded exclusion set for abilities that reference commit mechanics without being self-commit). The same `commitCapable` boolean also drives a small ◈ marker/tooltip next to the ability name — one call site drives both. `HudForceTab.tsx`'s `handleCommit`/`handleCancelCommit` (desktop; the old `MobileHudLayout.tsx`'s mirrored release handler was deleted by the Mobile Shell Rebuild task and has no replacement yet — mobile currently has no Force commit/release path at all) calls the shared `fetchFreshCommitState` (`forceUtils.ts`) immediately before writing — re-reads `force_rating_committed`/`force_commitments` fresh rather than trusting a possibly-stale local prop, the same race-safe pattern `purchaseTalent`/`purchaseForceAbility` use — then fire `logPurchaseNotification` (`purchase_type: 'force'`, `xp_cost: 0`) so a commit/release shows up in the GM roll feed. No overcommit guard exists by design (the GM polices it; an overcommitted socket just renders as excess). The GSAP flight-ceremony (`runCommitFlight`) is purely cosmetic — the write (`onCommit(...).finally(...)`) is never awaited on or gated by the animation, and is skipped entirely (not shortened) under `usePrefersReducedMotion`.
  - `normalizeDescription`/`renderedPlainText` (uses `parseSymbols` to strip shortcode/heading markup before comparing) suppresses an owned upgrade's description in the drawer only when it is byte-identical to the power's own base description after normalization — checked against the live respec dataset, no power/ability pair currently matches (ability descriptions always carry materially more content), so this defensively-implemented suppression does not currently fire for any power; not a bug, just unexercised.
  - When `canGainForceRating` is true (eligible via a Force-sensitive spec, Force Rating still 0, not yet purchased), the panel renders a "Gain Force Rating 1 — 10 XP" CTA instead of the power grid, calling `onPurchaseForceRating` (`handlePurchaseForceRating` in `useCharacterData.ts`). Desktop's `HudLeftRail` Force nav tab is gated on `canAccessForceTab` (`isForceUser || isEligibleForForceRating(...)`, computed in `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx`) rather than `isForceUser` alone, so eligible-but-unpurchased characters can still reach the tab to buy in; the Force Check quick-action button remains gated on `isForceUser` (Force Rating ≥ 1) only. A dismissible "Force Sensitivity Awakens" prompt (inlined in `HudModalsOverlay.tsx`, driven by `pendingForceRatingOffer` state in `useCharacterData.ts`) offers the same purchase right after buying a Force-sensitive specialization; "Later" does not revert the specialization purchase. Mobile (`MobileForceScreen`/`MobileRunner`) does not yet implement this gating — tracked as a follow-up.
  - "Purchase New Force Power" (`src/app/character/[id]/talents/page.tsx`) now uses `ForcePowerSelectorList` (below) instead of the old ad-hoc portal, mirroring `BuySpecButton`'s shell.
- `ForceCheckOverlay` (`src/components/force-check/ForceCheckOverlay.tsx`) — third adopter of the **horizontal focus console**, replacing the vertical `.hud-quick-drawer` form. Shares the `Modal` shell, the ignition convention, the `.fc-*` classes, the three-column `.fc-cols` grid and the full-width footer with Combat and Skill; `.fc-cols.is-force` widens the spend column to `1fr 1.05fr 1.25fr`. Per-check accent: `.cc-modal.is-check-force` / `.fc-pulse.is-check-force` → `--check-accent: var(--hud-accent-purple)`, so the top bar reads purple-leads-theme-trails and the ignition flares purple from `.hud-rail-btn-force`.
  - **The redesign came out of player testing**, which found three things broken and which the new layout exists to fix: rolled points sat at the top of the panel while spending happened at the bottom (currency disconnected from the shop); step 2 was a large unexplained orb captioned "tap to channel the Force" — *channel* is theme, not mechanics, and players did not know what the circle was; and nothing visibly connected the dice roll to the Force Points that appeared.
  - **① Power** — rows in the shared style, selection marks the column done. The old purchased/total **dot row is gone** (unexplained, read as noise); it is now a plain `{purchasedCount} / {totalCount} upgrades` count, both values already supplied by `useForcePowers`. The selected row carries the power's own `description` via `RichText`, 3-line clamped with a More/Less toggle — this is where `ForceDescriptionPanel`'s power blurb went.
  - **② Roll Force Dice** — **the orb is retired**; the column renders the actual dice. One `.fc-fdie` per Force die, face-down with a neutral `?`, styled as the white FFG die with its cyan `--die-force` glow (the sealed dice token used for its actual purpose — a rendered die face). Guidance is stated in plain mechanics ("Your Force Rating gives you N Force dice… generates the Force Points you'll spend"), deliberately **not** using *channel* as the explanatory verb. Rolling tumbles the dice with cycling faces, then each settles showing its ACTUAL result; a die that produced nothing shows a visible `—` blank, and the summary counts blanks explicitly. After the roll the column enters its done state with the dice frozen — that is the roll record and it stays on screen.
  - **The flight** — once the dice settle, each Force Point animates from **the die that produced it** into the bank at the head of column 3, making the roll → currency causation visible. One flyer per point (a die showing two pips sends two flyers, sourced from the individual `[data-fp]` pip elements), `FLY_MS` 550ms travel, `power2.inOut`. The stagger is `min(120, 840 / points)` — **the GAP compresses past ~7 points, never the individual flight**, so the tail is bounded at ~1.3s for any Force rating. Flyers are `position: fixed` nodes appended to `document.body`, tracked in `flyNodesRef` and removed both on completion and in the effect's cleanup, so an interrupted flight leaves no orphans. **The animation never gates state**: `points` is derived from `forceRoll` and is complete the instant the roll resolves, so every point is armable and spendable mid-flight; the timeline only controls when each bank token becomes visible (`landedIds`). Reduced motion skips the flight entirely and fills `landedIds` immediately.
  - **③ Spend** — the Force Point bank sits at the **head** of the column, directly above the effects it pays for, and is `position: sticky` so it stays put whether `.fc-console-body` scrolls (short list) or `.fc-fx-scroll` scrolls internally (long list); it needs an opaque two-layer background because the accent wash over it is translucent. Tokens tap to arm (lift + orbiting dashed ring), tap an effect to place, tap an armed token again to disarm. Effect rows carry checkbox, name and the upgrade's rules text **inline via `RichText`**, 2-line clamped with More/Less — this is what retired `ForceDescriptionPanel`.
  - **Allocation model.** `alloc: Record<upgradeKey, ForcePoint[]>` is the source of truth, where `ForcePoint = { kind: 'light'|'dark', idx, dieIndex }` and `idx` is the point's index within its own kind. **`lightSpentIdx` / `darkSpentIdx` are now `useMemo` projections of `alloc`** rather than primary state — their meaning (a set of per-kind indices marked spent) is unchanged, and `handleChannelForce` still reads only `.size`, so all three write payloads are byte-identical. **An effect accepts ANY number of points** — there is no per-effect cap, because `pip_cost` is a text-derived heuristic that must never gate, cap, validate or be displayed. Placed points render as chips; each chip taps to return that one point to the bank. A row holding points cannot be toggled off. An active effect with ZERO points is valid and supported (passives, condition-only effects like Sympathy/Spite). A row holding a dark point takes the `--die-challenge` edge.
  - **Footer** — status left, commit right with the live `N Light · N Dark` tally. Any dark point placed swaps the status line to the advisory consequence (flip a Destiny token, take N Conflict, resolve with the GM) in `--die-challenge`. **Advisory only — no automation.** Commit calls the unchanged `handleChannelForce`.
  - **Unchanged and deliberately so**: `handleRollWithAnimation` (dice count, randomisation, result derivation, and its 1500ms tumble + 220ms/die reveal timing), `handleCta`, `toggleUpgradeActive`, `clearTimers`, and every `force_notifications` / `combat_log` / `roll_log` payload including the known `roll_log.result` field-smuggling and the hardcoded `is_dark: false` on `activated_upgrades` — both are separately logged and out of scope here.
  - **Base-power detection — fixed (Prompt A).** `basicUpgrade` used to be found with `key.toUpperCase().endsWith('BASIC')`. The real keys are inconsistent, and materially worse than first reported: under the **active respec dataset only 3 of 25 powers matched** (`BATTLEMEDREBASIC`, `JERINFBASIC`, `WARFORBASIC`) — `MOVEBASIC`/`SENSEBASIC` exist only on the inactive oggdude rows, so even Move and Sense were broken (`MOVEBARESPEC`, `SENSEBARE`). For each of the other 22 the base power rendered as an ordinary toggleable row **and was written into `activated_upgrades` on commit**, i.e. campaign roll records contained the base power recorded as a purchased upgrade. A data-integrity bug, not a display one; verified live against Commune (`COMMUNEBP`).
    Detection is now **structural**: `ForcePowerDisplay.basicAbilityKey`, resolved from `ability_tree.rows[0].abilities[0]` at the two places that built the type at the time (`useForcePowers.ts`; `MobileSessionCompanion.tsx`, since deleted by the Mobile Shell Rebuild task — the drift risk noted below no longer applies, there's only one producer now), and matched by exact key equality in `ForceCheckOverlay`. Row 0 col 0 is the base node in every tree in **both** datasets — audited across all rows: row 0 holds a single distinct ability key across its whole span (25/25 respec, 44/44 oggdude) and that key is never reused in any later row, so no dataset branch is needed. `basicAbilityKey` is `null` when a power has no `ability_tree`, in which case nothing is treated as basic and every row stays toggleable — the pre-fix behaviour, no crash. Force Points can still be allocated onto the basic row: allocation is not activation, and only the `activated_upgrades` membership changed. No other detection site exists in `src/`.
    **Second bug, found and fixed after live use: the basic-power row was rendered forced-`on` unconditionally** (`const on = row.basic || activatedKeys.has(row.key)`), i.e. pre-selected/checked from the moment a power was chosen, before the dice were even rolled and regardless of whether any Force Point was ever placed on it. Real FFG mechanics require a base power to be activated by spending a point (or occasionally toggled free, exactly like an upgrade), and some base abilities can be invoked more than once per check by placing more than one point (e.g. Heal/Harm's base heal — each ◐ heals again). Fixed: `on` is now `activatedKeys.has(row.key)` with no `row.basic` special case, so the basic row behaves identically to every other effect row — starts inactive, activates on tap-with-an-armed-point (or a free toggle), and accepts any number of points via the existing uncapped `alloc[key]` array (no code change needed there — it never had a per-row cap). `row.basic` now only affects styling (`.fc-fx-row.is-basic`) and the `activated_upgrades` write-payload exclusion, never activation state. A matching CSS bug was fixed alongside it: `.fc-fx-row.is-basic .fc-fx-head { cursor: default }` made the row look non-clickable even though its `onClick` always worked — removed. `activeCount` (drives the "Spend" stage's done-state and the "Ready to commit" status line) was scoped to `nonBasicUpgrades` only, under the old assumption that the basic row's activation was constant and not worth counting; broadened to `upgrades` (basic + non-basic) so a check where the *only* thing activated is the base power still reads as ready. Live-verified on Commune: basic row `is-on:false` both before the roll and immediately after (no pre-selection); placing one point sets `is-on:true` with 1 chip; placing a second point keeps it on with 2 chips (multi-activation confirmed).
    **Historical rows deliberately not migrated** — 2 `roll_log` rows and 0 `force_notifications` rows carry a basic key; accepted as historical noise.
  - **Resolved by deletion, not a fix — `MobileSessionCompanion.tsx` was a second producer of `ForcePowerDisplay`.** It built the type inline (abilities map + tree nodes + connections) instead of calling `useForcePowers`, so the two constructors had already drifted (mobile's `canPurchase`/connection builder still used the `col + 1` and `span > 0` logic that the hook fixed), and `basicAbilityKey` had to be added twice for this reason. The Mobile Shell Rebuild task deleted `MobileSessionCompanion.tsx` outright — confirmed it was never actually mounted anywhere in the app (`grep "<MobileSessionCompanion"` returned zero hits), so nothing was lost. `useForcePowers.ts` is now the only producer.
  - **Logged, not fixed — `combat_log` has no `activated_upgrades` column**, yet `ForceCheckOverlay.tsx`'s `handleChannelForce` includes one in its `combat_log` insert. Either PostgREST drops the unknown key silently or the insert is partial; the column exists only on `force_notifications`. Needs its own investigation.
  - **Mobile** — `MobileSessionCompanion.tsx` (deleted by the Mobile Shell Rebuild task, and confirmed never actually mounted anywhere) used to render this same component, covered by the existing `@container (max-width: 900px)` rule, which collapses `.fc-cols.is-force` to a single column. Mobile currently has no Force Check entry point at all — reintroducing one is a later shell prompt's job, not this one's; there remains deliberately no second Force Check implementation to write when it is.
- `ForceDescriptionPanel` — **DELETED.** The slide-out ⓘ panel is gone: its two trigger buttons never passed *which* item was tapped, so it always rendered the same content regardless. Everything it showed is now reachable inline — the power's own description on the selected power row in column 1, each upgrade's rules text on its own row in column 3, both via `RichText`. Its `pip_cost` "cost icons" were deliberately not carried over: `pip_cost` is a heuristic and must not be displayed.
- `CombatCheckOverlay` (`src/components/combat-check/CombatCheckOverlay.tsx`) — **centred modal**, not a drawer (prompt 1b). It renders through the shared `Modal` shell (`maxWidth="min(1180px, calc(100vw - 48px))"`, `panelBackground="var(--hud-surface-hi)"`), which portals it to `document.body`. **The portal is required, not cosmetic:** the old `.hud-quick-drawer` root carried `backdrop-filter: blur(20px)`, which creates a containing block for `position: fixed` descendants and would anchor the overlay inside the drawer on Safari. The drawer also capped the panel at `clamp(15rem, 17vw, 18rem)` (240–288px), which is what truncated weapon names.
  - Scrim, Escape, scrim-click close and z-index all come from `Modal`; scrim click routes to the existing `handleClose`, so there is no second close path. **`Modal` has no focus trap and no scroll lock** — neither exists anywhere in this app, and Combat Check deliberately matches the shared pattern rather than growing a one-off. Adding them belongs in `Modal` so all callers benefit.
  - `.cc-modal` is the inner shell: `display:flex; flex-direction:column; max-height: min(90vh, calc(100vh - 64px))`. The `min()` keeps it at or under `Modal`'s own `90vh` panel cap so there is never a double scrollbar. The stage area scrolls internally and `.fc-tray` stays `position: sticky` at the top of that scroll region.
  - Entry animates the inner `.cc-modal` wrapper via GSAP (never the positioned scrim), gated on `usePrefersReducedMotion()` which is in the effect's dependency array. **Exit is not animated** — `Modal` unmounts synchronously on `open=false`, and delaying that would mean wrapping the close path.
  - `ForceCheckOverlay` still uses `.hud-quick-drawer`; the two check panels are intentionally inconsistent until Force Check is promoted too.
  - **Per-check accent identity.** The check consoles **share one skeleton and wear their own accent** — the layout similarity is deliberate (a player who learns one check has learned all three), so only the identity surfaces recolour. Four inherited custom properties, declared once in `globals.css` on `.cc-modal, .fc-pulse` and overridden by a `.is-check-<name>` modifier on the console root:

| Property | Combat (baseline) | Skill | Force (Prompt 3, reserved) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--check-accent` | `--hud-accent` | `--hud-gold` | `--hud-accent-purple` |
| `--check-accent-alt` | `--hud-gold` | `--hud-accent` | — |
| `--check-glow` | `--hud-glow` | `color-mix(in srgb, var(--hud-gold) 35%, transparent)` | — |
| `--check-highlight` | `--hud-gold` | `--hud-text` | — |

  - Resolved: Skill Check is `#C8A24E` in Ember, `#5BBCD8` in Kyber, `#8AAFC8` in gm-imperial — `--hud-gold` is defined in all three themes, so **no new global token was needed**. Combat resolves `--check-accent`/`--check-accent-alt`/`--check-glow`/`--check-highlight` to exactly `--hud-accent`/`--hud-gold`/`--hud-glow`/`--hud-gold`, i.e. the literal values every consumer named before scoping — Combat's rendered output is unchanged by construction, verified live in both themes.
  - Every consumer writes `var(--check-accent, var(--hud-accent))`, so an unscoped `.fc-*` element still resolves to the theme accent on its own (verified: a detached `.fc-diff-opt.is-selected` appended to `<body>` borders `#E03020` in Ember).
  - **`--check-highlight` exists because gold was doing two jobs.** `.fc-dstep-count`, `.fc-stepbtn.is-add`, `.fc-stepbtn.is-downgrade:hover`, `.fc-note b` and `RangeBandStep`'s selected-band treatment used a fixed `--hud-gold` as a SECONDARY highlight, distinct from the accent. Combat keeps that. Skill cannot — gold is its primary accent there and the two would collapse into one colour — so Skill remaps the secondary to `--hud-text`.
  - **`.fc-pulse` carries the modifier too.** `Modal` renders the pulse and the scrim as *siblings* of `.cc-modal` inside the portal, not descendants, so the pulse cannot inherit the console root's scope and needs the class applied directly (`portalExtra={<div className="fc-pulse is-check-skill" />}`).
  - **Ignition** already took `accent`/`glow` as parameters — no helper change. Combat's call site passes `var(--hud-accent)`/`var(--hud-glow)` exactly as before; Skill passes `var(--check-accent)`/`var(--check-glow)`, which resolve off the scoped elements, so the console and its flare can never disagree.
  - **Not recoloured, deliberately:** the console header title and its glyph (`⌖` / `⬠`) stay `--hud-text` / `--hud-text-faint` in every check — they were never accent-coloured, and tinting them would have changed Combat's appearance. **Sealed and untouched:** all FFG dice identity colours (`DICE_COLOR`/`SYM_COLOR`/`DICE_META`, `--die-*`), activation-type colours, difficulty diamonds, proficiency pips and die swatches — these render their own identity, never the check accent.
  - **Rail side** (`.hud-rail-btn-combat` / `-skill` / `-force`): each button wears the accent of the console it opens, so the button and the flare that answers it agree. Tint only — sizing, spacing and layout live on the shared `.hud-rail-btn` base and are untouched (measured identical: 60×48, `padding: 0`, `gap: 3px`). The raw rgba hand-mixes these replaced were theme-blind near-matches of the same tokens (`224,58,30` ≈ Ember `--hud-accent`; `200,170,80` ≈ `--hud-gold`), so they stayed red/gold in Kyber while the console they opened went blue/cyan. `.hud-rail-btn-skill .hud-rail-symbol` had no rule at all and fell through to the base text colour; it now exists.
  - **Focus console, horizontal three-column form.** Tray strip on top, three side-by-side stage columns (`.fc-cols`, `1fr 1fr 1fr`, 1px dividers between and none after the last), roll footer beneath — the whole check on one screen. **This is the convention Skill Check and Force Check inherit.** It replaced a vertical stage rail whose third stage fell below the fold before a weapon had even been chosen; that rail's markup and CSS (`.fc-stages`, `.fc-stage-body`, `.fc-stage-inner`, and the height-spring effect) are deleted, not conditionally bypassed.
  - Stages: 1 Weapon, 2 Range **or** Opponent (heading follows the weapon: ranged → Range, melee → Opponent, Opponent by default), 3 Adjust Pool.
  - **Sequencing survives the layout change** — it is what keeps the panel readable, and three equally-lit columns would recreate the original attention-scatter problem. Locked columns are dimmed and carry `pointer-events: none` (clicking one is verified to be a no-op); the active column lifts to `--hud-surface-mid` with a 2px accent underline and glow along its bottom edge; a completed column fills its stage number with the accent and shows a truncating summary in its header, and clicking that header returns focus to it for editing.
  - Locked dimming uses **`filter: opacity(0.3)`, not the `opacity` property** — the ignition content stagger writes an inline `opacity: 1` on these same columns (they are its stagger targets) and an inline style beats a class. `filter` is an independent channel, so the two never fight.
  - `min-height: 340px` per column keeps the console a stable shape as the player moves through it.
  - **Stacking is a container query, not a viewport breakpoint**: `.fc-console-body` is `container-type: inline-size` and `@container (max-width: 900px)` collapses `.fc-cols` to one column, degrading to the old vertical reading order. The panel is a modal whose width is its own business, so a viewport query would be wrong.
  - Column 1 lets the picker take the column's spare height (no four-row cap) with sticky section headers, and pins the Aim / 2nd Aim / Assist / Guarded row to the bottom. Column 2 staggers its children in with a short GSAP fade when the weapon type flips and the Range track swaps for the Opponent list — skipped under reduced motion. Column 3 keeps a two-column stepper grid, with the two upgrade rows spanning full width so label, `Ability → Proficiency` sublabel and an explicit `↓ Undo` button all fit.
  - **The stage rail is presentation over existing state.** `currentStep` and every other `CombatCheckState` field keep their meaning and their writers. The only added state is `openStage` (which stage is visually expanded) plus refs for the tray and the three stage bodies. Auto-advance is **composed at the JSX call site** (`onSelect={w => { handleWeaponSelect(w); setOpenStage(...) }}`) rather than in an effect, so `handleWeaponSelect`/`handleBandSelect` stay byte-identical and no `setState` runs synchronously inside an effect.
  - **Melee stage 2 — opponent selection.** For a melee weapon the stage lists the **enemy adversaries in the current encounter** (`meleeOpponents` filters `alignment === 'enemy'`; allied NPCs are never offered) and the player picks one, which routes through the existing `handleTargetSelect`. `primaryTarget` now resolves as *player selection → encounter's first enemy → null*. Difficulty comes from `getMeleeDifficulty(primaryTarget)`, unchanged.
    - **Manual-difficulty fallback** renders whenever `getMeleeDifficulty` reports a `fallbackReason`, which now means only "no target at all" (see the sparse-`skillRanks` fix below). It surfaces the existing `difficultyAdd` stepper (same `dssAdj`, same floor) inline in stage 2.
    - Melee stage completion is "an opponent is chosen, or there are none to choose"; ranged is unchanged (`selectedBand !== null`).
  - **Unarmed is a permanent row.** The picker offers it under every attack type, not only melee. `handleWeaponSelect` therefore forces `derivedType = 'melee'` for the `__unarmed__` sentinel rather than only when `attackType` is still null — otherwise picking the fist after a blaster left the check as ranged and demanded a range band.
  - **Dice tray** (`.fc-tray.fc-tray-strip`) is a full-width horizontal strip above the columns — label at left, dice flowing left-to-right and wrapping. No longer sticky: nothing scrolls past it in the horizontal form. Positive dice (proficiency, ability, boost, force) render left of a `.fc-tray-divider`, negative (difficulty, challenge, setback) right. Any pool change re-triggers a `.fc-tray-pulse` class animation. The pulse is decorative and **never gates a write** — the pool state is already committed when it runs; a six-tap burst on a stepper yields exactly +6, verified live.
  - **Roll footer** (`.fc-roll-bar.fc-roll-footer`) spans the full width below the columns: status text at left, button at right with `min-width: 220px`. The CTA (`.fc-roll-btn`) is inert until `canRoll`, then gains `.is-armed` (accent fill + sheen sweep). A subtitle states what is missing while inert (`Choose a weapon…` / `Choose a range band…` / `Pool is empty`).
  - `FcStepper` is the local Adjust Pool row atom: die swatch, name, optional sub-label, and `−`/count/`+`. On the two upgrade rows the minus is an explicit `↓ Undo` downgrade rather than a generic decrement, and is disabled (a no-op) at zero. **No new downgrade handlers were added** — the pre-existing `dssAdj` inverse already was the downgrade; this is a labelling change only.
  - `StageHead` and the picker's `PickerRow` live at **module scope**, not inside the component body — a component declared during render is a new type every render (React Compiler rejects it).
  - Dual-wield review keeps its exact place in the flow (`dualWieldReview`), but renders inside the stage-1 body instead of replacing the whole panel.
  - Shared styling lives in `globals.css` under the `.fc-*` "focus console" prefix so the Skill Check and Force Check panels can reuse one stylesheet rather than three near-copies. `.fc-dice-grid` uses `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, 240px), 1fr))` — two columns in the 680px modal, one when the container is narrow — the column count must follow the **container**, not the viewport; the same rule kept the layout correct through the drawer→modal move without a breakpoint change.
  - **Removed:** `TargetSelectStep.tsx` and `AttackTypeStep.tsx` (imported but never rendered) are deleted. `FlatSection` and `DssStepper` are gone with the flat layout.
  - `WeaponSelectStep` (`steps/WeaponSelectStep.tsx`) — **chip + picker**, replacing the always-expanded pill grid that cost permanent vertical space. A single `.fc-chip` shows the current weapon with a `Swap ▾` affordance; tapping opens `.fc-picker`, a capped-height scroll region (`max-height: 216px`, roughly four rows) with a bottom fade mask, scrolled so the current selection is in view on open. Section order is unchanged from prompt 1a: Equipped → Carried → Always Available, Unarmed last. The stowed-weapon `⚠ Equipping costs a Maneuver` confirmation keeps its existing flow. Two-Weapon Attack is an explicit toggle revealing Primary/Off-hand slots; the off-hand slot opens the **same picker component** populated from `findDualWieldCandidates`, and the resolution rule beneath the slots is lifted verbatim from `DualWieldReviewStep` (lower skill rank and characteristic, +1 same skill / +2 different, secondary hits on 2 Advantage or Triumph). Maneuver buttons (Aim, 2nd Aim, Assist, Guarded) keep their existing handlers and enable conditions — restyled only. Every picker row and the chip use the same crosshair placeholder in `.fc-chip-icon`; the raised-fist emoji and square-fill glyph introduced during the redesign are gone (a universal icon pass is planned separately). **Weapon qualities on picker rows (this task)**: `refWeaponQualityMap` (previously an unused prop) is now consumed — each `PickerRow` renders its weapon's `RefWeapon.qualities` as `QualityBadge` chips (`@/components/character/QualityBadge`, unmodified, same `desktop` variant and rank-in-label convention every other call site uses) beneath the stats line, wrapped in a flex-wrap row. `displayableQualities()` (module-level helper in this file) filters to qualities that are present in `refWeaponQualityMap`, have non-empty descriptions, and are not `STAGGER` (a `NON_QUALITY_KEYS` set) — STAGGER is a mechanical condition inflicted by Concussive, not a real weapon quality, and its ref row has an empty description besides. Guards `Array.isArray(ref.qualities)` before filtering (one ref row, `BARADIUMCHRG`, stores `qualities: {}` instead of `[]` — same guard style as `criticalUtils.ts`/`derivedStats.ts`). Display-only — no dice pool, difficulty, damage, or crit effect; Vicious's existing `checkCriticalEligibility` path is untouched. `src/lib/tooltips/weaponQualities.ts` (hand-written duplicate quality descriptions, zero consumers) was deleted as part of this task — `ref_weapon_qualities` is the only source of quality description text now.
    - **List order is Equipped → Carried → Unarmed.** `UNARMED_WEAPON` (the synthetic `__unarmed__` sentinel) always sorts **last**, after every equipped and carried weapon, never interleaved. It stays melee-only — the `attackType === 'melee' || attackType === null` gate is unchanged, so it never appears under a ranged attack type.
    - **Dual-wield off-hand selection.** `findDualWieldCandidates(selected, allEquipped, refWeaponMap)` returns **every** eligible off-hand and the card renders one row per instance for the player to choose. It replaced `findDualWieldPartner`, which returned a partner only when exactly one candidate existed (`candidates.length !== 1 → null`) — so a character with two or more eligible off-hands could not dual wield at all. Exclusion is **by instance id, never by weapon type or key**: two of the same blaster are individually selectable, and picking one as primary leaves the other available as off-hand.
    - A single candidate is **offered, not auto-selected** — the player always chooses explicitly.
    - Unarmed is never dual-wieldable in either direction: excluded from candidate lists, and selecting it as primary yields no candidates. The `refWeaponMap` lookup already misses for `__unarmed__`, but the sentinel check is explicit so adding a ref row later cannot silently enable it.
    - Every other eligibility condition is unchanged — same ranged/melee class and both weapons passing `canDualWield` (`src/lib/weaponHandedness.ts`, untouched). Second-weapon difficulty, `handleDualWieldSwap`, `DualWieldReviewStep`'s flow and `handleDualWieldContinue`'s write are all unchanged.
  - `RangeBandStep` (`steps/RangeBandStep.tsx`) — the ranged view is now a **segmented track** (`.fc-range-track`) with difficulty diamonds beneath each band; out-of-range bands are dimmed and disabled, and the max-range note is preserved. The melee view (opposed-roll box, `getMeleeDifficulty` fallback path, auto-select of `'engaged'` for engaged-only weapons) is unchanged.
  - `DicePoolReviewStep` (`steps/DicePoolReviewStep.tsx`) — **headless calculator only.** It is mounted inside a `display:'none'` wrapper purely to derive the pool and emit it through `onPoolChange`; the stage rail owns every visible control. Its rendered UI (context summary, dice rows, `AdjustControl` steppers) never painted and has been deleted along with the dead `handleUpgradeCheck`/`handleDowngradeCheck` pair, `SectionLabel`, `DiceRow` and `AdjustControl`. The pool derivation, `adjFloors`, `adj` and the `onPoolChange` effect are unchanged — the sole edit inside the derivation is `let` → `const` on the `stdPro`/`stdAbl` destructure, forced by `prefer-const` once the dead reassignment in the removed UI was gone. **`ManualAdjustments`/`EMPTY_ADJUSTMENTS` moved to `dice-engine.ts`** (this task) — this file re-exports both by name so existing `import ... from '.../DicePoolReviewStep'` call sites (e.g. `CombatCheckOverlay.tsx`) keep working unchanged. The modifier-application block (ability upgrade, difficulty add-then-upgrade, talent boost/setback-removal, final pool assembly) was deleted and replaced with one `applyModifiers()` call (see `dice-engine.ts` entry below) — same fields, same `challengeAdd`/`abilityUpgrades`/`difficultyUpgrades`/`forceAdd` semantics, just one shared implementation. The dual-wield branch's `baseDiff` no longer has `adjustments.difficultyAdd` pre-folded into it (that double-applied the delta once `applyModifiers` also applied it) — `difficultyAdd` is now applied exactly once, inside `applyModifiers`, for both branches.

### GM HUD Sub-components (`src/app/gm/`)
- `GmLeftRail` — 52px fixed left rail; navigation buttons (◉ Tokens/gold [id: 'map'], ⊞ Tools/blue, ◉ Party/teal, Combat with empire.png faction image/red); utility buttons (⬡ Dice/gold, ▦ Screen/gold, ⊟ Library/blue) below divider; uses `FONT_BODY`, `RADIUS`, `Z.fab` from tokens; Combat button uses empire.png faction image with CSS filter chain; the 'map' tab (id: 'map') was renamed to **Tokens** (label: 'Tokens', icon: '◉') in the MapToolsRadial feature — its panel now shows only token management via `GmTokenControls`
- `GmTopBar` — (mentioned in git status) top navigation bar for GM dashboard
- `GmShell` — (mentioned in git status) shell layout wrapper for GM interface
- `GmMapView` — interactive token map. Hover tooltip (`TokenTooltip`, PC-only since Prompt 11 — NPC/vehicle tooltips were removed entirely, since the Encounter Deck's cards and the dossier already surface that information; PC content/styling/behavior is unchanged from before Prompt 11: name/type/characteristics/wounds/strain/soak/defense as plain text, `pointerEvents: 'none'`, no click-lock/pin mode, no ± combat controls — `useEncounterCombatControls` is not called from this file). `onTokenClick` fires on a genuine tap (not a drag) for the Encounter Deck to focus the corresponding card. `handleRemoveToken` (right-click context-menu remove) does its own minimal, non-optimistic `combat_encounters` write for the initiative-slot cascade-delete. There is no `onOpenCombatCheck` prop or combat-check state owned here — Prompt 9 replaced the old weapon+target hand-off to `CombatCheckOverlay` with `CheckConsole`'s inline roller (see `CheckConsole` below); `CombatCheckOverlay` itself is untouched and still mounted by `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` for PC-side checks.
- `AddConflictModal` (`src/components/gm/AddConflictModal.tsx`) — modal for GM to add morality conflicts to Force-sensitive characters; filters character list to force-sensitive only; inserts to `character_conflicts` table with character_id, campaign_id, description, narrative, session_label, is_resolved, player_acknowledged
- `ForcePresenceGmPanel` (`src/components/gm/ForcePresenceGmPanel.tsx`, Prompt C) — conditional REPLACEMENT for `GmToolsPanel`'s Force tab Morality controls + `AddConflictModal` trigger when `morality_system === 'force_presence'` (branch lives in `GmToolsPanel.tsx`, not this file — `'vanilla'` renders the old controls byte-identically). Per force-sensitive roster character: a simplified static 10-pip read (no ambient motion, no portaled chooser — `ForcePresenceCard.tsx` exports no reusable sub-component to build on), "+1 Conflict"/"+1 Tranquility" award buttons, a running `NC · MT` counter readout, and a de-emphasized "Edit" toggle that reveals a small inline (non-portaled) chooser for the GM's own backup Balance Point correction. Award presses use a promise-chain queue per row (`queueRef`), not a `useState` busy flag — see `useGmCharacterActions.ts`'s note above for why. Also renders the Consolidate flow: a "Consolidate Session Conflict/Tranquility" button computes a disposable preview (GM-only readout, never sent to players) via `computeConsolidatePreview()`; only "Confirm & Reset Counters" writes.
- `PresenceFlash` (`src/components/player-hud/PresenceFlash.tsx`, Prompt C) — player-facing GM-award notification, one parameterized component (`variant: 'conflict' | 'tranquility'`) rather than two files, since the two are near-mirror images (violet-vs-cyan, dark-vs-light emblem, identical wash+flare+settle GSAP choreography). State-management shape matches `DestinyGMFlash.tsx` exactly (full-viewport `position:fixed;inset:0`, portaled to `document.body`, auto-dismissing, mounted by a callback-driven boolean in `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx`/rendered from `HudModalsOverlay.tsx`) — only the motion technique differs, since `DestinyGMFlash` uses plain CSS-transition fades with no GSAP at all, while this needs a richer center-screen flare. Applies the Prompt B stacking-context lesson from the start: GSAP only ever touches an inner wrapper, never the `position:fixed` root. **Reduced-motion bug found and fixed live**: `usePrefersReducedMotion` returns `false` on the very first render (before its own effect corrects it via `matchMedia`) — a component whose animation effect only runs once on mount needs `prefersReducedMotion` in its own effect's dependency array to react to that correction; an empty `[]` (as originally written) permanently captures the stale first-render `false`, so the full ~3.1s animated timeline ran instead of the intended ~900ms static reduced-motion hold. Fixed by adding `prefersReducedMotion` to the deps (safe to re-run given the existing `tl.kill()`/`clearTimeout` cleanup).
- `GmConflictPip` (`src/components/gm/GmConflictPip.tsx`) — read-only 10×10 purple circle pip; wraps `<Tooltip>` with conflict description + session label; used in `GmPartyMiniCard` pip row
- `GmPartyMiniCard` (`src/app/gm/panels/GmPartyMiniCard.tsx`) — compact character card in the GM party panel; pip row between strain bar and soak shows `CriticalInjuryPip` (with heal confirm) and `GmConflictPip` up to 3 each, with `+N` overflow badge
- `ArchivedCharactersModal` (`src/app/gm/panels/ArchivedCharactersModal.tsx`) — portal modal listing archived characters for a campaign; fetches lazily on open; provides **Restore** (calls `restoreCharacter`, invokes `onRestored` so `GmShell` can push the char back into active list) and **Delete permanently** (inline single-click confirm, calls `deleteCharacter`); opened via a "View Archived" footer button in `GmPartyPanel`
- `GmReferenceLibraryPanel` (`src/components/gm/GmReferenceLibraryPanel.tsx`) — searchable read-only reference panel accessed via the ⊟ Library rail button; `'library'` is part of `GmPanelId` and uses the existing left-side slide mechanism at 420px; Talents tab queries `ref_talents` (all rows) on first keystroke and filters client-side by name; Force Powers tab queries `ref_force_powers` + `ref_force_abilities` on first keystroke — matched powers render as purple cards with all their ability rows nested below; abilities matching without a matched parent power render as standalone cards labelled with the parent power name; both tabs show empty state until text is entered
- `GmMarketPanel` (`src/app/gm/panels/GmMarketPanel.tsx`, The Archive Market Prompt 2, widened in the Fix Pass) — GM-only rail panel (⌂ Market, `'market'` in `GmPanelId`) generating/managing the campaign's single active merchant. Width is `calc(100% - 10rem)` relative to its sliding-panel container (the map area, itself `flex:1` between the 3.25rem rail and the 16.25rem Roll Feed rail) — **not** a hardcoded px/rem guess: this self-derives at any viewport, always leaving a 10rem strip of map visible at the right, and can never reach the Roll Feed since it's structurally clipped by that container's own `overflow:hidden` regardless of the width value (≈1448px at a 1920px viewport — Playwright-verified Roll Feed fully visible with a clear map gap). Widest GM rail panel by a wide margin (`tools` is a fixed 35rem) — intended, it's a focus surface; every other panel's width is untouched (ternary diff confirmed byte-identical apart from the new `'market'` branch prepended). Header (name/location/live-to-players indicator) → two independently-scrolling columns (left: name/location/archetype/scale/legality params; right: stock grouped by tier with sticky tier headers) → footer pinned outside both scroll regions (Roll Stock / Snapshot ↓ / Restore ↑ / status / Open-Close to Players), matching `HudFullPanel`'s header/scroll/pinned-footer convention. Stock row: type/rarity/price/tier-actions are the only fixed-width columns; name is the sole `flex:1` column (`minWidth:10rem`) and carries a `title` attribute — at this width every catalogue item name renders in full except the single genuine outlier (`"Vac Attack Mk-127 Concealable Disc Launcher (Single-Line Mode)"`, 62 chars, the longest name across `ref_weapons`/`ref_armor`/`ref_gear`), which ellipsizes as the spec's documented last resort with its full name still reachable via the `title` tooltip. Price is edited through `NumberField` (`src/components/ui/NumberField.tsx`, the project's one canonical thematic number-stepper component — 49 fields across 17 files already used it before this one) rather than a bare `<input type="number">` — chevron ▴/▾ replace the browser spinner, value handling stays with the caller, still free-text editable and purely display-only. Tier headers show the skill required (Negotiation for back room, Streetwise for under the counter, none for open stock) and a difficulty rendered via the canonical `RichText` `:label:` colon-shortcode path (`rarityToDifficultyLabel()` from `marketGenerator.ts` → `parseSymbols.ts`'s `TIER_BASE_DIFFICULTY`) — never a hardcoded pip count, and `DICE_COLOR.difficulty` is referenced only through that path, never redefined. Catalogue is fetched once per mount (`ref_weapons`/`ref_armor`/`ref_gear`, `is_custom=false`, ~1213 rows across all three, well under PostgREST's 1000-row-per-table cap so no `fetchAllRows` pagination is needed). Form fields (name/location/archetype/scale/legality) sync from the loaded merchant exactly once (`syncedRef`) — a `touchedRef` flag (set the instant the GM edits any field) permanently disables that sync afterward, closing a real race where a slow catch-up SELECT could otherwise clobber an in-flight GM edit; `restoreSnapshot` explicitly resets both refs since a restore's entire point is to overwrite the form. All stock mutations (reveal/reveal-tier/tier-cycle/price/delete) route through `useMarketMerchant.ts`'s serialized read-fresh-then-write queue — Playwright-verified losing zero updates across 10 synchronous same-tick clicks on 10 different rows. **Fix Pass 2 — View action**: each row's action cluster (now `10rem` fixed to fit a fourth button) gained a `View` button opening `ItemDetailPopup` (`src/components/shared/ItemDetailPopup.tsx`, see below) — the same shared component `ItemDatabaseTab`'s Items tab uses, not a Market-specific fork. `useCatalogue()`'s single mount-time fetch was widened (weapon/armor/gear `select()`s gained `encumbrance,description,qualities,skill_key,damage,damage_add,crit,range_value,hard_points,defense,soak_bonus,encumbrance_bonus` alongside the fields the generator already needed) and now also builds a `details: Map<string, EditableItem>` keyed `` `${table}:${key}` `` — the popup's full data comes from this one existing fetch, not a second per-click query; `marketGenerator.ts`'s own `CatalogueItem` type is untouched (this map is a GmMarketPanel-local superset for display only). The panel holds its own `useItemIconContext(supabase, campaignId)` instance and local `handlePickIcon`/`handleResetIcon` (same upsert/delete-on-`item_icon_overrides` shape as `ItemDatabaseTab`'s, just a separate instance — same convention as every other independent GM surface holding its own icon-context instance) plus its own `ref_weapon_qualities` fetch for the popup's quality badges. Read/inspect + icon-override only — no award or inventory action is reachable, since `ItemDetailPopup` never had one to begin with. Playwright-verified: popup portals to `document.body` and renders centered on the true viewport (measured box center = 960px at a 1920px viewport, matching viewport center exactly — confirms it escapes the sliding-panel wrapper's inline `transform`, which would otherwise trap a non-portaled `position:fixed` popup inside the panel's own bounds); an icon override set from within Market persists to `item_icon_overrides` and is invisible to a second campaign; resetting to automatic removes the row; an in-progress price edit (typed but not yet blurred) survives opening and closing the popup untouched. **Prompt 3 — `activeChars`/`broadcastAll` props**: `campaignId, activeChars: Character[], broadcastAll: UseGmBroadcastReturn['broadcastAll']`, both threaded from `GmShell.tsx` (the same `useGmBroadcast(characters)` instance every other GM panel shares) — used solely by `handleToggleOpenToPlayers`, see `useMarketMerchant` hook entry above for what it does.
- `MarketStorefront` (`src/components/player-hud/MarketStorefront.tsx`, The Archive Market Prompt 3) — player-facing storefront popup. Props: `campaignId, credits: number, onClose`. Renders nothing (`usePlayerMarket` returns `null`) whenever there's no active/open merchant. Header: merchant name, `LOCATIONS`-resolved location label (from `marketGenerator.ts`, safe to import — pure data, not the generator logic itself), the character's credits (display-only, passed straight from `character.credits`, never validated or deducted anywhere in this file). Body: `market.visibleLines` as plain rows (type/name/restricted-R-badge/rarity/price, clickable to Inspect), then `market.lockedTiers` as neutral prompts — `"Make a [dice] [skill] check to see whether this merchant has more items available."` — wording that never implies stock exists or doesn't, difficulty rendered via the same canonical `RichText` `:label:` path as the GM panel. No buy button, no reserve, no "I want this" action anywhere in this file — the player talks to the GM, all transactions are manual. Inspect reuses `ItemDetailPopup` with only `resolveIcon` supplied (its own local `useCatalogueDetails()` hook fetches full public `ref_weapons`/`ref_armor`/`ref_gear` rows — the same columns `GmMarketPanel`'s `useCatalogue()` fetches, duplicated locally since this is the only other place that needs it; this data is fully public game-system knowledge, no leak concern, unlike the merchant's own stock selection). Portals to `document.body` at `Z.overlay` (below `ItemDetailPopup`'s own `Z.modal`, so Inspect naturally layers above the storefront). Dismiss is local state only (see `HudNotificationsDrawer`'s `vendor_offer` entry above for why) — `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` mounts this component gated on `storefrontOpen && vendorOfferPendingRow`, auto-opening once per newly-arrived `vendor_offer` pending-action id and force-closing the instant that row disappears (GM closed the merchant).
- `MapToolsRadial` (`src/app/gm/MapToolsRadial.tsx`) — floating arc-menu widget rendered in the GM map area when `isStagingTab === true` (injected by `GmMapView`). Four-arc SVG fan (Map Library ◫, Opening Crawl ▶, Token Scale ⊕, Table Display ⛶) fans from puck at 178°–272°. Three.js `WebGLRenderer` renders star field + wireframe terrain + scan-beam animated background. GSAP timelines drive open/close/hover/pick sequences. Map Library/Opening Crawl/Token Scale open floating popup panels as portals (`MapLibraryContent`, `OpeningCrawlContent`, `TokenScaleContent`); Table Display is a direct action — `window.open('/table?campaign=' + campaignId, '_blank')`, no panel — restoring the pre-radial "⛶ Table Display" button that was dropped from `GmMapPanel` when Map Library/Scale/Crawl moved into this widget. Self-contained: manages its own planet state via `useMapPlanets`, crawl row state, and map CRUD. Props: `campaignId`, `allMaps`, `activeMap`, `onDeleteMap`, `tokenScale`, `adjustTokenScale`. Draggable — drag offset snapshotted once on `mousedown` only; default position is bottom-right of the map area.
- `GmMapPanel` (`src/app/gm/panels/GmMapPanel.tsx`) — left-rail 'Tokens' tab panel (id: `'map'`); since the MapToolsRadial feature, shows **only** token management via `GmTokenControls`; Map Library, Token Scale, and Opening Crawl controls have been moved entirely into `MapToolsRadial`. Props: `campaignId`, `characters`, `tokens`, `addToken`, `removeToken`, `toggleVisibility`, `removeAllTokens`.
- `GmToolsPanel` (`src/app/gm/panels/GmToolsPanel.tsx`) — left-rail 'Tools' panel, tabbed: XP/Credits/Duty-Obl/Items/Talents/Adversary/Vehicle/Force. The Adversary/Vehicle tabs mount `AdversaryEditor`/`VehicleEditor` (`src/components/gm/{AdversaryEditor,VehicleEditor}.tsx`) — both already fully support an edit mode (`editId`/`template` props, update-vs-insert, "Edit X" vs "New X" title) since that's also how the older Library-via-Tokens workflow (`AdversaryLibrary`/`VehicleLibrary`'s own `openEdit`) edits a custom entry; GmToolsPanel just didn't feed those props before. Each tab now has an **Add New / Edit Existing** sub-mode toggle (mirrors the XP/Credits tabs' own `group`/`individual` toggle styling exactly). Edit Existing shows a searchable list of this campaign's custom entries — `allAdversaries.filter(a => a._isCustom)`/`allVehicles.filter(v => v._isCustom)`, both already fetched by this panel's existing `allAdversaries`/`allVehicles` effect (merges OggDude + this campaign's `ref_adversaries`/`ref_vehicles` custom rows via `dbRowToAdversary`/`dbRowToVehicle`) — no new fetch needed. Picking one sets a local edit-target state and remounts the editor (`key`'d on `_dbId`) with `editId`/`template` populated instead of blank; `onClose`/`onSaved` reset the sub-mode/target back to Add New. `Adversary` itself has no `_isCustom`/`_dbId` fields on its interface (unlike `Vehicle`, which declares them optionally) — `dbRowToAdversary` attaches them at runtime only, so this file defines a local `CustomAdversary = Adversary & { _isCustom?: boolean; _dbId?: string }` alias to read them back type-safely. Both editors previously always rendered as a full-viewport modal (`AdversaryEditor` via the shared `Modal` component, `VehicleEditor` via its own hand-rolled `createPortal` backdrop+centered-panel pair) — fine for `AdversaryLibrary`/`VehicleLibrary`'s own overlay-on-a-browsing-list flow, but wrong for GmToolsPanel: the modal backdrop is `position:fixed, inset:0`, portaled to `document.body`, so it visually covers the entire screen regardless of where in the tree it's mounted — the new toggle above it was there, just invisible underneath the backdrop, reading as "the tab is blank, a popup opens instead." Both editors gained a `presentation?: 'modal' | 'inline'` prop (default `'modal'` — zero behavior change for `AdversaryLibrary`/`VehicleLibrary`/`GmTokenControls`, which don't pass it); `'inline'` renders the exact same header/body/footer content in a plain bordered `HUD.panel` div with no portal/backdrop/fixed-positioning, so it sits naturally inside a host's own panel chrome. `GmToolsPanel` passes `presentation="inline"` on both editors, and its `onClose`/`onSaved` callbacks only reset the mode/target state now (no longer jump to the Items tab — that made sense for dismissing a modal, not for an inline "Cancel").
- `EncounterDeck` (`src/components/gm/EncounterDeck.tsx`) — pull-up card drawer docked to the bottom of the GM map panel; replaces the deleted Enemies tab (`EncounterAdversaryPanel`/`EncounterVehiclePanel`) and the map's former locked-tooltip combat controls. `buildRoster` and the `RosterEntry` interface are exported (previously module-private) so `EncounterDossier.tsx` can re-derive the same roster; `RosterEntry` carries an added `tokenId: string | null` field (the resolved `map_tokens.id` for the entity's slot, or null off-map) populated in `buildRoster`. **Nicknames**: `RosterEntry` also carries `displayName: string` (`entity.nickname || entity.name`), which `EntityCard` renders instead of `name` — `AdversaryInstance`/`VehicleInstance` each gained an optional `nickname?: string` field (purely cosmetic; `name` itself is deliberately untouched everywhere else — image keying via `advImages[a.name]`/the dossier's upload path, roll labels, initiative — so a renamed "Imperial Stormtrooper 2" still resolves its own per-instance image/rolls correctly). Editing the nickname happens in `EncounterDossier` (see below), not on the card itself, per this file's own established "wounds is the only editable control on this card" convention. The dossier's rename patches `map_tokens.label` directly (see below), but `MapCanvas.tsx`'s token-reconciliation loop previously only rebuilt an existing token's Pixi sprite when `token_image_url` changed — a `label` change alone just updated position/scale/alpha on the already-built sprite, whose label `Text` object (and its background-pill geometry) is computed once at `buildTokenSprite` time and never refreshed.
