Fixed by tracking `__label` alongside the existing `__imageUrl` marker on each sprite container and rebuilding on either changing, so an on-map token's visible label now follows a nickname rename too. **Prompt 12 — map-scoped roster**: `buildRoster(encounter, tokens, advImages, vehImages, activeMapId)` gained a required `activeMapId` param and now filters `encounter.adversaries`/`.vehicles` to `a.map_id === activeMapId` before building entries (both call sites — this file's own render and `EncounterDossier.tsx`'s re-derivation — already had `activeMapId` in scope as an existing prop). Previously the roster was campaign/session-scoped (JSONB on one `combat_encounters` row) while `map_tokens` was already map-scoped, so switching maps hid a map's tokens but left its *cards* visible everywhere — this was the mismatch fixed here. `AdversaryInstance`/`VehicleInstance` (`src/lib/adversaries.ts`/`src/lib/vehicles.ts`) each gained an optional `map_id?: string | null` field — the authoritative map association for BOTH on-map and off-map/benched entries (benching only deletes the `map_tokens` row via `removeToken`, never touches this field, so it needed no code change to "survive" benching). `RosterEntry` also gained `mapId: string | null` (mirrors `entity.map_id`), used by `deployEntry` as the redeploy target instead of whatever map happens to be currently active (they should always agree in practice — deploy only happens from within that map's own already-filtered deck view — but this is explicit rather than assumed, with `activeMapId` as a defensive fallback only). Every add path stamps `instance.map_id` at creation time: this file's `handleAddAdversary`/`handleAddVehicle`, `useGmSession.ts`'s `stagingAddToEncounter`/`stagingAddVehicleToEncounter` (the off-map-add strip's path), and `GmTokenControls.tsx`'s own independent `handleAddAdversaryToken`/`handleAddVehicleToken` (the "Tokens" rail tab has a *third*, parallel add-to-roster code path that also writes directly to `combat_encounters.adversaries`/`.vehicles` — found via full audit, not obvious from the deck alone). The collapsed handle's live enemy/friendly count badges (see below) are derived from a *local* `adversaries`/`vehicles` filter by `map_id === activeMapId` too, added for the same reason — without it the badge (sourced from `initiative_slots`, which has no map concept) would show a stale count disagreeing with the actual map-filtered cards once expanded. Migration `087_combat_encounter_roster_map_id.sql` is comment-only/documentation — `adversaries`/`vehicles` are JSONB arrays with no fixed per-element schema, so there is no column to `ALTER`; `map_id` is purely an application-level field. No backfill: confirmed via live DB audit that the only active `combat_encounters` row had an empty roster at the time, and historical (`is_active = false`) rows are never read as "the current roster" by any code path. Live-verified via Playwright + read-only DB checks against campaign "Legacy of Rebellion": add on Map A → switch to Map B (card and count badge both correctly absent) → switch back to A (both reappear) → bench (token gone, `map_id` preserved) → switch away and back (benched card still A-only) → deploy (token lands on the entry's own stored map) → remove (cleanup). `benchEntry`, `deployEntry`, `removeEntry`, `toggleHiddenEntry` are also exported standalone async functions (hoisted out of the component's `handleBench`/`handleDeploy`/`handleRemove`/`handleToggleHidden` closures, parameterized by an `opts` object of whatever they used to close over) — `EncounterDeck`'s own handlers are now thin `useCallback` wrappers around these, and `GmMapView.tsx` calls the same exported functions directly for `EncounterDossier`'s `onBenchDeploy`/`onRemove` props, so the cascade-delete/bench semantics exist in exactly one place. A `position:absolute` (never `fixed`) child rendered as a sibling of `MapToolsRadial` inside `GmMapView`'s map-area div when `isStagingTab === true`, confined by that div's `overflow:hidden`. Feature-complete end-to-end: full roster/library rail plus the add / off-map-add / bench / deploy / remove / hidden lifecycle, all wired to live Supabase writes (no stub state remaining). Collapsed state is a centered handle bar (`Z.sticky` — Fix Pass containment fix: was `Z.deck`=520, far above `Z.dropdown`=20 used by GM rail panels, which is what let the deck visually overlap an open panel; `Z.sticky`=10 keeps it above ordinary map content but below any open panel. The inner expanded-body div's own `Z.deckExpanded` is untouched — it's a flex-item child inside the same local stacking context the outer wrapper creates, so it only orders the deck's button-vs-body layering and was never the source of the cross-panel overlap) showing live enemy/friendly counts as red/green badges — enemy/friendly split for adversaries is derived from `encounter.initiative_slots[].alignment` (matched by `adversaryInstanceId`; `AdversaryInstance` itself has no `alignment` field), while `VehicleInstance.alignment` is read directly; both counts are first filtered to `map_id === activeMapId` (Prompt 12) so they never disagree with the map-filtered cards. Expanded body (`Z.deckExpanded`) is GSAP height-tweened (0 ↔ 236px, `power3.out`/`power2.in`) rather than `display`-toggled, and calls `onMapAreaResize?.()` in `onComplete`. Body has a search input toggling between two views: when empty, a horizontally-scrolling **roster rail** of `EntityCard`s built by module-level `buildRoster(encounter, tokens, advImages, vehImages, activeMapId)` (also derives adversary alignment via the same `initiative_slots` lookup — not a nonexistent `AdversaryInstance.alignment` — so it works for off-map entities too, which have a slot but no `map_tokens` row); when non-empty, `AdversaryLibrary`/`VehicleLibrary` (each with `onAddToken` wired to `handleAddAdversary`/`handleAddVehicle`, which call `adversaryToInstance(adv, groupSize)`/`vehicleToVehicleInstance(v, alignment, tokenImageUrl)` — 2-arg `adversaryToInstance` only, no alignment param — and auto-number repeat adds via `nextAutoName`; both spawn the new `map_tokens` row at `spawnPosition(tokens.filter(t => t.map_id === activeMapId).length)` rather than a hardcoded `(0.5, 0.5)` — every add used to land exactly on top of whatever was already there, so only the topmost-in-render-order token was ever visible, and since the `map_tokens` SELECT has no `ORDER BY`, GM and player sessions could independently land on a *different* topmost token, looking like "GM can't see it but the player can." `spawnPosition` (module-private, also used by `deployEntry`, which gained a `tokens` param for the same reason) spreads successive spawns via a golden-angle spiral around centre — no fixed pattern table needed), plus a slim **Off-map** strip (`flex: '0 0 6rem'`, visually secondary to the two full library columns; self-fetched via `fetchAdversaries()`, OggDude adversaries only, not custom homebrew — a disclosed scope limitation) — a single shared ⚔/🤝 toggle sets the default alignment for the strip, and each row is one compact `+` button (no per-row alignment split) via `handleAddOffMap` → `stagingAddToEncounter` (adversary-only; that hook has no vehicle overload). `EntityCard` renders a chamfered card with image/initial, OFF-MAP/HIDDEN/GRP badges, an HP/hull bar, a wound-or-hull stepper, a conditional strain stepper (nemesis adversaries and all vehicles), a conditional group-size stepper (minions), and a Bench-or-Deploy / Hidden-toggle / Remove icon row — wired respectively to `handleBench` (removes the token only, card stays with an OFF-MAP badge), `handleDeploy` (re-adds a token for an existing off-map entity's slot), `handleToggleHidden` (thin wrapper over `toggleVisibility`, unchanged), and `handleRemove` (cascade-deletes the adversary/vehicle instance, its `initiative_slots` entry, and its `map_tokens` row). `activeMapId: string | null` is a **required** prop (review fix — a `tokens[0]?.map_id` fallback previously risked writing `map_tokens` rows with `map_id: ''`); `handleAddAdversary`/`handleAddVehicle`/`handleDeploy` all early-return with a `console.warn` if `activeMapId` is null when they fire. **Wiring**: `GmShell.tsx` owns `deckOpen`/`focusedEntityId` state and passes `useGmSession`'s live `setStagingEncounter`/`saveStagingEncounter`/`markEncounterPending`/`clearEncounterPending`/`stagingAddToEncounter` down through `GmMapView` into `EncounterDeck`. **Fix Pass**: `handlePanelToggle` (the same single handler backing every `GmLeftRail` panel button, not a per-panel effect or a new state variable) now also `setDeckOpen(false)` whenever it's about to make a panel active — never when it's closing one. Direction is one-way: opening the deck (`onDeckToggle`, untouched) never touches `activePanel`, so a GM can freely open the deck while a rail panel is already showing; only opening a rail panel force-closes the deck. — all props are real, end-to-end wired Supabase writes (the earlier temporary stub-mount with no-op setters is gone). Clicking a token on the map (`GmMapView`'s `onTokenClick`, fired on a genuine tap and not a drag) opens the deck and sets `focusedEntityId` so the matching `EntityCard` highlights (gold border + glow); clicking an `EntityCard` itself (or a map token) opens `EncounterDossier` — see below. The roster/library rail's scrollable container reserves a static `paddingRight: '19rem'` gutter so cards never render underneath `MapToolsRadial`'s default bottom-right 300px-wide footprint (a fixed reservation, not radial-position-tracking — `MapToolsRadial` itself is untouched). `onMapAreaResize` fires `GmMapView`'s `handleMapAreaResize`, which increments a `recentreSignal` counter state passed to `MapCanvas` — this makes `MapCanvas` re-run `rebuildMap()` (recentring/rescaling the map image and grid to the current canvas size) once the deck's GSAP open/close animation completes, since the existing `ResizeObserver` in `MapCanvas` only resizes the Pixi renderer and never recentres the map content.
- `EncounterDossier` (`src/components/gm/EncounterDossier.tsx`) — expanded "card" view for a single adversary/vehicle instance, opened by clicking an `EncounterDeck` `EntityCard` or a map token (both existing click paths already forward a `DOMRect` of the clicked element). Mounted as a `position:absolute` sibling of `EncounterDeck` inside `GmMapView`'s map-area div (same `overflow:hidden` containment). Re-derives its own roster via the now-exported `buildRoster`/`RosterEntry` from `EncounterDeck.tsx` rather than receiving a `RosterEntry` prop (it's a sibling, not a child of `EncounterDeck`) — `entry = roster.find(r => r.instanceId === entityId)`. Props: `entityId`/`sourceRect` (null = closed, both lifted to `GmShell.tsx` as `dossierEntityId`/`dossierSourceRect`), `encounter`/`setEncounter`/`saveEncounter`/`supabase`/`campaignId`/`tokens`/`updateTokenWoundPct`/`markPending`/`clearPending`/`characters` (threaded through for the stats/check-console columns still to come), `onClose`, `onToggleVisibility`, `onBenchDeploy`, `onRemove` (the latter two dispatch to `EncounterDeck`'s exported `benchEntry`/`deployEntry`/`removeEntry` from `GmMapView.tsx`, not reimplemented). **Open/close animation**: a manual FLIP (not GSAP's `Flip.fit`) — `Flip.fit`'s `toEl` argument is resolved via `ElementState`, which calls `toEl.getBoundingClientRect()`/reads `toEl.parentNode` internally, so it requires a live DOM element or a captured `Flip.FlipState`, not a plain `DOMRect`; passing the click-captured `sourceRect` fails both the TS signature and at runtime. Instead: a `useLayoutEffect` sets `xPercent:-50, yPercent:-50` via `gsap.set` (so GSAP owns the full `transform`, not a raw CSS `translate(-50%,-50%)` string it would otherwise clobber), measures the panel's natural resting `getBoundingClientRect()`, computes the position/scale delta against `sourceRect`, and runs `gsap.fromTo` on `x`/`y`/`scaleX`/`scaleY`/`opacity` — `useLayoutEffect` (not `useEffect`) so the transform is applied before the first paint (no one-frame flash at the resting layout). Close mirrors the same delta math via `gsap.to`, `onComplete` calling `onClose()` + unmounting. Hero column (portrait or initial fallback, name, type badge, reveal/hide via `entry.tokenId` + `onToggleVisibility`, bench/deploy, remove) is complete. A `✎ RENAME` control button toggles an inline input (autofocus, save on Enter/blur, discard on Escape) over the name header, seeded from `entity.nickname`; saving patches `nickname` onto the matching `AdversaryInstance`/`VehicleInstance` in `encounter.adversaries`/`.vehicles` via `saveEncounter`, then — if the entity has an on-map token — calls the new `renameToken(id, label)` prop so the on-map token's label updates too. Clearing the input (blank) removes the override. Purely cosmetic — `entity.name` itself, and everywhere else that reads it, is untouched. `renameToken` (`src/hooks/useMapTokens.ts`) is a new mutator mirroring `updateWoundPct`'s exact shape — optimistic `setTokens` local update *before* the `supabase.from('map_tokens').update({ label })` write — so the map's own token label updates instantly rather than waiting on a Realtime round-trip (the original implementation did a raw one-off `supabase` write with no local state update, which only ever reflected after a reload/re-fetch). Threaded exactly like `updateTokenWoundPct`: `GmShell.tsx` destructures `renameToken: stagingRenameToken` from its own `useMapTokens(activeMap?.id)` instance (the same one backing `stagingTokens`/`stagingUpdateWoundPct`) and passes it into `GmMapView` as `renameToken`, which forwards it straight to `EncounterDossier` (added to that render's existing truthy-prop gate alongside `updateTokenWoundPct`). `EncounterDeck`'s own token mutators are untouched — this prop only exists on the dossier, since renaming only happens there. Stats column is complete: instantiates its own `useEncounterCombatControls({ encounter, setEncounter, saveEncounter, supabase, campaignId, tokens, updateTokenWoundPct, markPending, clearPending })` call (a second call site alongside `EncounterDeck.tsx`'s — both share the same lifted `encounter`/`setEncounter` from `useGmSession`, so external sync stays correct; each hook instance owns its own `debounceTimers` map, so the accepted residual risk is two rapid clicks for the *same* instance+stat landing in the same 350ms debounce window across *both* surfaces at once — realistically unreachable since the dossier is a modal overlay in front of the deck rail). Renders: a 6-box Characteristics grid (`adv.characteristics`); a Group Size stepper (minions only) calling `adjustGroupSize`, reading `adv.groupRemaining` directly (already the live alive-count, not re-derived); a Vitals row of two `VitalStepper`s (Wounds/Hull Trauma always; Strain/Sys Strain for nemesis-with-`strainThreshold`/vehicles, else a static "AS WOUNDS" placeholder) calling `adjustAdversaryWounds`/`adjustAdversaryStrain`/`adjustHullTrauma`/`adjustSystemStrain`; a Defenses/Vehicle Profile `Derived`-stat row (Soak/M Def/R Def, or Silhouette/Speed/Handling/Armor); a Weapons/Armament list normalizing `AdversaryWeapon` (`w.name`/`w.damage`/`w.crit` — **not** `w.critical`, confirmed against `src/lib/adversaries.ts`) and `VehicleWeapon` (no direct name/damage/crit fields — resolved via `vehicleWeaponStats(w.weaponKey)`/`vehicleWeaponDisplayName(w.weaponKey)` from `src/lib/vehicles.ts`, the same helpers `VehicleDetailPanel.tsx` uses) into one shared row shape, each with an `⌖ ATTACK` button that now drives `EncounterDossier`'s own `const [attackWeaponSignal, setAttackWeaponSignal] = useState<number | null>(null)` (bumped with the clicked weapon's index) in addition to still calling the optional `onAttackWeapon?: (weaponIndex: number) => void` prop (no-op if unset; Task 6 wires full weapon pre-selection); an Abilities list (`adv.abilities`/`veh.abilities ?? []`). Check-console column mounts `CheckConsole` (see below) in place of the former `#dossier-check-slot` placeholder, passed `entry`/`campaignId`/`roster`/`attackWeaponSignal` — `roster` is the same array this component already builds via `buildRoster` above, threaded down so `CheckConsole`'s vehicle Combat Check tab can populate its crew picker (Prompt 10); `CombatCheckOverlay` is no longer reachable from this file at all (Prompt 9 replaced the weapon+target hand-off entirely with an inline roller — see `CheckConsole` below), so there is no `onOpenCombatCheck` prop or combat-check state owned here anymore. `SHADOW.dossier` (`src/lib/tokens.ts`, backed by `--shadow-dossier` in `src/styles/holo-tokens.css`) and `.dossier-ctl-btn`/`.dossier-ctl-danger`/`.dossier-sec-label`/`.dossier-step-btn`/`.dossier-attack-btn` hover/focus CSS classes (`src/app/globals.css`) were added for the dossier shell in an earlier task.
- `CheckConsole` (`src/components/gm/CheckConsole.tsx`) — the check-console column mounted by `EncounterDossier`. Closes the real functional gap this whole feature exists for: a GM previously had no way to roll a bare skill check for an adversary (only weapon-based combat checks via `CombatCheckOverlay`). Two internal tabs (`'skill' | 'combat'`, local `useState`): **Skill Check** and **Combat Check**, both inline rollers (Prompt 9 replaced the earlier weapon+target-picker-only Combat tab that handed off to `CombatCheckOverlay` — that overlay is now untouched/unused from this file; `GmMapView` no longer mounts it from the dossier, though `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` still uses it for PC checks). Props: `CheckConsoleProps { entry: RosterEntry, campaignId: string, roster: RosterEntry[], initialTab?: 'skill' | 'combat', attackWeaponSignal?: number | null, onRollLogged?: () => void }`. Skill tab: fetches `ref_skills` (`key, name, characteristic_key`) once via a local `useRefSkills()` hook, lists every skill with a live-computed pool preview (`computeSkillPool`: `CHAR_FIELD_MAP[skill.characteristic_key]` from `src/lib/combatCheckUtils.ts` → `adv.characteristics[field]`, rank via `getAdversarySkillRank(adv, skill.name)` from `src/lib/adversaryAdapter.ts` → `getSkillPool(charVal, rank)` from `src/components/player-hud/dice-engine.ts`, the single source of pool-math truth). Clicking a skill row pre-fills the freely-adjustable pool tray (6 steppers, one per `DiceType` from `@/lib/tokens` excluding `force`); rolling calls `rollPool` (`dice-engine.ts`) directly and `logRoll` (`src/lib/logRoll.ts`) with `characterId: null`/`isDM: true`/`meta: { rollType: 'skill', alignment: entry.alignment }`. Vehicles have no `characteristics`/`skillRanks`, so the Skill tab doesn't apply to them — button doesn't render and tab state defaults/resets to `'combat'` whenever `entry` has no adversary entity (a `useEffect` keyed on `entry.instanceId`, not during render). A separate `useEffect` on `attackWeaponSignal` force-switches to Combat and pre-selects that weapon index when the dossier's weapons-list ATTACK button fires. `.cc-tab`/`.cc-skill-item`/`.cc-dstep`/`.cc-roll-btn` hover/focus CSS classes live in `src/app/globals.css` alongside the `.dossier-*` classes.

  **Combat tab**: adversaries get every real weapon plus a synthetic `Unarmed` entry (index `-1`, AoE Core p.224: Brawl, damage = Brawn, Engaged, Crit 5, Disorient 1 + Knockdown) appended last; selecting a weapon prefills the pool from the adversary's own rank in that weapon's governing skill (`computeWeaponPool`, keyed by `weaponSkillKey(w)` from `adversaryAdapter.ts` — reads `adv.skillRanks`/`adv.characteristics` directly rather than via `ref_skills.name`, since `AdversaryInstance.skillRanks` is keyed in the colon-separated form `ref_skills.name` doesn't match). No target picker or damage automation — the GM enters difficulty manually and applies wounds by hand; the roll only surfaces reference info (`DMG {d} · CRIT {c} · {range} · {qualities}`) baked into the `roll_label`, via `rollType: 'skill'` (not `'combat'`) so `RollFeedPanel`'s skill card renders that label as-is instead of computing an automated damage total. **Vehicles (Prompt 10)**: no synthetic Unarmed (vehicles have no Brawn/Brawl). A vehicle has no skills of its own, so firing a weapon requires a crew member: selecting a vehicle weapon (governing skill from `vehicleWeaponSkillKey(w.weaponKey)`, `src/lib/vehicles.ts` — defaults to `'GUNN'`, since every current entry and RAW itself route weapon fire through Gunnery, but the field is per-weapon so a future Piloting-governed entry needs no code change) reveals a **Select Crew** list of every non-vehicle `RosterEntry` (`roster.filter(r => r.kind === 'adversary')` — enemy adversaries and friendly NPCs alike, hidden ones included, other vehicles excluded), rendered with the same `.cc-skill-item` row styling as the weapon/skill lists (no new component). Choosing a crew member prefills the pool from that crew's rank in the weapon's skill via the same `computeWeaponPool`, which already handles minion-derived rank (`getAdversarySkillRank`) since crew entries are plain `AdversaryInstance`s. Roll stays disabled until both a weapon and a crew member are chosen; the crew choice is ephemeral — reset on every weapon (re)selection, entity switch, and completed roll — and never appears in the roll feed, only the vehicle + weapon reference info. An empty crew list shows an inline "No crew available — add a crew NPC to the encounter." message instead of a blank list.

### Tier 3 — Atoms & Utilities
- `ThemeInit` (`src/components/ThemeInit.tsx`) — client component (marked 'use client'); calls `initTheme()` on mount; returns null; rendered as first child of `<body>` in root layout to initialize theme on every page load
- `ThemeSwitcher` (`src/components/player-hud/ThemeSwitcher.tsx`) — HUD theme switcher component; exports `UiTheme` type; renders three theme color swatches (Binary Sunset, Rebel Operative, Kyber Archive); accepts `current` theme and `onChange` callback; fully controlled via props
- `RollFeedPanel` (`src/components/player-hud/RollFeedPanel.tsx`) — **Approach A feed layout** with state `expandedIds: Set<string>` for expand/collapse UI. Props: `rolls: RollEntry[]`, `ownCharacterId: string`, `isGm?: boolean`. Layout: top 2 most-recent skill/combat/force rolls render as **Design B cards** (tinted header band with alignment colour at 7% opacity + 15% border, character name, roll type, relative time; card body with large outcome word, result symbols, type-specific extras like damage calc or force pips, and dice pips); older rolls collapse to **compact single-line rows** (4px accent dot, character name, type label, outcome abbr, relative time); clicking a collapsed row expands it as a full card in-place; clicking the header band of an expanded history card collapses it (always-expanded top-2 cards have no collapse affordance). Initiative rolls always render as **compact non-expandable notifications** (one-liner with group count if multiple; grouped within 30-second window). System entries are **compact rows** (gear icon + message or award label); long system messages (>60 chars) get an expand toggle. Players never see hidden rolls; GMs see everything.
- `Modal` (`src/components/ui/Modal.tsx`) — shared portal modal: dark backdrop + blur, ESC key, click-outside dismiss, panel with HUD tokens. Props: `open`, `onClose?`, `maxWidth`, `zIndex`, `borderColor`, `shadow`, `backdrop`, `panelBackground`, `fullScreen`. `fullScreen` (default `false`, so every pre-existing caller is untouched) drops the scrim padding and renders the panel edge-to-edge with no max-width, radius or shadow — added for `InitiativeSetupModal`'s full-screen GM panel; pair it with an explicit `zIndex` when the host route has chrome above `Z.modal`.
  - **Animation hooks (all optional, all default-off).** `scrimRef` / `scrimClassName` expose the scrim so a caller can animate its opacity; `portalExtra` renders a node inside the portal as a sibling of the panel and above the scrim (this is where the ignition pulse lives, rather than being appended to `document.body` on every open); `exitMs` defers **unmount** by N ms when `open` flips false so a close animation can play. `exitMs` never defers the caller's `onClose` — that has already fired to flip `open`. With no new props the render guard collapses to `shouldRender = open`, evaluated in the same render, so the ~23 existing call sites unmount synchronously exactly as before — verified at runtime against a plain caller (gone on the next frame after Escape).
- `HudNotificationsDrawer` (`src/components/player-hud/HudNotificationsDrawer.tsx`) — the player-facing surface for `pending_actions`. Exports `HudNotificationsDrawer` and `useBlockingAutoOpen`. **`'vendor_offer'` wired (The Archive Market Prompt 3)**, following `'initiative'`'s exact render-prop shape: an optional `renderVendorOfferBody?: (a: PendingAction) => React.ReactNode` prop threaded through `HudNotificationsDrawer` → `ActionCard` → `CardBody`, supplied by `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` as a small "Open Storefront" button that just flips local `storefrontOpen` state — it never calls `resolve()` on the row. There is no "dismissed" status in the `pending_actions` schema (only `pending|resolved|cancelled`), and resolving would drop the row permanently, making it unrecallable — so dismissing the storefront is pure client-side UI state, and the notification card (still `status='pending'`) stays in the drawer as the recall affordance regardless of whether the storefront is currently open. The row only ever leaves `pending` when the GM closes the merchant (`cancelPendingActionsByType` in `GmMarketPanel`'s Open-to-Players toggle, see `useMarketMerchant` hook entry above).
  - **Alerts button** lives in `HudTopBar.tsx`, in the slot Print Sheet vacated (between the XP/Credits block and the theme switcher, one divider either side). Three tiers driven by `usePendingActions`: zero pending → `disabled`, dimmed, no badge, no click handler; pending with none blocking → `.is-active` (accent border + wash, count badge); any blocking → `.is-blocking` plus `.is-pulsing`. The pulse is a `::after` pseudo-element at `inset: -1px`, so it can never affect the top bar's height — measured at 39.94px in both the inert and pulsing states. `.is-pulsing` is gated on `usePrefersReducedMotion` in the component *and* by a `prefers-reduced-motion` media query in `globals.css`; reduced motion keeps the brighter static treatment. Labelled `Alerts`, not `Notifications` — the longer word does not fit the bar.
  - **Drawer is a grid sibling**, not an overlay: `PlayerHUDDesktop`'s main grid gained a row (`gridTemplateRows: 'auto auto auto 1fr'`) and the drawer sits at `gridColumn: '1 / -1'` between the top bar and the status strip, so opening it pushes content down. No portal, no `position: fixed`, and **no inline `position` on the drawer wrapper** — an inline value wins specificity and silently breaks both the spring and overlay containment.
  - **Height-spring** (`SpringBox`) follows `TalentsPanel.tsx`'s `AnimatedDrawer`, the codebase's only precedent: measure, animate `0 → height`, release to `height: auto` on complete. Used for both the drawer and each card body. `usePrefersReducedMotion` is in the effect's dependency array (it returns `false` on first render and flips in an effect, so capturing it in a closure would strand the reduced-motion path).
  - **Dual-host initiative (`InitiativeRollModal.tsx`).** The roll UI is one component rendered in two hosts, never two implementations — popups still fire alongside the queue, which is exactly why they must not be allowed to drift.
    - `InitiativeRollBody` (exported) owns every mechanic: skill selection and the `type !== 'stCombat'` filter, pool derivation, the roll, Force dice and the pips→advantage spend (`totalAdvantages = baseAdv + pipsSpent * 3`), the `initiative-result` broadcast, the `roll_log` insert, and the pending-row resolution. It assumes no modal chrome: no portal, no `position: fixed`, no inline `position`.
    - `InitiativeRollModal` (exported) is a thin wrapper — `Modal` shell plus close behaviour, zero roll logic. **The 1400ms self-close lives here only**; the inline host has no close target and fires no timer.
    - The body takes `onSubmitted?` (completion) rather than `onClose`, and renders its Cancel affordance only when given `onCancel`. The drawer card passes neither: resolution removes the card over Realtime, and collapsing the card is its cancel.
    - **One `usePendingActions` instance, in `PlayerHUDDesktop`.** `initiativePendingRow` and `resolve` are derived there and passed to both hosts (the drawer via `renderInitiativeBody`, the popup via `HudModalsOverlay`). This is not a style preference: `createClient` (`@supabase/ssr`'s `createBrowserClient`) is a singleton, so a second hook instance inside the body opened a **second Realtime channel on the same topic** (`pending-actions-<id>`), the topics collided, and the host's subscription silently stopped delivering — the drawer card never cleared after a resolve. Caught in the running app, fixed by passing the row down.
    - **Double-resolve guarded** three ways: a promise-chain queue plus a resolved-id `Set` inside the body (React state is not a synchronous mutex), the row leaving `pendingActions` over Realtime the moment the other host resolves it, and `resolve`'s own `.eq('status','pending')`.
    - The popup closes itself if its row is resolved elsewhere, gated on having actually seen a row first so a legacy broadcast-only request is not closed the instant it opens. Backdrop click and Escape still dismiss silently — but that **no longer loses the request**: the row survives and the card stays in the drawer. That is the guarantee the queue exists to provide.
  - **Card contract.** One card per row: glyph, title, one-line description derived from `payload`, a `Pending`/`Blocking` tag, and a chevron that rotates via `.pa-card.is-open`. Blocking cards add an accent border and a 3px accent left rail (`.pa-card-row::before`). Card bodies expand inline. Initiative renders the shared `InitiativeRollBody` inline (see the dual-host entry above) — the host supplies it through a `renderInitiativeBody` prop, so this file stays presentation-only. The earlier read-only summary and its `Open Roll` handoff were deleted, not left beside the new path. Every other `action_type` renders an honest placeholder line; no simulated resolve controls ship. `TYPE_META` carries all eight action types so the later prompt has the shape to fill.
  - **Blocking auto-open** (`useBlockingAutoOpen`) fires once per action id, tracked in a `Set` ref, so it never re-opens on re-render or after a deliberate close. It is **not a lock** — the rest of the HUD stays usable, since resolving a blocking action often means checking a talent or a threshold first. No enforced resolution order.
  - Drawer auto-closes ~600ms after the list empties. Realtime drives the list, so a row resolved on another surface removes itself and the badge count falls without a refresh.
- `NumberField` (`src/components/ui/NumberField.tsx`) — `<input type="number">` with the native browser spinner suppressed and an on-brand stacked ▴/▾ chevron pair in its place. A drop-in for a bare number input: every prop (`style`, `className`, `value`, `min`, `max`, `step`, `onChange`, `data-*`) passes straight through, the caller's `style` lands on the input verbatim, and the chevron column is absolutely positioned **inside** the field in the spot the native spinner occupied — so the wrapper measures exactly the input's width and converting a field causes zero reflow. Chevrons drive the input via its own `stepUp()`/`stepDown()` (browser enforces min/max/step) and then dispatch a native `input` event, which is what React's `onChange` listens for — the caller's existing handler runs unmodified, including any clamping of its own, so value handling and validation stay entirely with the call site. Chevrons are `tabIndex={-1}` and disable at the bounds (affordance only); native keyboard arrow stepping and free typed entry are untouched. Hover/active/disabled and the spinner suppression (`::-webkit-outer-spin-button`/`::-webkit-inner-spin-button` + `-moz-appearance: textfield`) live in `globals.css` under `.numfield*`. **The sweep is complete** — every `<input type="number">` in `src/` now goes through `NumberField`; a bare one is a regression. 49 fields across 17 files: `InitiativeSetupModal`, `DestinyRollModal`, `GmShell`, `MapToolsRadial`, `GmToolsPanel`, `AdversaryEditor`, `DutyObligationTab`, `GmLootModal`, `GmMapView`, `GmReferenceDrawer`, `ItemDatabaseTab`, `ItemEditor`, `LootAwardModal`, `VehicleEditor`, `VendorSellModal`, `GroupSheet`, `HudSpendCreditsModal`. `±` steppers built from buttons around a plain value display (`GmCharacterCard`, `VitalsCard`, `EncounterDeck` wounds, `GroupStorageModal` qty, `GmDiceRollerFAB` pools, etc.) are a separate, already-custom pattern and are deliberately out of scope — they are not `type="number"` inputs at all.
- `ItemDetailPopup` (`src/components/shared/ItemDetailPopup.tsx`, The Archive Market Fix Pass 2) — item inspect popup: icon (with override control via `IconPicker`), price/rarity/encumbrance, type-specific stats (weapon: Skill/Damage/Crit/Range/HP; armor: Soak bonus/Defense; gear+armor: encumbrance-threshold-bonus), weapon qualities, description. Read/inspect + icon-override only — no award, no inventory action, no credit/stock mutation of any kind (never had one to extract). Extracted as a pure move from `ItemDatabaseTab.tsx`'s previously-inline popup (screenshot-verified byte-identical before/after — same rendered output, same position, same behavior); the only other GM surface using it is `GmMarketPanel`'s stock-row `View` action (see above) — grep-verified exactly one `ItemDetailPopup` component in `src/`, no per-surface fork. Props: `item: EditableItem` (from `ItemEditor.tsx`, already the shared item-shape type) plus `onClose`, `resolveIcon` (required — `catalogEntries`/`pickerOpen`/`setPickerOpen`/`pickerBusy`/`onPickIcon`/`onResetIcon` all optional as of The Archive Market Prompt 3), `refQualityMap` — every write (the icon-override upsert/delete) stays owned by the caller, not this component, so each host's own `campaignId`/`supabase` wiring is unambiguous. **The icon-override control (the "Icon" button + `IconPicker`) is GM-only, gated by whether the caller supplies those five optional props** — omit all five together and the popup renders read-only: `resolveIcon` still shows whichever icon (a GM's pinned override included) is already resolved, it just can't be changed from here. `MarketStorefront.tsx` (player Market storefront, Prompt 3) is the first caller that omits them — the player's own `useItemIconContext` instance still supplies `resolveIcon` so a GM's override is visible, but no `IconPicker` trigger ever renders for a player. **Always portals to `document.body`** (`createPortal`) rather than rendering in-tree: both hosts mount inside `GmShell`'s sliding-panel wrapper, which carries an inline `transform` (translateX) at all times — per CSS, that makes the wrapper the containing block for any non-portaled `position:fixed` descendant, trapping it inside the panel's bounds instead of the real viewport. This was a latent bug in the original inline popup (never visibly wrong only because `GmToolsPanel`, its one prior host, happens to be narrow enough that the offset wasn't obvious) fixed once here rather than inherited into a second surface. `TYPE_COLOR`/`WEAPON_SKILL_NAME` and the tiny `actionBtn` style helper are local duplicates of `ItemDatabaseTab`'s own copies (a private CSS-object utility, not shared business logic — not worth a cross-file dependency for 8 lines).
- `AddParticipantModal` (`src/components/combat/AddParticipantModal.tsx`) was **deleted** during the NumberField sweep. It was dead code — zero imports, zero references, mounted nowhere — orphaned when the Encounter Deck replaced the earlier overlapping encounter-building systems. `src/components/combat/` now holds only `CombatLog.tsx`.
- `InitiativeRollModal` (`src/components/player-hud/InitiativeRollModal.tsx`) — player-side initiative roll, opened by `usePlayerBroadcast`'s `initiative-request` handler via `HudModalsOverlay`. Shows the GM-requested skill and its pool, plus a **"use a different skill?"** affordance listing the character's non-combat skills (`hudSkills` filtered on `type !== 'stCombat'`; `hudSkills` is threaded `useCharacterData` → `PlayerHUDDesktop` → `HudModalsOverlay`, since `CharacterSkill` alone carries no name or linked characteristic). **No approval gate** — picking a skill resets the roll state and the player rolls immediately with the new pool; the GM can always override the numbers afterwards. `requestedSkillKey` (from the GM's per-row assignment, carried on the request broadcast) overrides the Cool/Vigilance default. The `initiative-result` broadcast payload gained `skillKey`/`skillName`; the `roll_log` row labels with the skill actually used. Force-dice pip spending (+3 Adv each) is unchanged.
- `PlayerTokenTooltip` (`src/components/player/PlayerTokenTooltip.tsx`, Prompt 11, hover-reliability fixes in Prompts 13-14) — image-first, GSAP holographic-materialization tooltip for every token a player can currently see (PCs, and any revealed enemy/friendly NPC or vehicle); replaces the old Kyber-stat-block `TokenTooltip` copy that used to live in `HudSessionTab.tsx`. Props: `{ entity: PlayerTooltipEntity | null, tokenRect: DOMRect | null }`. Content is React state (drives what renders); position/flip/animation are direct ref writes across **two** `useLayoutEffect`s (split in Prompt 13 — originally one). A "decision" effect keyed on `[entity, tokenRect]` only decides show/hide/same-token and updates `displayEntity`/`sameTokenRef`; a separate "DOM" effect keyed on `[displayEntity, tokenRect]` does all `wrapRef.current` reads/writes (position math + GSAP). This split matters: doing both in one effect meant that on a *fresh* mount (tooltip had fully hidden and unmounted, then a new hover arrived) `setDisplayEntity(entity)` didn't synchronously attach `wrapRef.current` in that same pass — React hadn't committed the render yet — so positioning/animation silently no-op'd on `if (!wrap) return`, and the card never actually appeared. Confirmed live via Playwright (headless Chromium, campaign "Legacy of Rebellion") sampling `getComputedStyle` on the real DOM node over time: before the fix, opacity/clipPath/left/top sat permanently at their untouched JSX/CSS defaults after a cold hover; keying the DOM effect on `displayEntity` itself (which only fires after React commits the render where it went non-null) fixed it — confirmed via the same live sampling, plus 6/6 repeated identical hovers all landing on `opacity:1`. A second, smaller race also existed and is fixed: the same-token ("already shown, just reposition") branch didn't kill an in-flight *hide* timeline, so leaving-and-returning to a token fast enough could let a stale hide animation's later steps stomp the opacity back down after this branch set it — also confirmed via live repeated-hover sampling (alternating visible/invisible before the fix). `tl.kill()` cleanly cuts off an in-flight timeline whenever the pointer moves rapidly between tokens. Portaled to `document.body`, which — since `PlayerHUDDesktop`'s `data-theme` override lives on its own wrapper div, not `<html>`/`<body>` — means the card structurally sits outside any Kyber override the player has personally toggled and always renders `:root`'s Ember Tatooine defaults; no hardcoding needed to enforce "Ember Tatooine, not Kyber cyan." Role edge-line/fallback-initial colour comes from `COLOR.red`/`COLOR.green`/`HUD.accentPurple` — the same enemy/friendly/purple tokens already established by `EncounterDeck`'s `EntityCard` and `GmConflictPip`, not new values. Type tag (MINION/RIVAL/NEMESIS/VEHICLE, omitted for PCs) is positioned top-left with the same safe-inset convention as `EncounterDeck`'s roster-card role tag (Prompt 6). Portrait is `MapToken.token_image_url` directly — the same shared identity image already drawn on the map disc/rectangle, dossier, and roster card, no separate image system. Wired from `HudSessionTab.tsx`'s `tooltipEntity` memo (mirrors the old `tooltipProps` memo's PC/adversary/vehicle/minion branch structure, trimmed to wounds/strain/minionPips only — no soak/defense/characteristics). Positioning needs the hovered token's true screen rect, not just a cursor point (tokens are Pixi sprites, not DOM nodes) — `MapCanvas.tsx`'s `onTokenHover` callback gained a 4th `tokenRect: DOMRect` argument (via the token's Pixi `container.getBounds()` translated into page coordinates), additive and non-breaking for the GM's own `onTokenHover` consumer which ignores it. Each token's Pixi container also gets an explicit `hitArea` (`buildTokenSprite`'s `applyLabelScale`, computed from `container.getLocalBounds()` + a few px of jitter padding, spanning the disc/rect through the label pill below it) — Pixi's default per-child hit-testing left real gaps (the ring graphic is stroke-only, so its own `containsPoint` was a thin band, not the disc interior); a single container-level `hitArea` makes the whole visual footprint one solid hit region, confirmed correct/solid via the `DEV_DEBUG_HOVER` overlay (see below) before the actual Prompt 13 bug (the React ref-timing race above) was found — the hitArea was never actually the problem, but the fix is still correct/warranted and stayed in. `MapCanvas.tsx` has a `DEV_DEBUG_HOVER` const (default `false`) that, when flipped on locally, draws each token's live `hitArea` as a magenta translucent overlay and console.logs a coordinate-mapping bundle on every `pointerover` — kept as permanent (off-by-default) diagnostic tooling for any future hover issues. **Prompt 14**: `MapCanvas.tsx`'s per-token `hoverOutDebounce` (a 60ms `setTimeout` before calling `onHoverEndRef`, absorbing a real Pixi quirk where crossing between a token's own child graphics can fire a spurious pointerout/pointerover pair) is a per-token-container closure — leaving token A starts A's own timer, and nothing cancels it if the pointer lands on a *different* token B within that window (only A's own re-entry cancels A's timer). `onTokenHoverEnd` therefore now carries the leaving token's id, and both consumers (`HudSessionTab.tsx`'s `tooltipEntity`-driving `tokenHoverInfo` state, and `GmMapView.tsx`'s `handleTokenHoverEnd`) only clear their hover state if it still matches that id (`prev?.tokenId === id ? null : prev`) — otherwise the callback is stale (superseded by B's hover already landing) and is a no-op. Without this, hopping quickly between two tokens showed no tooltip at all: B's tooltip would appear, then ~60ms later A's delayed hide fired and unconditionally nulled the shared hover state, wiping B's tooltip a moment after it appeared. Confirmed live via Playwright (rapid A→B `mouse.move`, no lingering pause, sampling `getComputedStyle` well past the 60ms window). New `.ptt-*` CSS classes (static geometry only — clip-path frame, corner brackets, notch, pips, scanline/scrim) live in `src/app/globals.css`; `SHADOW.tooltip`/`--shadow-tooltip` (`src/lib/tokens.ts`/`src/styles/holo-tokens.css`) backs the card's drop-shadow. **Prompt 14 (PC tooltip bug fix + enrichment)**: PC tokens showed "WOUNDS 0/1" with no strain row because `HudSessionTab.tsx`'s `allChars` fetch filtered on `.eq('is_active', true)` — the `characters` table has no `is_active` column (it's `is_archived`, default `false`) — so the query errored, `allChars` silently never updated past its `[character]` (own-character-only) seed, and any *other* PC's token failed the `tooltipEntity` memo's `allChars.find()` lookup, falling all the way through to the generic last-resort branch (hardcoded `wounds: { current: 0, max: 1 }`, no strain, no `role: 'pc'`/purple edge). Fixed to `.eq('is_archived', false)`; the PC branch's own `char.wound_threshold`/`char.strain_threshold` reads were already correct (same effective-value column `derivedStats.ts` treats as authoritative). Portrait crop fixed by adding `object-position: top center` to `.ptt-art-img` — PC portraits reuse `character.portrait_url` (a tall character-sheet hero image, same source as `CenterHero.tsx`/`CharacterLoader.tsx`/`TransitionCard.tsx`, all of which already crop it with `top center`), whereas NPC/vehicle token images come pre-cropped-square from `adversary_token_images`/vehicle token caches, so default center-crop only ever looked wrong for PCs. Enrichment: `PlayerTooltipEntity` gained an optional `subtitle` field (species + career, e.g. "Droid Bounty Hunter") rendered under `.ptt-name` — **reverted in Prompt 15**: stacked under the already-2-line-clamped name it read as an oversized, class/title-like text block dominating the card, which the mockup and Prompt 15's acceptance criteria explicitly rule out ("no class/title/lore text"). `subtitle` (type field, render, `.ptt-subtitle` CSS) and the `refCareers`/`refSpeciesAll` props threaded through `HudSessionTab.tsx`/`PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` to compute it were all removed; the card's identity block is name-only again. A critical-injury count pill was scoped for Prompt 14 but skipped: `character_critical_injuries` rows are discrete/countable (`is_healed` flag) but only the *current* player's own crits are loaded anywhere in the app (`useCharacterData.ts`) — showing a count for an arbitrary hovered PC would need a new campaign-wide fetch, so it was left as a future item per that prompt's explicit "don't add a new fetch" constraint. **Prompt 19 (defeated markers)**: `PlayerTooltipEntity` gained a required `defeated: boolean` field, computed in `HudSessionTab.tsx`'s `tooltipEntity` memo directly from the same live encounter fields already read for `wounds`/`minionPips` — rival/nemesis/vehicle: current ≥ threshold; minion group: `groupRemaining === 0` (whole squad wiped, not per-member); always `false` for PCs and for any branch with no live encounter data (pre-combat fallback stats). No new column — purely derived at render time, so healing an entity back below threshold clears the marker automatically. When `defeated`, the tooltip applies `.ptt-dead` (desaturate+dim the portrait, mute the name, dim the wound bar), shows the shared `SkullGlyph` (`src/components/shared/SkullGlyph.tsx`) as a full watermark over the portrait plus a "☠ DEFEATED" tag top-right (opposite the existing top-left type tag), with a GSAP scale-in+fade flash (`skullRef`, ~380ms) that plays only on the live alive→defeated crossing for the currently-shown token (tracked via `prevDefeatedRef`), not on every reposition or on a fresh hover of an already-defeated token. Minion squad pips (Part D escalation): each individually-dead pip gets a micro `SkullGlyph` stamp while at least one minion survives; once the whole group is wiped (`e.defeated`), per-pip skulls disappear (pips fall back to plain dimmed) and the one big watermark takes over instead — implemented as a `showPipSkull = !alive && !e.defeated` guard per pip. The Pixi map-token layer (`MapCanvas.tsx`'s `buildTokenSprite`) mirrors the exact same treatment independently from `token.wound_pct` (an existing, already-derived field — `wound_pct >= 1` on an adversary/vehicle token, since `useEncounterCombatControls.ts` already clamps it to 1 exactly when wounds/hull-trauma reach threshold): grayscale+dim `ColorMatrixFilter` on the art sprite, a dimmed/softened wound arc and ring, and the same shared skull glyph rasterized into a Pixi texture (`loadSkullTexture`, cached module-scope, built from `skullSvgMarkup()` in `src/lib/skullGlyph.ts` — the single geometry source both the DOM `SkullGlyph.tsx` and this Pixi loader read from, so the two renderers can't visually disagree) plus a flat tinted-black duplicate sprite as a cheap drop-shadow (no `@pixi/filter-drop-shadow` dependency). Exposed per-token as `container.__applyWoundState(woundPct, animate)`, called once at build time (no animation) and again from both of `syncTokens`'/`syncTokensCb`'s existing token-update paths whenever `wound_pct` changes (animate the GSAP flash only on a genuine false→true transition, guarded by a `lastDefeated` closure var) — this is also what makes the arc/skull live-update at all: the prior code only ever drew the wound arc once at sprite-construction time, silently going stale on any wound change that took `syncTokensCb`'s existing image/label-only "fast path" (which never rebuilds the sprite). One real bug caught live via Playwright before shipping: the skull/shadow sprites start on `Texture.EMPTY` (0×0) with no explicit width/height set — Sprite's width/height setters divide by the texture's `orig` size to derive scale, so setting them against a 0×0 texture produces an `Infinity` scale (confirmed rendering as a giant black blob covering much of the map); fixed by only ever sizing the sprites inside the `loadSkullTexture().then()` callback, once a real non-zero texture is assigned, and by giving `skullSvgMarkup()`'s root `<svg>` explicit `width="64" height="64"` attributes (an SVG with no intrinsic size rasterizes at the browser's 300×150 replaced-element default, not its `viewBox`) so the resulting texture's pixel size is a known, deterministic baseline for that scale math. Same shared component/data for GM and player views — `GmMapView.tsx`, `HudSessionTab.tsx`, and `src/app/table/page.tsx` (Table Display) all render the identical `MapCanvas` code path, so this required no isGM branching. Encounter Deck roster cards and `EncounterDossier.tsx` are explicitly out of scope and untouched.
- `QuartermasterModal` (`src/components/QuartermasterModal.tsx`) — player-facing buy/sell shop modal; two tabs: Buy (type-filter pills, search, item rows with stock badges + price, Buy button) and Sell (character inventory rows with offer at `sell_pct`%, Sell button); loads active character via `character_sessions` → `characters`; uses `useQuartermaster` for data + mutations. Props: `campaignId`, `characterName`, `supabase`, `onClose`. Rendered from `GroupSheet` when `showQm` is true.
- `TickerText` (`src/components/ui/TickerText.tsx`) — renders a scramble-ticker animation for a text string; wraps `useTicker`; outputs `.ticker-ready` span containing one `.ticker-char` span per character (unsettled chars get `opacity: 0.5`, `aria-hidden`); includes a `.sr-only` span with the full text for screen readers. Props: `text`, `isOpen`, `delayMs?`, `className?`.
- `RichText` — renders OggDude markup with icon font; accepts optional `style` prop. Every inline dice/symbol glyph gets a dark chip backing (`GLYPH_CHIP`, `box-shadow: 0 0 0 1.5px var(--plaque-body)` + `border-radius: 3px`) — the sw-rpg-icons font and `DiceFace`'s SVG fills were authored for a dark background; on a bright surface (the talent plaque E4 owned-plate, gold in Ember / cyan in Kyber) setback's near-black translucent fill vanished and only its pale outline remained, perceptually reading as boost instead of setback (confirmed live on Read The Wind). `box-shadow` was chosen deliberately over `background`/`padding`: it is purely presentational, paints outside the box without contributing to that box's dimensions, so it cannot change line-height or line-box height in any context (confirmed: identical `19.908px` computed line-height with and without glyphs present, and the 4-line clamp — see `TalentTree.module.css`'s `.body > span` above — still resolves to exactly 4 lines on all four named cases with the chip in place). It is also the only technique compatible with all 16 shortcode types uniformly, including the neutral Force pip, which already draws its own gradient via `background` + `background-clip: text` — a `background-color` chip would have collided with that. `--plaque-body` (near-black, per-theme) is reused rather than inventing a colour: on a normal dark surface it is literally the same value as the surrounding background, so the chip is invisible there by construction and only becomes visible where the background actually needs it — verified in both themes, both owned and dark (reachable/locked) plaque states, and on every surface RichText renders in (talent plaques, Force power tree, sig ability tree, dossier, Force check overlay's `.fc-fx-desc`), since the chip lives in this one shared component rather than being applied per-surface. `DICE_COLOR`/`SYM_COLOR` (tokens.ts) are untouched — this pass added zero diff to that file. **Found and fixed in the same pass, a second and independent legibility bug:** the six result-symbol icons (`success`/`failure`/`advantage`/`threat`/`triumph`/`despair`, via `.ffi-swrpg-*`) had no `color` of their own anywhere — `i.ffi` in globals.css sets no colour — so they rendered in whatever `color` the surrounding text had. On an owned plaque that is `--plaque-plate-text` at 88% alpha, i.e. very close to `--plaque-body`, the SAME colour as the new chip's own backing: the glyph was rendering almost invisibly on top of its own dark island (confirmed via screenshot: a success star was a barely-visible near-black silhouette). Fixed by applying the existing sealed `SYM_COLOR[key]` value explicitly on these six icons, stopping the inheritance — not by touching `SYM_COLOR` itself, the chip colour, or any other glyph. The two Force-pip variants (`light`/`dark`) and the neutral `fp` gradient already set their own colour explicitly and needed no change.
- `HandOverlay` (`src/components/player-hud/HandOverlay.tsx` + `.module.css`, H2) — player-facing "hand of cards": active talents (`activation != taPassive`) and owned Force power BASE nodes only, as floating cards over the HUD, no panel/background. Static fan layout + tuck/peek only — no drag/reorder/discard/ceremony-integration/check-glow/focus-view (all later prompts); geometry, card silhouette, hover-lift and tuck mechanics are ported 1:1 from the reviewed `talent-hand-v3.html` mockup (172x238 notched-corner card, arc fan via `off*(spread+46)` x-offset / `|off|*14` y-offset / `off*6.5°` rotation, hover GSAP `y:-64 scale:1.13`, tuck `translateY(196px)`). Mounted as a sibling in `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx`'s root, gated `!isGmMode` (isGmMode gating is at the call site, not inside the component — GM view shares the same `PlayerHUDDesktop` tree, per H0's finding that no structural GM/player mount split exists). **Z-index: `Z.popover` (420), set as an inline style from `@/lib/tokens` in `HandOverlay.tsx` (not the CSS module — CSS modules can't reference `tokens.ts`, so the value lives in one real place, not a hand-copied literal).** H2 originally shipped `Z.overlay` (100), "below modals" — corrected here: the hand must stay visible AND interactive DURING a check, since H5's check-time glow and H7's "Play Power" both depend on reaching it while a check is open. Real z-index inventory, confirmed by reading source rather than assumed from names: `ForceCheckOverlay`/`CombatCheckOverlay` both render through the shared `Modal` shell with no override, i.e. `Z.modal` (410); `SkillRollPopover` sets `Z.overlay` (100) directly (`SkillRollPopover.tsx:284`) — the three check surfaces are NOT all on the same tier. `Z.popover` (420) clears the highest of the three with a 10-unit margin and sits below the `Z.fab` (500) tier, where two *real* blocking decision dialogs render — `VendorPurchaseDialog.tsx:139` and `BuySpecButton.tsx:65`'s spec picker, both full-screen backdrop + centred panel, both something the player must act on — confirmed by reading their JSX before picking a value, not assumed. Further below `Z.toast`/`Z.dialog`/`Z.tooltip`, so an error toast or blocking confirmation is never covered; sonner's toaster hardcodes `z-index: 999999999` internally regardless. One deliberate exact tie: the conflict-notification `Modal` in `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` renders at `Z.modal + 10` = 420, same as the hand. Safe and deterministic, not coincidental — that `Modal` portals to `document.body`, appended after `HandOverlay` in the DOM, so at equal z-index it always paints on top (CSS stacking-order ties resolve by DOM order). Live-verified against all three real check overlays: Force Check (`fc-scrim-ignite` z=410) and Combat Check (same) both render *behind* the hand, hand cards visibly on top of the backdrop with working hover-lift (`transform` responds to a real hover event mid-check); Skill Check (`SkillRollPopover`, z=100) same. Scoped to this component only — the wider `Z.*`/`--z-*` token-system drift stays out of scope. Source spec is read from the **raw `CharacterTalent[]` array** (`talents`, not `hudTalents`, which collapses multi-spec ownership — H0's finding); a talent owned via more than one spec shows the first owning spec found in that array, a stated simplification. Force base-node identification reuses `ForcePowerDisplay.basicAbilityKey` (already resolved in `useForcePowers.ts` from the earlier Force base-power-detection fix) rather than re-deriving via `buildForceCellKeyMap` — upgrade nodes never appear as cards. Rank pips use the real rebel-starbird raster asset (`public/images/factions/rebel.png`) as a CSS mask via the theme-agnostic `--rebel-mask` custom property (`src/styles/state-tokens.css`), tinted `--faction-rebel-red` (also theme-agnostic — faction identity, not theme decoration) — replaced the earlier hand-drawn SVG-path approximation (`--phx-mask`, removed) once the real asset shipped. `TalentFocusView.module.css`'s and `ForcePowerFocusView.module.css`'s own rank/node-pip rules were separate copies that had drifted (`TalentFocusView` hardcoded `--hud-gold` instead of the faction token, and its `.ranks` container had no `justify-content`, rendering bottom-left instead of matching the hand fan's bottom-right) — both corrected to the shared `--rebel-mask`/`--faction-rebel-red` pair with `justify-content: flex-end`. `ForcePowerFocusView`'s upgrade-lattice node pips are a Force-power concept, not a faction rank pip, and were also drawn from the phoenix mask by mistake — switched to `--jedi-mask` (`public/images/factions/jedi.webp`), keeping their existing `--hud-accent-purple` tint. Live-verified via computed style/screenshot in both themes (identical faction-red across Ember/Kyber) since no character currently owns rank 2+ on an active ranked talent to show natively. **Tuck state is local-only in H2** — seeded from `character_hand_state.is_tucked` on mount (`useHandState.ts`, read-only, treats a missing row as `false`, matching H1's schema intent) but the toggle never writes back; persistence is explicitly deferred (a later prompt wires the write). **Bug found and fixed during build, not in the mockup**: `.cardMeta`'s `justify-content: space-between` let a long activation label (`Incidental (OOT)`) and a long spec name (`Nightsister`) render edge-to-edge with zero gap at the card's 172px width — live-measured (`typeRect.right === specRect.left`) before `flex-wrap` was added so the spec label drops to its own line instead of overlapping. RichText's glyph chips (see that entry above) work unchanged at card scale — confirmed via computed style, same `box-shadow` mechanism, no font-size dependency. Empty state (zero active talents AND zero owned Force base powers): renders nothing — no discard pile or passive-deck view exists yet in H2 to give an empty fan context, so an empty fan with only a tuck control would be confusing chrome with nothing to explain it; revisit once H3's deck/discard piles exist alongside it. Verified against Bylethia Ford's real live data: 9 cards (5 active talents — including one campaign-custom GM-authored talent, `dataset_source='oggdude', is_custom=true`, which correctly appears since `refTalentMap` isn't dataset-filtered — plus 4 owned Force base powers: Commune/Heal-Harm/Influence/Sense). Mobile untouched — no mobile file appears anywhere in this component's diff.
  **H3 — passive deck viewer + discard pile with drag.** The card builder was split into `buildTalentCard` (shared by active AND passive talents — one implementation, never a second renderer) plus a still-separate `forceCards` builder (Force powers were never filterable by activation, so nothing to share there). `<CardFace>` and `<CardGridOverlay>` were extracted as internal sub-components, reused by the fan, the passive-deck grid, and the discard grid — `onReturn`'s presence/absence is the only behavioural difference between the two grid uses. **Card-key disambiguation was already resolved by H2**, confirmed rather than re-decided: talent keys are unprefixed (`species_`-prefixed only for species-rank grants), Force cards are `force_<powerKey>` — that scheme already round-trips cleanly through `discarded_keys`, no new tagging needed.
  **Write pattern for `discarded_keys` (`useHandState.ts`), proven race-safe, not assumed:** the promise-chain-queue pattern already established in `InitiativeRollModal.tsx`'s `resolvePendingAction` (`queueRef.current = queueRef.current.then(...)`) — reused, not reinvented. The queue alone isn't sufficient for a full-array-replace column, since it only serializes *when* writes run, not *what* they write: paired with a `discardedRef` that every handler updates synchronously (optimistic) *before* enqueueing, so a queued write always reads the ref's value **at write time**, not a stale closed-over snapshot. A write superseded by a later one before it runs still persists correctly, because by the time it executes the ref already reflects every change queued ahead of it. Live-proven: two cards dragged to the pile back-to-back with no settle wait between them, then a **direct SQL read** (not local state) — `discarded_keys: ["SUMMONITEM","SENSEADV"]`, both present. Writes go through `.upsert(..., { onConflict: 'character_id' })` since H1 defined a character with no row as valid default state — the first-ever discard creates the row.
  **Persistence verified via a real reload**, not local state: discarded a card, reloaded the page fresh, fan correctly excluded it and the pile count/grid reflected it; returned it via the discard grid's "return to hand," reloaded again, confirmed it stayed returned. `card_order` and `is_tucked` were confirmed untouched by every discard write (`card_order: []`, `is_tucked: false` throughout) — H3's scope stayed exactly at `discarded_keys`, no incidental expansion.

  **Passive-in-hand (migration 120, `hand_placed_keys`).** `useHandState.ts` gained a fourth persisted column, `hand_placed_keys`, plus `placeCard`/`unplaceCard` — the identical `handPlacedRef`-then-enqueue commit shape as `discardedRef`/`commitDiscardedKeys`, not a new pattern.
  **Drag mechanics: native pointer events, not dnd-kit** — a deliberate choice, not a default. This project's one existing dnd-kit usage (`InitiativeSetupModal.tsx`) is exclusively `useSortable`/`SortableContext`, i.e. reordering within/between lists; discard-to-pile is a different shape entirely — one card dragged onto ONE static drop target, never reordered against siblings. dnd-kit's sortable machinery (collision strategy, `SortableContext`, `DragOverlay`) solves a problem this interaction doesn't have. The reviewed mockup's own ~40-line `pointerdown`/`pointermove`/`pointerup` + `setPointerCapture` + rect hit-test does exactly what's needed and is what was reviewed/approved — ported directly, with a 6px movement threshold disambiguating click from drag (a tap with no movement does nothing yet; H6 wires click-to-focus for Force cards on top of the same handler).
  **Two real bugs caught by live screenshot, not spec-written**: (1) `.gridPanel` had no `min-width`, so `justify-content:center` on the grid had no container width to center columns within and it collapsed to a single 172px column regardless of viewport size — fixed with `min-width: min(600px, 90vw)`. (2) `.gridTitle` and `.gridClose` were both absolutely positioned relative to that same collapsed-width panel and visually overlapped — fixed by reserving `.gridClose`'s width as right-padding on the centered title, independent of panel width.
  Empty states: passive deck with 0 owned passives and discard pile with 0 discards both open to an inert "No … owned/discarded" message rather than a blank grid — the discard pile trigger itself always renders regardless of count (a live drop target even at 0), per spec. GM mode confirmed absent (0 cards, 0 deck/discard triggers). Both themes confirmed via screenshot (Kyber's cyan/indigo family recolors the deck-trigger/grid chrome identically to the fan). Mobile untouched.
- `EquipStateButtons` — equipped/carrying/stowed toggle
- `DicePoolDisplay` — characteristic + skill rank → die faces
- `DestinyPoolDisplay` — light/dark pip tracker
- `StowPill` — colored badge for stow location
- `StowLocationModal` — portal modal for picking stow destination
- `SpecSelectorList` — specialization selector with talent tree preview
- `SkullGlyph` (`src/components/shared/SkullGlyph.tsx`, Prompt 19) — DOM renderer for the "defeated" skull glyph, reading path/ellipse geometry from `src/lib/skullGlyph.ts` (the single shared geometry source — `MapCanvas.tsx`'s Pixi layer rasterizes the same data via `skullSvgMarkup()` into a texture rather than reusing this component directly, since Pixi renders to canvas, not DOM). `fill`/`hole` props default to `var(--hud-text)`/`var(--hud-bg)`; used at two scales by `PlayerTokenTooltip.tsx` — a full watermark over a defeated token/portrait, and a micro stamp (`fill="var(--hud-text-faint)"`) on an individual dead minion pip. See `PlayerTokenTooltip`'s entry above for the full defeated-marker feature.
- `ForcePowerSelectorList` (`src/components/shared/ForcePowerSelectorList.tsx`) — Force power browse/purchase list, mirrors `SpecSelectorList`'s list → detail-panel → preview-modal → confirm pattern (kept as a separate parallel component rather than generalizing the two, since the underlying data shapes differ enough that a shared abstraction would be messier). Detail panel shows name, min-Force-Rating badge, description, a "Preview Power Tree" button opening `ForcePowerTreePreviewModal` (renders `<ForcePowerTree previewMode>`), and a "Purchase Base Power" button.
- `PurchaseCeremony` (`src/components/character/PurchaseCeremony.tsx`, Prompt 9, extended Prompt F4) — shared portal (`document.body`, `Z.hudSupreme`) for three moments: the talent-purchase ceremony (`TalentCeremonyPayload`, `kind: 'talent'`), the spec-purchase celebration (`SpecCeremonyPayload`, `kind: 'spec'`, lists newly-granted career skills), and the Force-ability purchase ceremony (`ForceCeremonyPayload`, `kind: 'force'`, Prompt F4 — shows the ability name + owning power name, since a character may own several Force powers at once, plus an optional `rankLabel` for a multi-purchase node's 2nd+ position such as JERINFRANGE/WARFORDURATION). `force` shares the exact same lift/drain/burst/stamp/return timeline branch as `talent` (keyed off `payload.kind === 'talent' || payload.kind === 'force'` throughout), just with Force-blue (`var(--die-force)`) card colouring instead of an activation-token colour, since Force abilities have no activation concept. Renders only AFTER the real DB write has already resolved — callers (`TalentTree.tsx`'s `dossierAction.onConfirm`, `SigNodeGrid`'s equivalent in `TalentSurface.tsx`, `talents/page.tsx`'s `BuySpecButton` `onBuy`, `ForcePowerTree.tsx`'s `dossierAction.onConfirm`) `await` the mutation and only construct a payload on a truthy/non-throwing result, so the animation never gates or masks the write. The talent/force ceremony's card is a portalled replica (not the real plaque) that animates from a caller-captured `sourceRect` to centre stage and back — by the time it fades out, the real plaque underneath is already re-rendered in its owned state (the mutation's own optimistic update fired well before this component even mounts), so no clone persists. Skippable by clicking the backdrop (kills the GSAP timeline, same `touched[]`/`clearProps` convention as `runEnergyTrace`/`runTreeEntrance`). Under `usePrefersReducedMotion`, renders the same final content statically for a fixed (talent/force) or skill-count-scaled (spec, up to 4s) hold instead of animating — information is never skipped, only the motion is. DEDI (dedication) talent purchases deliberately do not trigger this — `TalentSurface.tsx`'s `onPurchase` wrapper returns `undefined` for that branch on purpose (it hands off to `DedicationModal` instead), and `TalentTree.tsx` treats any falsy purchase result as "no ceremony" with no DEDI-specific check needed on that side. `purchaseForceAbility` (`useCharacterData.ts`) returns `Promise<string | undefined>` (the new row id, or `undefined` on any refusal — insufficient XP, Force Rating < 1, or an XP-race loss) for the same reason `purchaseTalent` does: it's the truthy/falsy signal `ForcePowerTree.tsx` gates its ceremony on.
  **H4 — hand-bound exit.** Both `TalentCeremonyPayload` and `ForceCeremonyPayload` gained an optional `handBound?: boolean` flag: true for a purchase that lands in the player's hand (an active, non-Passive talent; a Force power's base/row-0-col-0 node — always true for `talents/page.tsx`'s `forceBaseCeremony`, since that call site is hardcoded to the base node). H4's own Step 0 audit found a real blocker before any code changed: none of the four `<PurchaseCeremony>` call sites (`talents/page.tsx` ×2, `ForcePowerTree.tsx`, `TalentTree.tsx`) share a React tree with `HandOverlay` (mounted only inside `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx`, reached from the talents route only via a full `router.push` navigation away) — so there is no real hand DOM element/rect on that page to fly a card toward. Resolved (user decision, not a unilateral call): talents route never shows hand cards at all; a hand-bound ceremony instead exits by shrinking/fading toward the bottom-center of the viewport (`top:96%, left:50%, scale:0.4, opacity:0`, `power2.in`, 0.35s) rather than reversing to `sourceRect` — a directional "sent to hand" cue, not a literal landing, since no real hand element exists on this route to land on. Player is never navigated anywhere; the ceremony is an overlay on top of the talents screen the whole time, so "returned to the talents screen" falls out for free once the overlay closes. Non-hand-bound purchases (passive talents, Force upgrade nodes past the base, spec purchases, sig abilities) are completely unaffected — `handBound` defaults falsy and the original reverse-to-`sourceRect`/fade behavior is untouched, including `TalentSurface.tsx`'s sig-ability ceremony, which was never wired to `handBound` at all (out of scope, matching H0/H2's finding that sig abilities were never part of the hand's data model). Reduced-motion path unchanged for all cases — static hold, then unmount, no positional difference. **Superseded by H4b below** — the `top:96%` placeholder destination no longer applies.
  **H4b — ephemeral hand-reveal (resolves H4's stop).** Rather than permanently mounting `HandOverlay` on the talents route (would need its own tuck-state/layout questions), a new component `TalentsRouteHandReveal` (`src/components/character/TalentsRouteHandReveal.tsx`) renders a single-card strip reusing `HandOverlay`'s own `CardFace` and `.handZone`/`.hand`/`.card` CSS classes (both now exported from `HandOverlay.tsx`, alongside `FORCE_HEADER_BG`/`FORCE_HEADER_TEXT` for the Force-card header colour) — never a second card renderer. `PurchaseCeremony` owns its lifecycle entirely: when `payload.handBound` is true (talent or force `kind` only — `SpecCeremonyPayload` has no `handBound` field at all, so spec/sig ceremonies are structurally excluded, not just behaviorally), it builds a minimal `HandCard` from the payload and renders `<TalentsRouteHandReveal>` as a sibling inside its own portal, positioned in DOM after the backdrop (so it isn't dimmed by the backdrop's `color-mix`/blur) and before the flying replica card (so the replica visibly settles on top of it, later paint = on top at equal z-index). The strip is measured via `getBoundingClientRect()` synchronously in the same effect that builds the GSAP timeline, before the timeline runs, so a real rect always exists at tween-start, never mid-flight, and that rect becomes the handBound branch's actual destination (`handRect.top/left/width/height`), replacing H4's `top:96%` placeholder. Reveal (`opacity:0,y:60` to `opacity:1,y:0`) is chained at the same timeline position as the backdrop's own fade-in; withdrawal is chained at the same position as the branch's existing hold-then-exit beat — one shared timeline, no second independent timer, so reveal/withdraw can never race the ceremony's own beats. Reduced motion: the strip is set fully visible (`gsap.set`, no tween) alongside the already-settled static card, then disappears with the rest of the ceremony on unmount — no fly, per H4's original reduced-motion finding. Live-verified via Playwright on two real characters/purchases: an active talent (Stim Application, Hugo Sass) and a Force base power (Endure, Grevi Unduli, force-rating-eligible) both showed the ephemeral hand holding the correct card mid-ceremony then cleanly unmounted after; a passive talent (Grit) and a Force upgrade node (Strength, under Endure) both confirmed to never show it; spec/sig purchases confirmed structurally exempt by payload shape (spec) and by `TalentSurface.tsx`'s sig ceremony never setting `handBound` (unchanged from H4/H0's finding).
  **H5 — check-time glow.** Hand cards whose talent maps to the active Skill/Combat Check's skill now glow — the payoff for H1's `talent_skill_relevance` table (migration 119). New hook `useTalentSkillRelevance` (`src/hooks/useTalentSkillRelevance.ts`) loads the full table once (scoped `dataset_source = 'respec'`, the active dataset) into a `talent_key -> Set<skill_key>` map — small/static, no realtime subscription. `HandCard` gained a `talentKey: string | null` field (the raw, unprefixed talent key for talent cards; always `null` for Force cards, since Force relevance is out of scope here — `talent_skill_relevance` is talent-keyed only, noted as a future gap, not worked around with a heuristic). `HandOverlay` resolves `isRelevant(card)` per card (`false` immediately if `!card.talentKey`, so Force cards structurally can't match) via set-membership against a new `activeCheckSkillKey` prop, correctly handling talents mapped to more than one skill. **Exposing the active skill required two callback props, not one** — Step 0's assumption that `PlayerHUDDesktop`'s existing `skillPopover` state covered the Skill Check surface was wrong: the rail's "SKILL CHECK" button opens `HudSkillQuickList`, a separate component with its own previously-local `selected: HudSkill | null` state; `skillPopover` is actually a different surface (`HudSkillsTab`'s per-row quick-roll popover, rendered via `HudModalsOverlay`). Both are legitimate "a check is active" sources, so both gained an `onActiveSkillChange?: (skillKey: string | null) => void` callback (`HudSkillQuickList.tsx`, `CombatCheckOverlay.tsx`), firing the current skill key when open and `null` on close/deselect. `CombatCheckOverlay` has a second wrinkle: Unarmed has no `RefWeapon` row, so its normal `refWeapon?.skill_key` derivation resolves `null` for it — fixed by hardcoding `isUnarmed ? 'BRAWL' : (refSkill?.key ?? null)`, matching how the same component already hardcodes Unarmed's own "Brawl" label elsewhere. `PlayerHUDDesktop` derives one `activeCheckSkillKey = skillPopover?.skill.key ?? quickSkillActiveKey ?? combatActiveSkillKey ?? null` (first non-null wins; mutually exclusive in practice via `handlePanelToggle`, but the derivation doesn't depend on that) and passes it to `HandOverlay`. Glow token: reused `--hud-glow` (existing, theme-aware, both themes) for `box-shadow`/`::after` inset glow rather than adding a new token — no CLAUDE.md token addition needed. Reduced motion: state (border-color + box-shadow) is always applied via `.card.relevant`; only the `::after` pulse layer's `glowPulse` animation (existing keyframe, reused) is swapped for a static `opacity: 0.8` under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. Live-verified via Playwright against Hugo Sass (archived test character, `is_archived = true` — per [[feedback_test_with_archived_chars]], no live-PC XP/purchase revert needed) after granting him `FRENZ` (Frenzied Attack, maps to both `BRAWL` and `MELEE` in real backfilled data — a genuine multi-skill case): glows correctly for the Skill Check console (Brawl selected → glow; switched to Charm → glow clears, no staleness; closed → clears), for Combat Check (Unarmed/Brawl weapon selected → glow via the hardcoded-Brawl fix above; closed → clears), and never for the unmapped `STIMAP` card regardless of active skill. Force-card exclusion is structural (verified by code, not live — Hugo owns no Force powers): `talentKey === null` always short-circuits `isRelevant`. Both themes confirmed render without breakage (kyber screenshot-verified).
  **H6 — Force power focus view + upgrade lattice.** Clicking a Force card in the hand (talent cards stay inert on click, per H2's original design intent — the click handler already lived on `handleCardPointerUp`'s "tap with no movement" branch, unused until now) opens `ForcePowerFocusView` (`src/components/player-hud/ForcePowerFocusView.tsx` + `.module.css`), a full-screen focus overlay: the power's base ability renders as a large centre-stage card, and every real tree position (`treeNodes.filter(n => n.span > 0 && n.abilityKey !== basicAbilityKey)`, the same span-owner filter `ForcePowerTree.tsx`/`useForcePowers.ts` already apply) fans out beside it in a `190px`-column grid — owned nodes (`ownedRank > 0`) full-opacity with a purple owned border and phoenix-mask rank pips for multi-rank positions, unowned nodes at `opacity: 0.5` with name/description only, no purchase affordance anywhere in the view (purchases remain exclusive to the talents/tree route). Sourced entirely from data already in scope at `HandOverlay` — `ForcePowerDisplay.treeNodes`/`treeConnections` (re-resolved by `powerKey` from the existing `allForcePowers` prop, keyed off a new `focusPowerKey` state rather than carrying the whole tree on the card) — no new query. Built as its own lattice renderer rather than reusing `TalentDossier`: that component's contract is one node's full purchase detail at a time (focus trap, single confirm action); this view's shape — every node in the tree at once, read-only — doesn't fit it without fighting its contract. "Play Power" renders on the big card per the reviewed mockup but is an explicit stub in this prompt (H7 wires the real Force Check launch): it currently just closes the focus view, a stated "not yet active" affordance rather than a silently-dead button. Z-index: `Z.popover` (420) — same tier `HandOverlay`'s own grid overlays already use, which per H2/H4's audit already clears the check overlays' `Z.modal` (410) tier, so the focus view stays reachable while a Skill/Combat/Force Check is open with no new tier needed. Reduced motion: CSS `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` on `.module.css`'s own `focusFadeIn`/`focusScaleIn` keyframes swaps them to `none` — information is identical either way, only the scale/fade-in motion is suppressed. Live-verified via Playwright against Hugo Sass (archived, per [[feedback_test_with_archived_chars]] — granted `force_rating: 1` and two real `BIND` tree purchases, `BINDBARE` base + `RANGEBINDRE` upgrade, disposable test state on an archived character, no revert needed): clicking the `force_BIND` card opened the focus view showing BIND's real description on the big card, "Range" rendered owned (purple border, full text) while Magnitude/Strength/Confounding Bindings/Bound Aggression rendered ghosted; closing via backdrop click removed all focus-view DOM cleanly, confirmed across two repeated open/close cycles. No purchase logic touched or reachable from this view.
