  **Talent card focus view.** Clicking a TALENT card in the hand now opens `TalentFocusView` (`src/components/player-hud/TalentFocusView.tsx` + `.module.css`) — a lattice-less sibling of H6's `ForcePowerFocusView`, not a `lattice: null` variant of that component: the two shapes (single card vs. card + node grid, "Play Power" vs. no such action) diverge enough that one component bending to fit both would fight its own contract more than it'd save. Same big-card visual language, backdrop, `Escape`/backdrop-click close, `Z.popover` z-index, and reduced-motion contract as H6 (CSS duplicated rather than shared for the same reason). Renders `HandCard`'s full un-clamped `description` via `RichText`, activation label + spec label as meta, and rank pips (phoenix mask) if ranked — no purchase affordance, no lattice. This is an intentional addition of click-to-open for talent cards (H2's original inert-on-click was a stated design choice then, not a bug being fixed now); wired into the same `handleCardPointerUp` "tap with no movement" branch H6 already used for Force cards, branching on `card.kind`. Live-verified via Playwright against Hugo Sass (archived, per [[feedback_test_with_archived_chars]]): clicking `STIMAP` (Stim Application, a real owned talent with a previously-clamped description) opened the focus view showing the complete un-clamped text; closing returned cleanly to the hand with no leftover DOM; Force cards' own H6 focus view (`BIND`) smoke-checked immediately after and confirmed unaffected.
  **H7 — "Play Power" wired to the Force Check modal.** The button (previously a stub that only closed the focus view) now opens `ForceCheckOverlay` with the clicked power pre-selected, then closes the focus view the same way the stub always did. Minimal addition, no modal-internals rewrite: `ForceCheckOverlay` gained one optional prop, `initialPowerKey?: string | null`, read only inside its existing reset-on-open `useEffect` (`setSelectedPowerKey(initialPowerKey ?? null)` in place of a hardcoded `null`) — `selectedPowerKey` was already the modal's sole internal selection state, set identically whether by this prop or by the picker's own click handler, so a pre-selected power behaves exactly like a manually-picked one from that point on (same pool, same allocation, same roll — nothing pre-assembled or auto-advanced). Wiring: `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` owns a new `initialForcePowerKey` state and a `handlePlayPower(powerKey)` (sets it, then opens the Force Check the same way the rail's own `force` entry does); threaded down `PlayerHUDDesktop -> HandOverlay -> ForcePowerFocusView` as an `onPlayPower` prop, the same shape as H5's `activeCheckSkillKey`/the tuck toggle's `onToggleHand` precedent, since `ForcePowerFocusView` mounts inside `HandOverlay`, not `PlayerHUDDesktop` directly. The normal rail entry point (`handlePanelToggle`'s `id === 'force'` branch) now explicitly clears `initialForcePowerKey` before opening, so a prior Play Power use can never leak a stale pre-selection into the next manual open — live-verified, not just reasoned: used Play Power on Bind, closed, then opened Force Check via the rail button and confirmed no power was pre-selected. Live-verified via Playwright against Hugo Sass (archived, per [[feedback_test_with_archived_chars]] — temporarily granted a second real base power, `ENDURE`/`ENDUREBARE`, disposable test state, reverted after) with two distinct powers: Play Power on the `BIND` card opened the check with Bind highlighted/selected and its real upgrades listed; on the `ENDURE` card, Endure. Both themes confirmed (Ember screenshot-verified; no CSS touched in this prompt, the modal's own styling is untouched).
  **H8 — drag-reorder + persistence + viewport-aware fan width.** Extends H3's existing custom pointer-drag rather than introducing dnd-kit (deliberate, per this prompt's own architecture decision — one pointer handler decides reorder vs. discard vs. click, matching the fan's non-standard arc-layout shape dnd-kit's sortable assumptions don't fit). Three parts:
  1. *Reorder.* Dragging a card horizontally live-shuffles the others aside as slot boundaries are crossed. `slotTransform(i, n, spread)` (module-scope, pure) was extracted from the H2-era layout effect so both the layout effect and the drag/drop-snap logic compute a slot's transform identically. A **real, live-caught bug** blocked this initially: rendering the fan from the live drag-preview array (`displayCards`, reordered DOM children) silently broke native `setPointerCapture` — confirmed via a global `pointerup` listener firing while the per-card React handler never did, the instant the dragged card's DOM position moved. Fixed by rendering the fan in a **stable** order (`orderedFanCards`, changes only on commit) always, and driving on-screen position purely through the layout effect's gsap transforms (keyed by card key via `cardRefs`, independent of DOM order) — `displayCards` (drag-preview order) now only feeds the layout effect's iteration, never JSX child order. A second bug, also live-caught: the layout effect used to keep rewriting the DRAGGED card's own `dataset.home*` to its live-shuffled slot, and `pointermove` re-read that dataset every tick and added the pointer's CUMULATIVE delta on top — so each boundary crossing shifted the anchor AND kept the full delta, compounding into a runaway drift that made longer drags miss the discard pile's hit-test entirely. Fixed by freezing the drag anchor (`homeX`/`homeY`) into `dragRef` once, at pointerdown, never re-read from the live-updating dataset; the drop-snap likewise computes the dragged card's FINAL transform directly from the order about to be persisted (via `slotTransform`) rather than from that same stale dataset. Hit-test precedence unambiguous and unchanged from H3: discard (drop on pile) is checked first at pointerup and wins outright; otherwise, if the drag crossed at least one slot boundary, the live-shuffled order commits. A drag that never crosses a boundary (e.g. toward the pile and back, or a vertical-only jiggle) writes nothing. Click-to-focus (H6/talent) is untouched — same `MOVE_THRESHOLD`-gated moved/not-moved branch as H3.
  2. *Persistence.* `useHandState.ts` gained `cardOrder` (loaded alongside `is_tucked`/`discarded_keys`) and `reorderCards(next)`, the same ref-then-enqueue commit shape as `commitDiscardedKeys`/`toggleTucked` — `orderRef` always updates synchronously so a queued write reads the LATEST order at execute time, proven (not assumed) via two rapid reorders with no settle wait between them, then a **direct SQL read**: DOM order and persisted `card_order` matched exactly. Written once per completed drag (on drop), not per pixel of live-shuffle — live-shuffle only touches a local `dragOrderKeys` preview state in `HandOverlay`, never the hook, until commit. Uses the identical key scheme as `discarded_keys` (unprefixed talent keys, `force_<powerKey>` for Force cards) — no new tagging needed, confirmed in Step 0. `cardOrder` is a **partial ordering hint**, never authoritative membership: `orderedFanCards` (a `useMemo` merge) walks `cardOrder` first, keeping only keys that are ALSO in `fanCards` (so a currently-discarded card's saved slot is skipped, not lost — it isn't deleted from `cardOrder`, just filtered out for that render), then appends any `fanCards` entries not yet in `cardOrder` (a fresh purchase, or a card returned from discard whose key was never reordered) in their existing default order. A card can never be dropped: it either has a saved slot, or falls back to append. Live-verified: a discarded-then-returned card reappeared at its ORIGINAL saved position (not appended to the end) since its key stayed in `cardOrder` the whole time; a brand-new purchase (simulated via a real `character_talents` insert on Hugo Sass, archived, per [[feedback_test_with_archived_chars]]) appeared at the end without disturbing the rest of a real saved order, confirmed via direct SQL read that `card_order` itself was untouched by the arrival.
  3. *Viewport-aware fan width.* The flat `340` spread-budget constant (`Math.min(58, 340/n)`) let the rightmost card nudge into the discard corner at 1366px — the discard stack's real screen position (map-panel-anchored, per the prior chrome prompt) was never part of the calculation. Recommendation from Step 0f, implemented: available half-width between the two corner stacks (`fanSafeHalf`, derived from the same `corners` ResizeObserver measurement the stacks already use) is the correct basis — it transitively folds in both viewport width AND map-panel width, and degrades to a generous flat fallback when `corners` is unmeasured or the hand is tucked (collision safety is moot when the stacks are hidden). `computeFanSpread(n, safeHalf)` numerically solves (binary search, not a closed form) for the largest spread — capped 58, floored 18 — whose outermost card's real on-screen half-extent fits inside `fanSafeHalf`. Getting the "real on-screen half-extent" right took two iterations: a rotate-about-center trig estimate correctly predicted the ROTATED card's bounding-box width growth (confirmed live: a 172x238 card at 13° really does measure ~221px wide) but still collided at 1366px, because `.card`'s CSS sets `transform-origin: 50% 118%` (the fan pivots around a point below the card, per the reviewed mockup's hand-of-cards silhouette) — rotating around that off-center pivot adds a lateral shift beyond plain `translateX`, live-measured against the actual pivot lever arm (`CARD_H * 0.68 ≈ 161.8px`) before the collision was fully eliminated. Verified via Playwright at 1366px and 1920px, at 4/5/8 cards (8 achieved by temporarily granting Hugo Sass three extra real talents, reverted after — disposable test state on an archived character): zero bounding-box collisions with either corner stack in any combination; at 8 cards the fan is visibly densely shingled (spread near its 18px floor) — an accepted consequence of prioritizing collision-avoidance over spacing at high card counts, not a new problem (the arc fan already deliberately overlaps cards by design, H2).
  Smoke-checked unaffected: H5 (glow — `isRelevant`/`talentSkillMap` logic untouched, live-reconfirmed glowing correctly), H6 (talent + Force focus views open correctly, click-to-focus not triggered by drag gestures), H7 (Play Power still opens the Force Check with the correct pre-selected power). Both themes confirmed (Ember screenshot-verified).
  **H9 — Force nameplate shader, one shared canvas.** `ForceNameplateField.tsx` (new) is the only new file — one WebGL context for every Force card's header, never one per card, per this prompt's own explicit architecture decision (browser context caps; PresenceSmoke.tsx's own history of cutting Three.js from a surface for the same reason). Follows `PresenceSmoke.tsx`'s teardown sequence exactly: `setAnimationLoop(null)` -> `ResizeObserver.disconnect()` -> `geometry/material/renderer.dispose()` -> DOM removal (no `gsap.killTweensOf` needed here — `uTime` is driven straight from a `THREE.Clock`, no tweened numeric targets). **Masking, a deliberate deviation from "behind":** the canvas paints ON TOP of the fan (`position:fixed;inset:0;pointer-events:none`, mounted after `.hand` in DOM so it paints over the cards, `zIndex: Z.popover` matching the hand's own tier), not behind it — a header sits nested inside `.card`'s own OPAQUE background, so a transparent header alone would reveal `.card`'s own gradient, not a canvas positioned behind the whole fan; showing the real canvas through would need CSS-mask surgery cutting an exact header-shaped hole through `.card`'s structure, the "significant extra work" this prompt explicitly permits declining. Painting on top at low alpha (~0.2, matching `PresenceSmoke`'s own 0.16 convention) reads as a shimmer over the header text without obscuring it — exactly this prompt's own offered Option A (feed each rect as a shader uniform, paint only those regions). **Rect tracking:** `headerEl.getBoundingClientRect()` for up to 8 (`MAX_RECTS`) Force-card headers per frame — deliberately NOT reconstructed from `gsap.getProperty` + manual transform/pivot math, because GSAP only ever touches `el.style.transform` (compositor-only, never invalidates layout) and the bounded (≤8) rect-read batch at the top of one rAF callback never interleaves with a write, so it's cheap; more importantly it's *exact* (rotation, H8's off-center `transform-origin: 50% 118%` pivot, and all) where hand-rolled matrix math is the fragile approximation H8 itself got wrong twice before landing on the correct geometry. `data-force-nameplate` (new, harmless on every other card) marks the header DOM node `CardFace` renders, keyed off `card.kind === 'force'` — not `isForceTalent` (a force-*flavoured* talent card is not a Force power and gets no shader). Header element refs are cached per card key (invalidated when `cardRefs`' owner element for that key changes — a card remounting under a returned/re-drawn key) to avoid a `querySelector` every frame. **Gate:** mount/unmount IS the gate — `HandOverlay` only renders `<ForceNameplateField>` when `!tucked && !prefersReducedMotion && forceCardKeys.length > 0`, the same "mount/unmount is the pause mechanism" convention `ForcePresenceCard` already established for `PresenceSmoke`. `forceCardKeys` derives from `orderedFanCards` (H8) filtered to `kind === 'force'` — discarded Force cards drop out for free, exactly like H8's own discard handling, no separate tracking. `useHudPanelContext` does NOT apply here (confirmed, Step 0d) — that gates the separate Force **tab** panel (`ForcePresenceCard`'s own mount), not the always-mounted hand overlay; the hand's own `tucked` state is the correct and only needed gate. **Colour:** `--force-spark` (not `--hud-accent-purple`) — the token this card's own ✦ glyph/text-shadow already uses, more specifically "Force identity" than the general-purpose accent-purple token; no new literal, no sealed namespace touched. **Reduced motion:** zero canvas mounts at all (not merely paused) — `FORCE_HEADER_BG` itself was layered with a permanent faint `--force-spark` radial gradient so the header still reads as textured rather than perfectly flat even with no shader running (the "layered gradient nameplate" static fallback this prompt asks for; the shader, when motion is allowed, paints on top of this same base). **Focus view's big card: explicitly deferred**, not silently skipped — it's owned by a separate component (`ForcePowerFocusView`) not guaranteed mounted alongside the hand's canvas/registry; wiring it in would mean prop-drilling the shared rect registry into a different component tree for a single additional card, which is real added complexity for one already highly-distinguished detail panel. Live-verified via Playwright: exactly one WebGL context confirmed (not assumed) by diffing canvas count across tuck/untuck — a differential test, since the map's own Pixi canvas is also present and asynchronous, a raw one-time count is not reliable evidence on its own; `parentElement`'s inline style (`position:fixed;inset:0px;...;z-index:420`) additionally confirmed unique identity. Reduced motion (`reducedMotion:'reduce'` context option): zero canvas. Repeated mount/unmount (3 navigate-away-and-back cycles): exactly one canvas afterward, no leak, no duplicate. Position tracking confirmed live through hover-lift (header rect moved with the lift) and a real drag-reorder (rect moved to the new slot, no stale position, no orphaned smoke patch) — since the shader reads the DOM rect fresh every frame, there's no separate position cache that could go stale by construction. Discard-then-return (Hugo Sass, archived, per [[feedback_test_with_archived_chars]]): canvas correctly unmounted the instant the last Force card left the fan (0 Force cards -> gate false) and remounted on return. Talent cards confirmed visually unaffected (flat header colour, no smoke bleed) in the same screenshots the Force header was verified in. Both themes confirmed (Ember screenshot-verified — Force purple is intentionally theme-invariant, same as `FORCE_HEADER_BG` already was pre-H9). H5/H6/H7 smoke-checked via the same Playwright pass (glow, both focus views, Play Power all still function); H8 smoke-checked via the drag-reorder test above, which exercises the exact same pointer-capture path H8 depends on.
  **Hand polish pass.** Five discrete fixes, no behavioural/data changes beyond what each states. (1) `.card` (the fan's cards only, not `.gridCard`/focus-view cards) got `cursor: grab`, plus a `.dragging` modifier (`cursor: grabbing`) toggled imperatively in `handleCardPointerDown`/`Up` via `el.classList` — same non-React-state convention as the existing `el.style.zIndex` there, to avoid a re-render per drag start/end. (2) `.card` also got `user-select: none` (screenshot-confirmed regression: dragging highlighted body text); `.gridCard` explicitly resets both back to `cursor: default; user-select: text`. Click-to-focus unaffected — verified live, since that branch is keyed on pointer movement distance (`MOVE_THRESHOLD`), never on native selection/click semantics. (3) **Root cause of the non-wrapping upgrade lattice, diagnosed before fixing (not assumed):** `.lattice`'s `grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, 190px)` was itself correct — the bug was that `.lattice` is a flex child of `.stage` (`display:flex`), and a `repeat(auto-fill, Npx)` grid's min-content contribution is exactly ONE column by spec; with the default `min-width:auto` flex floor, `.lattice` collapsed to that one-column min-content size instead of growing. Adding `flex:1 1 0; min-width:0` to `.lattice` alone did NOT fix it (verified live, lattice still measured 190px/one column) — the deeper cause was that `.stage` itself had only `max-width` (an upper bound), never a definite `width`, so it hugged its children's content size with nothing for `.lattice`'s `flex-grow` to expand into. Fixed by giving `.stage` `width: min(1200px, 90vw)` alongside its existing `max-width` — live-confirmed afterward: `.lattice` measured 859px, `grid-template-columns` resolved to four `190px` tracks, wrapping correctly. Separately, per explicit user decision, `ForcePowerFocusView.tsx` now filters to `ownedUpgradeNodes` (`n.ownedRank > 0`) only — the `.node.unowned`/ghosted-at-0.5-opacity display H6 shipped is removed entirely (CSS class deleted, left as a comment pointing to why); zero owned upgrades renders a plain centered message (`.emptyLattice`, "No upgrades purchased yet — see the Talents route to buy more.") rather than an empty grid; one owned upgrade renders as a single normally-sized grid cell, not stretched or malformed — both live-verified (BIND's one owned upgrade "Range", and a temporarily-granted zero-upgrade ENDURE base on Hugo Sass, archived, per [[feedback_test_with_archived_chars]], reverted after). (4) **Root cause of the discard-viewer header truncation, diagnosed before fixing:** `.gridTitle`'s available width is `.gridPanel`'s width (min `600px`) minus the `--space-16` H3 reserved for `.gridClose` — sized against H3's own shorter test strings. "Discarded — hover a card to take back," combined with `.gridTitle`'s `letter-spacing: 0.3em` (heavy tracking across ~38 uppercase characters), exceeded that budget at the 600px floor. Fixed by shortening the string to "Discarded — hover to take back" (same meaning, chosen over widening `.gridPanel` further — a fixed min-width chases a specific string length and could just as easily prove insufficient again later; the shorter string is the durable fix) — live-confirmed full text, no truncation, no `.gridClose` overlap, at both 1366px and 1920px. (5) `HudLeftRail`'s Hand toggle: pinned flush to the rail's bottom edge via `marginTop: 'auto'` on its wrapping div (first item in that div, plus the existing divider, get pushed down to fill the column's remaining space — same technique as any flex-column "footer stuck to bottom"), independent of how many items render above it. The `⌃`/`⌄` thin carets were replaced with solid `▲`/`▼` triangles — matching the rail's existing icon "set" (plain Unicode glyphs at a consistent solid/geometric weight — `⌖≋⬠⚙★▣✦⬡⊗` — not an icon font or library), which read far more clearly at the rail's 16px symbol size than the original chevrons; same tucked/raised semantic mapping (▲ = raise, when tucked; ▼ = tuck, when raised), no new icon source introduced. Live-verified: button's bottom edge sits at the viewport bottom minus the rail's own `8px` padding regardless of item count above it; icon and title flip correctly on toggle in both directions. Smoke-checked via the same Playwright pass: H5 (glow, `relevant` class confirmed applying to Frenzied Attack under an active Brawl check), H7 (Play Power still opens the Force Check with Bind pre-selected), H8 (a drag still triggers its intended branch — this pass's own test drag happened to land on the discard pile and correctly discarded, confirming that path unaffected; reverted after), H9 (exactly one WebGL context, confirmed via the same tuck/untuck differential method H9's own verification used). Both themes confirmed (Ember screenshot-verified).
  **Hand chrome: rail tuck toggle + map-anchored stacks + tuck hides all.** Three changes, one pass. (1) The tuck control moved out of the discard corner entirely and into `HudLeftRail`'s bottom-most entry (see that component's own entry above) — a state-labelled `⌄`/`⌃` toggle rather than an in-overlay button, gated absent in GM mode alongside the rest of the hand. (2) `useHandState`'s state (`tucked`, `discardedKeys`, `discardCard`, `returnCard`, plus a new `toggleTucked`) was lifted from inside `HandOverlay` up to `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` and threaded down as props — the same cross-component-state shape H5's `activeCheckSkillKey` already established — since the rail toggle needed to read/set `tucked` from outside `HandOverlay`'s own tree. `is_tucked` is now persisted (`toggleTucked`'s commit mirrors `discardCard`/`returnCard`'s existing optimistic-then-queued-upsert pattern in `useHandState.ts`) — H2's original deferral no longer applies now that tucking is a deliberate, discoverable rail action rather than a corner button; `card_order` remains the only untouched column (still H8). (3) The passive-deck and discard-pile stacks (`.deckCorner`/`.discardCorner`) now anchor to the **map panel's** bottom corners instead of the viewport's — the map panel (the center grid column in `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx`, where `HudSessionTab` renders) resizes when the notifications drawer opens (`gridTemplateRows: 'auto auto auto 1fr'`, drawer is a grid sibling in an `auto` row, fixed 100vh total), so plain CSS anchoring inside that column would track correctly — but `HandOverlay` mounts as a viewport-level sibling *outside* that grid entirely, so CSS anchoring alone can't reach it. Resolved via a `mapPanelRef` (attached to the center column div, passed down to `HandOverlay`) plus a `ResizeObserver` in `HandOverlay.tsx` that recomputes `position: fixed` left/right/bottom pixel coordinates for both corners on any resize — covers the drawer case and plain viewport resize alike. This also resolves the roll-feed collision the discard corner previously had at the viewport's top-right-adjacent bottom-right: anchoring to the map panel (which sits left of the roll-feed column) keeps the discard stack clear of it by construction. Tucking now hides both stacks entirely (`!tucked && corners &&` guard) — "not thinking about cards" clears the whole system except the rail's own toggle, the only way back; the passive deck and discard pile are deliberately unreachable while tucked, no workaround. Live-verified via Playwright against Hugo Sass (archived, per [[feedback_test_with_archived_chars]]): rail toggle present/bottom-most, matches rail item styling, flips `⌄`↔`⌃` correctly both directions and persists across an `is_tucked` upsert (confirmed via response logging, not just local state); deck/discard stacks anchored clear of the roll feed at 1440×900; tucking hides both stacks while the rail toggle remains, untucking restores them; GM mode (`?gm=1`) confirmed the rail toggle absent alongside the rest of the hand; Ember theme confirmed (rail toggle recolors via the same `--hud-accent`/CSS-var chain as every other rail entry, no new tokens). Notifications-drawer resize specifically was not observable live (no pending alerts existed on the test character to open it) — verified by mechanism instead: the anchor is a `ResizeObserver` on the map panel's own DOM node, which fires on any resize regardless of cause, so drawer-open is not a special case.
  **Passive-in-hand + generalized grid focus view.** Passive talents (`activation === taPassive`, previously fan-excluded) can now be voluntarily placed into the fan: the passive-deck grid's `CardGridOverlay` gained an `actionLabel`/`onAction` pair, generalized from the discard grid's old single-purpose `onReturn` (`"→ Place in Hand"` calling `placeCard`, vs. the discard grid's `"↑ Return to hand"` calling `returnCard` — same component, two configurations, no fork). Placed passives feed `ownedCards` via a new `placedPassiveCards` memo (`allPassiveCards` filtered to `hand_placed_keys` membership, tagged `isPlacedPassive: true`), alongside the existing `activeTalentCards`/`forceCards`. Un-placing reuses the existing discard-drag gesture rather than a new one: the drop-on-pile handler branches on `droppedCard?.isPlacedPassive` — `unplaceCard(key)` for a placed passive, `discardCard(key)` (unchanged) for everything else — so a placed passive dragged to the pile is removed from `hand_placed_keys`, never written into `discarded_keys`. Both grids also now open the same click-to-focus view H6/the talent-focus-view addition already wired for the fan (`onCardClick`, required on `CardGridOverlay` rather than optional) — clicking a card in the passive-deck or discard grid opens `ForcePowerFocusView`/`TalentFocusView` exactly as clicking it in the fan would. **Fix-round bug**: `focusTalentCard`'s lookup only checked `ownedCards`, so clicking an unplaced passive in the passive-deck grid (a card that is real and clickable but, being unplaced, was never in `ownedCards`) silently failed to open its focus view — fixed with the same `allPassiveCards` fallback (`ownedCards.find(...) ?? allPassiveCards.find(...)`) that `openFocusFor`'s own key-resolution already used, so both lookups now agree.
- `ForcePowerTree` (`src/components/character/ForcePowerTree.tsx`) — Force power tree grid, rendered both by `ForcePanel`'s in-HUD expandable power cards and by the `/character/[id]/talents` route's `'force'` rail destination. `buildForceCellKeyMap` (Prompt F1/F3) resolves any span-covered (row,col) to its owning plaque's key, since Force spans can start at any position with any width (unlike the sig tree's fixed row-0/4-wide base) — used for connector/plaque adjacency rendering AND (Prompt F4) by `buildForceEnergyGraph`, which converts a tree's nodes/connections into the `EnergyGraphNode[]`/`EnergyGraphEdge[]` shape `findCheapestPath`/`runEnergyTrace` (`buildTalentTree.ts`) already consume for talent trees — same shared pathfinder, no second implementation. A multi-purchase ability occupying several distinct tree positions (JERINFRANGE, WARFORDURATION) yields one graph node per position, each keeping its own cost, exactly like a ranked talent's per-row cost. Hovering a genuinely locked (adjacency-unreachable) node traces its cheapest route and shows a cost chip (XP total + ability count); the chip additionally appends "⚠ REQUIRES FORCE RATING 1" whenever the character's Force Rating is below 1, since the trace only answers the adjacency/cost question and the actual purchase at the end of that route would still be refused server-side until Force Rating is met (Force Rating never blocks the trace itself, only the eventual purchase). A confirmed purchase plays `<PurchaseCeremony>` with a `ForceCeremonyPayload` (kind `'force'`) resolving into the real owned plaque, same portal handoff pattern as `TalentTree.tsx`. Does NOT have `TalentTree.tsx`'s tier-by-tier mount entrance — confirmed out of scope in Prompt F4's audit, not wired. Accepts an optional `previewMode?: boolean` prop (threaded into its internal `Plaque`) — when true, every node renders as reachable/readable with no purchase flow, no energy trace, no ceremony; used by `ForcePowerSelectorList`'s read-only preview modal.

---


### Modal ignition — the canonical check-panel open/close animation

Named convention, established by Combat Check and **intended for reuse by Skill Check and Force Check**. The modal visibly emanates from the button that launched it.

- **Helper, not a component.** `igniteModalOpen(targets)` / `igniteModalClose(targets)` in `src/lib/utils.ts`, plus `.fc-pulse` / `.fc-scrim-ignite` in `globals.css`. There is deliberately no `<IgniteModal>` wrapper, hook file or animation module — the caller owns its refs, its `usePrefersReducedMotion()` value and its effect lifecycle, and hands the elements over.
- **`IgniteTargets`**: `{ inner, scrim, pulse, origin, accent, glow, restShadow, reducedMotion }`. **Accent and glow are parameters** — nothing panel-specific is hardcoded, so each panel passes its own. `origin` is the triggering element; a missing node falls back to the viewport centre so the animation still plays, just without the spatial link.
- **Open (~450ms):** scrim fades 120ms from t=0 · radial pulse from the button's centre, scale 0.4→16, opacity 0.9→0 over 420ms `power2.out` from t=0 · ignition from t=40ms, inner `scale: 0.08 → 1` over 340ms `back.out(1.4)` with `transformOrigin` at the button expressed relative to the modal's own box, starting on a hot border (`0 0 60px <glow>, 0 0 0 2px <accent>`) that cools to the resting shadow over 280ms overlapping by 80ms · content stagger from t=160ms, `opacity 0→1, y 14→0`, 300ms each, 50ms stagger. Stagger targets are tagged `data-ignite-stagger` at any depth; DOM order is tray-first, which is correct.
- **Close (~300ms, always faster):** content out (120ms, 20ms stagger, `power2.in`) · inner collapses to `scale: 0.08, opacity: 0` toward the same origin (240ms `power3.in`, overlapping by 40ms) · scrim out (160ms, overlapping by 60ms). `IGNITE_EXIT_MS` (300) is what you feed to `Modal`'s `exitMs`.
- **Animate the inner wrapper only.** The positioned anchor holds `position: fixed` and the centring transform; GSAP must never touch it.
- **Never gates anything.** The state write happens first and independently; the timeline is decorative. If they ever disagree, the write wins.

**Canonical effect shape** (the well-behaved pattern already used by `PresenceFlash`, `HudNotificationsDrawer` and `TalentsPanel`, now codified):

```ts
const prefersReducedMotion = usePrefersReducedMotion()
const tlRef = useRef<gsap.core.Timeline | null>(null)
useEffect(() => {
  tlRef.current?.kill()                       // spam-safe: kill in-flight first
  tlRef.current = open ? igniteModalOpen({...}) : igniteModalClose({...})
  return () => { tlRef.current?.kill() }      // clean up on unmount
}, [open, prefersReducedMotion])              // hook is false on first render —
                                              // it MUST be a dependency
```

Reduced motion collapses to a plain opacity fade (120ms in, 100ms out): no pulse, no scale, no stagger.

> **Known debt, out of scope here:** roughly half the app's GSAP consumers predate this convention — `MapToolsRadial`, `EncounterDeck`, `EncounterDossier`, `PlayerTokenTooltip`, `PresenceSmoke` ignore `usePrefersReducedMotion()` entirely, and `EncounterDeck`, `EncounterDossier`, `PresenceSmoke`, `ForcePanel` never kill their timelines. Retrofitting them is a separate cleanup.


### Focus console — two-phase form (Skill Check)

A documented **alternative to the three-column form**, for panels where one decision needs the full width and the rest do not. Skill Check is the first adopter; the shell, tray strip, footer, ignition, locked/active/done language and `.fc-*` classes are all shared with the three-column form.

- `HudSkillQuickList` (`src/components/player-hud/HudSkillQuickList.tsx`) is now a **centred modal** mounted by `PlayerHUDDesktop` (`open={activeQuickPanel === 'skill'}`), not a `.hud-quick-drawer`. `HudSessionTab` no longer hosts it — the drawer, its backdrop, and its `activeQuickPanel`/`onCloseQuickPanel`/`onOpenSkillPopover` props are gone. **Difficulty is now base + delta (this task)**, matching the combat panel's `ManualAdjustments.difficultyAdd` shape: `baseDifficulty` (set by the `DifficultySelector` ladder, 0–5) and `difficultyAdd` (signed delta from the `±` stepper, floored at `-baseDifficulty`, no ceiling) are separate state; effective difficulty fed into `buildSkillCheckPool` is `Math.max(0, baseDifficulty + difficultyAdd)`. Selecting a new ladder rung resets both `difficultyAdd` and `diffUpgrades` to 0 (same reset contract as before, when they were one field). The ladder always shows the chosen rung (`DifficultySelector selected={baseDifficulty}`); the panel summary and the Adjust-Pool stepper's `sub` text show the delta explicitly (e.g. `Hard (+1)` / `+1 vs Hard`) rather than folding it into a re-selected rung.
- **Ignition** consumes the documented convention with `origin: document.querySelector('.hud-rail-btn-skill')`; a missing node falls back to the viewport centre (verified). Timings are not re-derived — they live in the ignition section.
- **Phase 1 — skill grid.** Six characteristic columns across the full 1180px, `.fc-char-grid`. **Every skill appears, including rank 0**: untrained is a valid check that rolls the characteristic, so tiles render quieter (`.fc-sk-tile` without `.is-trained`) but stay fully selectable. The pip row is `max(charVal, rank)` long — filled for ranks, hollow for the remainder — so a skill ranked *above* its characteristic correctly shows more filled than hollow. The filter **dims and inerts** (`.is-dim`) rather than removing, so the grid never reflows.
- **Phase 2 — chip + two panels.** `.fc-p2-cols` is `1fr / 1.3fr`: Difficulty (six levels as a 3×2 grid) gets the **narrower** column and Adjust Pool the wider, the latter locked until a difficulty is explicitly chosen. The selected-skill chip returns to phase 1. The ratio was originally the other way round and that was the defect: Adjust Pool's steppers (swatch + label + `−` + count + `+`) wrapped the `+` onto its own line, so a one-row control cost three and the panel overflowed. `.fc-p2-cols .fc-dice-grid` pins the stepper grid to two columns rather than inheriting the shared `auto-fit, minmax(150px, 1fr)` rule, which on the widened column resolves to three ~197px cells — wide enough to fit but narrow enough to ellipsise `Difficulty` / `Add / remove`. The Combat Check's own `.fc-dice-grid` (a ~250px drawer) is untouched. `align-items: start` on the grid: the two panels have genuinely different heights and the default `stretch` drew Difficulty as a tall box with ~230px of empty space beneath its last row. `.fc-diff-opt` vertical padding is one step, not two — the tile is a single overline label plus a diamond row; type scale is unchanged. Measured live at a 1600×900 viewport: phase 2 is **496px**, the modal 724px, `.fc-console-body` does not scroll.
- **Phase crossfade + height spring.** Both phases are `position: absolute` in `.fc-phases` so the fade has nothing to reflow, and `.fc-phases` is GSAP height-tweened between them (phase 1 measured at 742px for a typical PC, phase 2 at 496px) — previously it was a fixed `min-height: 470px`, which gave dead space under phase 2 *and* a scrollbar inside Adjust Pool at the same time. The spring follows `TalentsPanel.tsx`'s `AnimatedDrawer`, the codebase's precedent: measure the incoming phase, tween to it. It does **not** release to `height: auto` on complete — with both children out of flow, `auto` collapses the wrapper to zero, so the inline height is the wrapper's only height and stays. The height tween is added to the timeline **last with an explicit position (`0.1`)** so the cross-fade's own relative timings are untouched while the two read as one motion. Three consequences worth keeping straight:
  - `.fc-phase-abs` is `top/left/right: 0` with auto height, deliberately **not** `inset: 0`. A bottom-anchored phase is stretched to the wrapper's current height, so measuring it would report the wrapper back and the spring could never shrink.
  - The effect is `useLayoutEffect` and has **`open` in its dependency array**. `Modal` renders its children only while open, so the refs are null until the console is on screen — without `open` the effect only ever ran on mount (refs null, early return) and the wrapper, which has no natural height, stayed collapsed at 0. That state is not merely cosmetic: it made the whole console 228px tall with the skill tiles unclickable underneath the tray and footer.
  - First run of a freshly-mounted console snaps the height (`phaseMountedRef`) instead of tweening `0 → full`, which would be a second motion competing with the ignition. Reduced motion snaps both the height and the swap.
- **Locked panels use `filter: opacity()`, never the `opacity` property** — the same conflict that bit the three-column form: GSAP writes inline `opacity` during the stagger and an inline style beats a class.
- `difficultyChosen` is **presentational** state: `selectedDifficulty` defaults to 2 (Average) and so cannot answer "has the player chosen yet".
- **Skill selection resets** Boost/Setback/upgrades/Force and returns difficulty to Average — pre-existing behaviour in `selectSkill`, deliberately preserved.

## State Management Pattern

Holocron uses **minimal global state** — Zustand stores for transient UI selections (e.g., selected character). Primary state flows as:

```
Hook (owns DB + realtime) → Page shell (owns display transforms) → Panel (pure display)
```

1. Hooks fetch from Supabase and expose raw DB rows + computed stats
2. Page shells (`PlayerHUDDesktop`, `gm/page.tsx`) do **all** the mapping into HUD-friendly display objects (`hudWeapons`, `hudSkills`, `hudGear`, etc.) via `useMemo`
3. Panels receive props and render — they call back up for mutations

**Mutation flow**: Panel calls prop callback → Page shell delegates to hook handler → Hook optimistically updates local state + writes to Supabase → Realtime subscription fires on other clients → All clients re-render

---

## Build & Dev Server (`next.config.ts`)

**Workspace root is pinned — do not remove it.** `turbopack.root` and `outputFileTracingRoot` are both set to this directory (`path.resolve(__dirname)`).

**Why.** This app lives at `C:\Projects\Holocron\star-wars-rpg`, one level below `C:\Projects\Holocron`, which carries a stub `package.json` + `package-lock.json` (an `npm init -y` leftover: zero dependencies, one empty `packages` entry, no `node_modules`). Next detected two lockfiles, inferred the **parent** as the workspace root, and rooted Turbopack's file watcher a directory above the app — every module id read `[project]/star-wars-rpg/...`.

**What that caused (measured, not theorised).** A single ordinary edit to `globals.css` on a fresh dev server with a clean `.next` took `/table` from 200 to **500 or 404 and it never recovered** — `⨯ Could not find the module "…" in the React Client Manifest`, the entire client manifest lost, not just CSS. Reverting the edit did not bring it back; only a restart did. Measured **3 of 3 fresh servers collapsed on the first CSS edit** before the fix, **0 of 3 after**. This is the likeliest explanation for the "stale CSS survived a dev-server restart" reports — the server was serving nothing, not stale bytes.

**What it did NOT fix — a separate, known upstream Turbopack bug that is still live.** Edits to `globals.css` are served **one revision behind**: the CSS you get is the content of your *previous* edit, and it only advances when a further file event occurs. Measured after the root fix, 10 sequential edits: **2 current, 8 stale.**
```
wrote mk1 -> served contains: mk1      wrote mk6  -> served contains: mk5
wrote mk2 -> served contains: mk1      wrote mk7  -> served contains: mk6
wrote mk3 -> served contains: mk3      wrote mk8  -> served contains: mk7
wrote mk4 -> served contains: mk3      wrote mk9  -> served contains: mk8
wrote mk5 -> served contains: mk4      wrote mk10 -> served contains: mk9
```
- **Scope is narrow and worth knowing**: only `src/app/globals.css` — the PostCSS *entry* file — is affected. `@import`ed partials (`src/styles/*.css`) measured 5/5 current and CSS modules 4/4 current. If a style edit seems not to land, that is the file to suspect.
- **`touch src/app/globals.css` is the workaround** and works for the reason above: the touch is not re-emitting a correct file, it is supplying the next file event that flushes the pending one.
- **Production builds are unaffected** — verified 4/4 emitting correctly both before and after the root fix. `npm run build` is trustworthy; only `next dev` lags.
- **webpack is clean on both symptoms** (8/8 CSS updates current, no collapse). Not adopted — Turbopack remains the dev bundler; recorded only as evidence that the defect is Turbopack-side.
- Upstream: [next.js#87884](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/issues/87884) (open, `CSS`+`Turbopack`), [next.js#90034](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/discussions/90034) (maintainer-acknowledged: Turbopack reads an outdated content snapshot while seeing a newer timestamp, when Tailwind is pulled in via `@import "tailwindcss"` — line 1 of `globals.css`). The Tailwind-side half was fixed in 4.1.3 ([#17514](https://github.com/tailwindlabs/tailwindcss/pull/17514), [#17554](https://github.com/tailwindlabs/tailwindcss/pull/17554)); this project is on 4.2.1 and still reproduces, so the remaining defect is Turbopack's.
- Environment when measured: tailwindcss/@tailwindcss/postcss 4.2.1, lightningcss 1.31.1, React 19.2.3, Node 24.14.1, npm 11.11.0, Windows 11.

**Next 16.1.6 → 16.3.0 was applied and measured. It did NOT fix the lag.** Next only; no peer bump was required (16.3.0 wants `react ^19.0.0` / `react-dom ^19.0.0`, both satisfied at 19.2.3; `sass`, `@playwright/test`, `@opentelemetry/api`, `babel-plugin-react-compiler` are all optional; `engines.node >=20.9.0` satisfied). Measured across the three identical harnesses:

| | 16.1.6, parent-rooted | 16.1.6, root pinned | **16.3.0, root pinned** |
|---|---|---|---|
| CSS edit reaches browser (10 trials) | 0/10 (server died on edit 1) | 2/10 | **4/10** |
| Dev-server collapse (3 fresh servers) | 3/3 collapsed | 0/3 | **0/3** |
| Production build (4 runs) | 4/4 correct | 4/4 correct | **4/4 correct** |

4/10 vs 2/10 is within noise on a 10-trial sample — treat the lag as **unchanged**. The bump was kept anyway: it costs nothing measurable, carries 16.2's ~200 Turbopack fixes, and regressed nothing (build clean, 13 routes unchanged, `tsc --noEmit` zero errors, ESLint identical at 95 errors / 215 warnings over `src`, all three check consoles open/roll/close, ignition fires, Modal deferred unmount intact, zero GSAP warnings across repeated open/close, Pixi map renders under WebGL, Supabase realtime connects).

**Side effect of 16.3.0 to know about:** `next dev` now writes a `<!-- BEGIN:nextjs-agent-rules -->` block into `CLAUDE.md` on every run (generated by `node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/generate-agent-files.js`). Deleting it from a diff only makes the next `next dev` re-add it — commit it alongside the work to keep the tree clean.

**`eslint-config-next` is still pinned at 16.1.6** while `next` is 16.3.0. Deliberate — the brief was Next only. Worth matching on a later pass.

**The parent stub files still exist.** `C:\Projects\Holocron\package.json` and `package-lock.json` are unreferenced — nothing imports them, there is no parent `node_modules`, and the only real content in that tree is this repo. Deleting them would remove the ambiguity at source, but the pinned config makes the inference explicit regardless, so deletion is optional cleanup rather than a requirement.

## Design Token System

**Source of truth**: `src/lib/tokens.ts`
```typescript
import { COLOR, HUD, FS, SP, RADIUS, Z, SHADOW, EASE, CHAR_COLOR, DICE_META, SYM } from '@/lib/tokens'
```

**Dice identity colours**: `DICE_COLOR` (fill) and `DICE_OUTLINE` (edge) in `tokens.ts` are the single source of truth — `components/dice/DiceFace.tsx` re-exports `DICE_COLOR` as `DICE_COLORS` rather than keeping its own copy (that duplicate is how setback drifted to a blue-grey indistinguishable from boost). Setback is the one die whose fill (`#0D0F12`, black) cannot double as its ink: it takes the light grey `DICE_OUTLINE.setback` for strokes/borders, and the `.die.setback` chip in `globals.css` pairs `--die-setback-fill` (body) with `--die-setback` (ink/edge). Any solid-shape die chip must draw the outline or the black die disappears against the HUD.

**Dice SHAPES are identity, exactly like dice colours — `DICE_META[type].shape` is the single source of truth and is not negotiable per-surface.** The FFG dice each have one physical shape and every renderer must draw it:

| `shape` | Dice | How to draw it |
|---|---|---|
| `octagon` | **Force**, Proficiency, Challenge | `clip-path: polygon(30% 0%,70% 0%,100% 30%,100% 70%,70% 100%,30% 100%,0% 70%,0% 30%)` — the `CLIP_OCT`/`CLIP_OCTAGON` constant duplicated in `CombatCheckOverlay.tsx`, `HudSkillQuickList.tsx` and `RangeBandStep.tsx` |
| `diamond` | Difficulty, Ability | `clip-path: polygon(50% 0%,100% 50%,50% 100%,0% 50%)` (`CLIP_DIA`) |
| `rounded` | Boost, Setback | `border-radius: RADIUS.sm` |

**A `border-radius` square is never a valid Force / Proficiency / Challenge die.** This regressed once — the Force Check console's rebuilt dice (`.fc-fdie`) shipped as 12px-rounded squares — so when adding any new die renderer, read `DICE_META[type].shape` rather than eyeballing it, and check the three existing `CLIP_*` constants first.

**Glow on a clipped die must be a wrapper `filter: drop-shadow`, not `box-shadow`.** `clip-path` clips `box-shadow` away entirely, so an outer halo declared on the clipped element itself renders as nothing; only `inset` shadows survive. The established fix is a plain wrapper element carrying `filter: drop-shadow(...)`, which follows the child's painted pixels around the octagon instead of its bounding rectangle — see `.fc-fdie-glow` in `globals.css` and the identical wrapper idiom in `HudSkillQuickList.tsx`'s tray dice.

**CSS custom properties**: `src/app/globals.css` defines `--color-gold`, `--font-rajdhani`, etc. using `clamp()` for fluid scaling.

**Theme-aware semantic CSS variables**: New opacity stops for HUD colors enable theme overrides via `[data-theme]` selectors without component changes:
- `--hud-accent-10`, `--hud-accent-20`, `--hud-accent-25`, `--hud-accent-35`, `--hud-accent-40`, `--hud-accent-45`, `--hud-accent-50`, `--hud-accent-60`, `--hud-accent-border`
- `--hud-gold-subtle`, `--hud-gold-border`, `--hud-gold-40`

**Faction image styling** (`.hud-fi` base class): 18×18px container with per-faction CSS filter chains applied via `.hud-fi-rebel`, `.hud-fi-jedi`, `.hud-fi-empire` classes; theme-specific overrides via `[data-theme="operative"]` and `[data-theme="kyber"]` selectors

**Rail button styling**: `.hud-rail-btn-*` classes for quick/nav/utility buttons; `.hud-rail-btn-adversaries` for red-tinted adversaries button

**Backward-compat shims**: `src/components/player-hud/design-tokens.ts`, `src/components/wireframe/wf-tokens.ts`, and `src/lib/styles.ts` re-export from `tokens.ts`. New code must import directly from `@/lib/tokens`.

## Key Utilities (`src/lib/`)

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `types.ts` | All TypeScript interfaces and type aliases |
| `activeDataset.ts` | Exports `fetchActiveDataset(supabase)` and `resetActiveDatasetCache()`. Reads `campaign_settings.active_dataset` with module-level caching; returns `'respec'` as fallback on failure. |
| `refDataCache.ts` | Prompt 7c — shared in-memory, session-scoped cache for the static-per-dataset ref tables `ref_skills`/`ref_talents`(+custom merge)/`ref_specializations`/`ref_careers`. Keyed by dataset string (`Map<string, RefDataSet>`); `getRefData(ds)` de-dupes concurrent in-flight requests for the same dataset via a second `Map` of pending promises; `warmRefDataCache(ds)` is a fire-and-forget wrapper for hover/focus prefetch that never throws. Used by `useCharacterData`, `useTalentSurfaceData`, `useCharacterSigAbilities`, and `characterDataPrefetch.ts`. Does NOT cache the `ref_specializations` retired-spec fallback merge — that depends on which specs a specific character owns, so each hook still does that lookup itself on top of the cached dataset-scoped rows. |
| `characterDataPrefetch.ts` | Prompt A — hoists `useCharacterData`'s full cold-load query batch (character-specific tables + `getRefData`) into a characterId-keyed, in-flight-deduped module cache so `src/app/page.tsx`'s `handleCardSelect` can fire it the instant a character is clicked, instead of waiting for the ~5.6s hyperspace animation to finish. `prefetchCharacterData(id)` fires fire-and-forget (self-catches, orphan-swept after 30s); `consumeCharacterDataPrefetch(id)` is a one-shot claim used only by `useCharacterData.loadCharacter`'s initial load — a cache miss (direct URL entry, refresh, back-nav, or a realtime-triggered silent reload) falls through to `fetchCharacterDataBatch(id)` fired fresh, so the batch is never fetched twice for one load. |
| `supabase/client.ts` | Singleton Supabase browser client |
| `tokens.ts` | Design tokens — colors, fonts, spacing, z-index, radius, shadows |
| `theme.ts` | Theme management: `ThemeId = 'ember' \| 'kyber' \| 'gm-imperial'`; `getTheme()`, `setTheme()`, `initTheme()` functions; persists selection in localStorage (key: `holocron_theme`); sets `data-theme` attribute on `<html>` for CSS targeting. `gm-imperial` is set directly by `GmShell.tsx` on mount (not via ThemeSwitcher) and removed on unmount. |
| `parseSymbols.ts` | OggDude shortcode `[su]`, `:colon:` → typed segment array; used by `RichText`. Full authoritative shortcode list: `docs/richtext-shortcodes.md`. Colon pre-pass now also accepts `:tier+N:` (e.g. `:hard+2:`) — upgrades the tier's base difficulty count per FFG RAW (`upgradeDifficulty(base, n)`, exported) before the bracket parser runs; expands to adjacent `[difficulty:N][challenge:N]` tokens. `:simple+N:` behavior (base 0 difficulty) is unconfirmed against a human default — see doc for the flagged open question. Unrecognised `[key:N]` (count-suffixed) tokens now render as a `.richtext-unknown-chip` error chip (red outline, `var(--red)`) instead of literal text — e.g. `[d:3]` chips. Count-LESS unrecognised tokens (`[d]`, `[Engaged]`, `[Short]`, `[Medium]`, `[Planetary]`, price `[R]`) still fall through to literal text unchanged: real ref content uses bracket syntax for non-shortcode prose (range bands, credit symbol) with no `:N` suffix, so only the `:N` form was safe to flag as a likely typo. `RichText.tsx` groups any run of 2+ consecutive dice/symbol icons from one expansion (a `[key:N]` or a `:tier+N:` split) into a single `.richtext-dice-group` (`globals.css`, `display:inline-block; white-space:nowrap`) so the group can't wrap mid-line; a lone icon is emitted unwrapped, unchanged from before. |
| `utils.ts` | `cn()` class merge, `stripBBCode()`, `randomUUID()`, and the **modal ignition** helpers `igniteModalOpen`/`igniteModalClose`/`IGNITE_EXIT_MS` (see the convention below) |
| `derivedStats.ts` | Stat computation helpers (soak, encumbrance via `computeEncumbranceStats` — single source of truth, see `useCharacterData` entry above — XP, force rating) |
| `combatCheckUtils.ts` | Dice pool assembly, combat check helpers. **`getMeleeDifficulty` treats an absent `Melee` key as 0 ranks, not "no skill".** `AdversaryInstance.skillRanks` is a *sparse* map — only skills with ≥1 rank are stored — so the old `?? -1` sentinel plus a `meleeRank < 0` bail-out misread every unskilled adversary (5 of 9 in live encounter data) as having no Melee skill and returned **zero** opposing dice with a "set difficulty manually" note. Every character has every skill; 0 ranks simply means you roll the linked characteristic. With rank 0 the existing maths is already correct — `proficiency = min(brawn, 0) = 0`, `ability = |brawn - 0| = brawn` — so a Brawn 2 unskilled defender now correctly opposes with 2 difficulty / 0 challenge. `fallbackReason` is now set **only** when there is no target at all. Consumers: `CombatCheckOverlay`, `DicePoolReviewStep` (the live pool), `RangeBandStep`. **Phase 0 check-calc extraction**: `getDualWieldPool(...)` — pure dual-wield pool/difficulty math (min ranks, min characteristics, max range-difficulty of the two weapons + same/different-skill penalty), extracted from `DicePoolReviewStep`'s dual-wield branch; `mergeManeuverAdjustments(adjustments, aimBoosts, assistActive, guardedActive)` and `totalPoolDice(pool)`, extracted from `CombatCheckOverlay`'s maneuver-merge and roll-readiness calcs. **Upgrade rule reconciliation (GM-approved behaviour change)**: `DicePoolReviewStep`'s ability-upgrade and difficulty-upgrade conversions now call `upgradeAbility`/`upgradeDifficulty` (`dice-engine.ts`, see below) instead of their old bespoke maths — see that entry for what changed and why roll outputs differ from pre-change sessions. |
| `adversaries.ts` | OggDude adversary JSON types + `fetchAdversaries()`. **`Adversary.soak` is always the FINAL soak** — OggDude's `derived.soak` already includes armour, and `AdversaryEditor` totals Brawn + every gear `soak` on save (migration 116). Never add gear soak on top of it at a read site. |
| `vehicles.ts` | OggDude vehicle JSON types + `fetchVehicles()`; `vehicleWeaponSkillKey(key)` resolves the SWRPG skill key that governs firing a vehicle weapon (per-entry `VehicleWeaponEntry.skillKey`, defaults to `'GUNN'` — used by `CheckConsole.tsx`'s vehicle crew step) |
| `encounters.ts` | `ensureEncounterForMap(supabase, campaignId, mapId)` / `fetchEncounterForMap(...)` — the map's persistent encounter deck, created lazily on first add (migration 115). Replaces three near-identical `ensureActiveEncounter` copies (StagingFloatingToolbar, GmTokenControls, and inline selects in AdversaryLibrary/VehicleLibrary) that keyed on `is_active = true`. Handles the unique-index race on concurrent first-adds by re-reading. Callers: `useGmSession`, `EncounterDeck`, `GmTokenControls`, `StagingFloatingToolbar`, `AdversaryLibrary`, `VehicleLibrary`, `GmShell` (read-only). |
| `refLibraryEvents.ts` | Cross-component refresh bus for the custom ref libraries (`ref_adversaries` / `ref_vehicles`). `emitRefLibraryUpdated(kind)` is called by `AdversaryEditor`/`VehicleEditor` after a successful write; `useRefLibraryRefresh(kind?)` returns a counter consumers put in their fetch effect's dep array. Consumers: `GmToolsPanel`, `EncounterDeck` (inline search), `AdversaryLibrary`, `VehicleLibrary`. Window `CustomEvent` based — no provider needed, works across the separate trees that each fetch these tables independently. |
| `combat.ts` | `CombatEncounter`, `InitiativeSlot` types + encounter helpers |
| `damageEngine.ts` | Damage calculation: base + modifiers + qualities → final damage |
| `player-hud/dice-engine.ts` | Pure FFG dice engine — `rollPool`, `rollForceDice`, `getSkillPool`, `poolSize`. **Phase 0 check-calc extraction**: `buildSkillCheckPool(params)` — full skill-check pool composition, extracted from `HudSkillQuickList`'s pool `useMemo`; `getAvailableForceDice(forceRating, committedForce)`, extracted from `ForceCheckOverlay`. **Upgrade rule reconciliation (GM-approved behaviour change, roll outputs may differ from pre-change sessions)**: a prior audit found the skill panel (`buildSkillCheckPool`, via effective-rank folding) and the combat panel (`DicePoolReviewStep`, via unconditional add+convert) implemented "upgrade ability"/"upgrade difficulty" with two different, and per AoE Core's "Upgrading More Dice Than Available" rule both WRONG, algorithms. Replaced with one correct pair of pure functions: `upgradeAbility(proficiency, ability, upgrades)` and `upgradeDifficulty(difficulty, challenge, upgrades)` — each upgrade converts one die of the source type to the target type; if none remain, the upgrade instead ADDS a die of the source type and is consumed doing so (only a later upgrade converts that newly-added die). **`applyModifiers()` extraction (this task)**: `ManualAdjustments`/`EMPTY_ADJUSTMENTS` moved here from `DicePoolReviewStep.tsx` (which re-exports both for backward compat) so one shared type serves both panels. `applyModifiers(basePool, adjustments, extras?)` is the single implementation of the modifier-application step (add `difficultyAdd` → `upgradeAbility`/`upgradeDifficulty` → flat boost/setback/challenge/force adds, rulebook add-then-upgrade order, never negative); `extras` (`talentBoost`/`talentSetbackRemove`) is combat-only. `buildSkillCheckPool` is now a thin wrapper: `getSkillPool` on the true rank, then `applyModifiers` with no extras. `DicePoolReviewStep`'s inline modifier block (ability-upgrade, difficulty-add-then-upgrade, talent boost/setback-removal, final pool assembly) was deleted and replaced with one `applyModifiers` call; its dual-wield branch no longer pre-folds `adjustments.difficultyAdd` into `baseDiff` (that double-applied it) — `difficultyAdd` is now applied once, uniformly, inside `applyModifiers` for both the dual-wield and standard paths. Both panels — and both branches of the combat panel — now agree exactly for the same inputs; `buildSkillCheckPool`'s signature and callers are unchanged. |
| `dice.ts` | Dice pool types and roll result types |
| `forceRoll.ts` | Force die roll utilities |
| `forceUtils.ts` | Force rating availability helpers (`isForceUserSensitive`, `getAvailableForceRating`), Morality helpers (`isDarkSideFallen`, `getFreeForceAlignment`); `isForceCommitCapable(description)`/`isForceCommitCapableForAbility(key, description)` — prose-only detection (`commit` regex + `[FO]` substring, plus a hardcoded 5-key exclusion set) of whether a Force ability can be committed for an ongoing effect, since no dataset column encodes this; answers only "can this be committed", never a dice count (descriptions are fixed/variable/formula-based, so the UI uses a manual stepper). `fetchFreshCommitState(supabase, characterId, fallback)` — shared race-safe fresh-fetch for a commit/release read-modify-write, re-reading `force_rating_committed`/`force_commitments` immediately before the caller derives its update; used by `HudForceTab.tsx` (desktop) — its mobile counterpart, `MobileHudLayout.tsx`'s release handler, was deleted by the Mobile Shell Rebuild task and has no replacement yet, so mobile currently has no Force commit/release path at all. `computeBalancePointFlip(fromState, toState, currentLight, currentDark)` (Prompt C) — pure guard for a Force Presence Balance Point flip, validating the transition (dark→light is never valid) and clamping to `light+dark<=10`; returns `null` for an invalid transition. `fetchFreshBalancePoints(supabase, characterId, fallback)` — the matching fresh-fetch for `light_points`/`dark_points`. Both extracted this prompt and shared by the player's own flip (`useCharacterData.ts`'s `handleFlipBalancePoint`) and the GM's correction-path flip (`useGmCharacterActions.ts`'s `gmFlipBalancePoint`) — `ForcePresenceCard.tsx` exports no reusable sub-logic of its own, only the top-level component. |
| `moralitySystem.ts` | Force Presence system selector (migration 095, applied 2026-07-22) — `MoralitySystem = 'vanilla' \| 'force_presence'` type, `fetchMoralitySystem(supabase)` reads `campaign_settings.morality_system` (logs + throws on error/RLS-denial rather than silently falling back, unlike `activeDataset.ts`), `resetMoralitySystemCache()`. First real consumer wired (Prompt B): `useCharacterData.ts`'s `loadCharacter` fetches it once per character-sheet load (parallel with the active-dataset fetch, not per render), exposing `moralitySystem`/`moralitySystemError` — a fetch failure surfaces as a visible error state, never a silent default to either system. Threaded desktop-only through `PlayerHUDDesktop.tsx` → `HudForceTab.tsx` → `ForcePanel.tsx`'s swap point (below) and into `computeDerivedStats` (`derivedStats.ts`). Prompt C wired the SAME fetch-once convention into the GM-facing path: `useGmData.ts`'s `loadData` fetches it once per GM session load (same self-contained try/catch, parallel with the main roster batch), exposing `moralitySystem`/`moralitySystemError` threaded through `GmShell.tsx` → `GmToolsPanel.tsx`'s own swap point (below) — a second, independent fetch, not a shared one across desktop/GM, since they're different sessions/hooks, but the module-level cache inside `fetchMoralitySystem` means whichever side fetches first serves the other for free within the same browser tab. `campaign_settings.morality_system` defaults `'vanilla'`; this campaign's row is `'force_presence'`. `characters` also has `light_points`/`dark_points`/`session_conflict`/`session_tranquility` (all `integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, `CHECK` enforcing `light+dark <= 10`) — 10 Balance Points per character, Neutral derived as `10 - light - dark`, never stored. Vanilla Morality (`morality_value`, `ref_moralities`, `character_conflicts`, etc.) is untouched and remains fully functional as the default system. |
| `forceEligibility.ts` | `isDroid()` / `isClone()` species predicates (droids/clones can never gain a Force Rating) + `isEligibleForForceRating()` — eligible if owning a Force-sensitive spec and not droid/clone. Follows the `dathomiriUtils.ts` species-key-membership pattern. |
| `criticalUtils.ts` | Critical injury roll + severity helpers |
| `buildTalentTree.ts` | Constructs talent tree grid from specialization + purchased talents; `buildCharacterTalentTree(specKey, refSpecMap, refTalentMap, talents)` is the character-aware wrapper shared by `PlayerHUDDesktop`'s Talents tab and the `/character/[id]/talents` route. Also hosts the energy-trace path-finding (Prompt 8): `findCheapestPath(nodes, edges, targetKey)` — pure Dijkstra over a node-weighted graph (owned nodes cost 0, entry-point/tier-1 nodes always reachable from a virtual source, ties broken by fewest nodes) — and `runEnergyTrace(container, bars, path, opts)` — GSAP DOM orchestration (not pure, not React) that paints the connector bars and plaques along a path and reverts cleanly via `clearProps` on `.kill()`. Both `TalentTree.tsx` and `SigNodeGrid` (in `TalentSurface.tsx`) build their own `EnergyGraphNode[]`/`EnergyGraphEdge[]` from their existing node/connection data (no new queries, no re-derived eligibility) and call these on hover of a locked node. Also hosts `runTreeEntrance(container, tiers, bars, opts)` (Prompt 9) — same GSAP/`clearProps` conventions as `runEnergyTrace`, tier-by-tier (row-by-row) reveal of plaques (opacity/scale) and their outgoing connector bars (scaleX/scaleY wipe) on tree mount/spec-switch. Each tier gets one GSAP timeline label; every bar in that tier is positioned at that same label so they wipe in parallel with each other and the plaque fade rather than chaining one bar after another (an early version serialized them and pushed total reveal time for a 5-tier spec to 3.4s — fixed to ~1.6s). `revert()` (used both on `.kill()` and via `tl.eventCallback('onComplete', revert)` on natural finish) briefly sets `el.style.transition = 'none'` around each `clearProps` call, forces a layout read, then restores it — `.plaque`'s own CSS `transition: opacity …` (TalentTree.module.css) would otherwise race the instant clearProps snap, animating a locked plaque from gsap's inline `opacity:1` down to its CSS-cascade `opacity:0.28` over another ~250ms, which both made a "cut short by click" entrance keep visibly drifting well after the click and left every plaque (including locked ones) permanently stuck at inline `opacity:1` after an *uninterrupted* entrance, since nothing had ever cleared it before the `onComplete` hook was added. `TalentTree.tsx` guards the start with a `specName`-keyed ref (fires once per real tree-data change) plus a `barsReadyFor` state tag rather than depending on the `bars` array itself — `bars` gets a new reference on every incidental ResizeObserver remeasure, and depending on it directly tore down (and never restarted) the just-begun entrance the instant a second remeasure landed, often before a single frame painted. |
| `adversaryAdapter.ts` | Converts an `AdversaryInstance` → the Character/CharacterWeapon/CharacterSkill stubs `CombatCheckOverlay` needs (`adaptAdversaryForCombatCheck`); also `charactersToAdversaryStubs` (PCs → `AdversaryInstance` stubs, for use as combat targets), `getAdversarySkillRank`, and `weaponSkillKey` (classifies an `AdversaryWeapon` into a SWRPG skill key from its `skillCategory`/`range`/`name` — the single source of truth for "what skill does this weapon use", reused by `CheckConsole.tsx`'s Combat tab for its melee/ranged preview) |
| `adversary-abilities.ts` | Parses adversary special ability descriptions |
| `adversary-talents.ts` | Maps adversary talent keys to display names |
| `resolve-weapon.ts` | Resolves a character's weapon key → full weapon display data |
| `weaponHandedness.ts` | Brawn-linked weapon handedness overrides |
| `logRoll.ts` | Writes roll results to `roll_log` table |
| `characters.ts` | Character fetch helpers: `archiveCharacter`, `restoreCharacter`, `deleteCharacter`; `computeCareerSkillKeys(careerSkillKeys, ownedSpecs)` — pure union of career skills across career + ALL owned specs; `persistCareerSkills(characterId, careerSkillKeys, ownedSpecs, allSkillKeys)` — writes `character_skills.is_career` to match that union (both directions: true for the union, false otherwise). Called from `handleBuySpecialization` and the specialization-refund branch of `useGmPurchaseRefund`; character creation calls `computeCareerSkillKeys` directly to build its insert payload. `useCharacterData.ts`'s `buySpecialization` (Prompt 9) also calls `computeCareerSkillKeys` twice directly (owned specs before/after the new purchase) to compute `SpecPurchaseResult.newlyGrantedSkillKeys` — the set of skills the spec-purchase celebration announces — entirely in-memory, independent of `persistCareerSkills`'s own DB write. |
| `itemIconResolver.ts` | Single source of truth for catalogue item images (weapon/armor/gear), replacing the old `useCharacterData.ts` inline path templates and `EquipmentImage.tsx`'s manifest+`equipment-icons.ts` duplicate path (that file is deleted). `resolveItemIcon(ctx, table, key, categories)` walks a ladder, stopping at first hit: (1) `override` — GM-pinned image via `item_icon_overrides` (migration 123); (2) `exact` — `{table}-{key}.png` exists per `public/images/manifest.json`; (3) `category-pair` — donor sharing this item's two most-specific categories; (4) `single-category` — donor sharing the second-most-specific category alone (only reachable when the item has 2+ categories); (4b) `only-category` — donor sharing the item's single (non-top-level) category, for flat-taxonomy tables like gear where every row has exactly one category and rungs 3/4 are structurally unreachable; (4c) `broad-category` — for deep-hierarchy items (4+ categories) where rungs 3 and 4 both failed because their top-2 categories were near-singleton values, retries progressively broader from `categories[2]` onward until a donor is found (a wrong-silhouette donor beats a generic glyph; the GM override rung exists to correct a bad match); (5) `fallback` — per-table generic glyph (`_fallback-weapon/armor/gear.png`, present on disk). `TOP_LEVEL_CATEGORIES` (`'Ranged'`, `'Melee'`) are stripped before any specificity rung runs and excluded at every rung including 4b/4c, so a thrown knife never inherits from a heavy blaster pistol. Donor selection is deterministic at every rung (candidates sorted lexicographically, first wins), independent of catalog iteration order. Real-catalog impact of adding 4b/4c (1228 ref rows): gear fallbacks 471→98 (373 newly resolve via `only-category`), weapon fallbacks 235→116 (118 via `only-category`, 1 via `broad-category`), armor fallbacks 58→38 (20 via `only-category`) — zero-category counts (rows with no categories at all: weapon 63, armor 38, gear 43) are unchanged, confirming the fix only reached previously-skipped rungs, not previously-uncategorized rows. `createIconResolverContext(overrides, catalogs)` builds a per-load context with an internal memoization cache; `useCharacterData.ts` builds one via `useMemo` keyed on the campaign's `item_icon_overrides` + ref tables and exposes `resolveIconUrl(table, key, categories)`, used to populate `WpnDisplay`/`ArmDisplay`/`GearRow`'s `iconUrl`/`refKey`/`categories` fields. `EquipmentImage.tsx` (legacy surfaces: GmLootModal, VendorPurchaseDialog, character-sheet WeaponsCard/EquipmentCard/InventoryContent, HudNotificationsDrawer loot flash) is a thin wrapper over the same resolver, built from a single-item context (only rungs 2/5 can ever resolve there — no sibling catalog or campaign overrides in scope). |
| `ItemReadoutPlate.tsx` (`src/components/shared/`) | Presentation shell for a resolved item icon. `size: 'row'` renders bare tinted artwork only (used at 44px in `ItemThumb` and five other row-scale surfaces — a full plate is illegible there); `size: 'detail'` renders the plate (bordered, faint coordinate grid, dashed centrelines, corner registration brackets, scan-line hover pass) used in `ItemDetailHero` (58px) and `ItemDatabaseTab`'s View popup (48px). Artwork is a white-ink-on-transparent PNG tinted via CSS `mask-image` (alpha channel as mask, `background-color` paints the visible pixels) — fully theme-reactive through `var(--hud-gold)`/`var(--hud-accent)` with no JS recoloring or per-theme image variants; the artwork box is 84%×72% of the plate (independent width/height, not a square, so `mask-size:contain` doesn't shrink a landscape illustration further than necessary), leaving clearance from the corner brackets at any source aspect ratio. The plate's REF designation and category tags (originally rendered top-left/bottom-right) were removed rather than kept: at both real consumer sizes (48-58px) their fixed-rem text always overflowed the box and visually collided with the artwork, and there is currently no larger detail variant that would give them room — gating them behind a size that doesn't exist yet would have been dead code. If a larger detail variant is added later, reintroduce label rendering keyed to it rather than restoring it unconditionally. The hover scan-line animates `transform`/`opacity` on an absolutely-positioned inner div only, never the plate or any ancestor, so nothing gates first-paint visibility; respects `usePrefersReducedMotion`. `ItemDetailHero.tsx`'s `item_image_url` (a per-character-instance GM upload) outranks the resolver's `iconUrl` whenever set and loading successfully; an `onError` on that banner demotes it back to the resolver's plate instead of a broken-image icon. |
| `useItemIconContext.ts` (`src/hooks/`) | Shared icon-resolver wiring for the GM-facing surfaces added in Prompt 3 (Quartermaster, ItemDatabaseTab, ItemEditor, LootAwardModal) — each is an independent component/hook needing the full ref-table catalog (`key,name,categories`, all 3 tables, unscoped by any surface's own filters — donor matching needs the whole table) plus this campaign's `item_icon_overrides`. Returns `resolveIcon(table,key,categories)`, `catalogEntries(table)` (for `IconPicker`'s browse list), `overrides`, and `refetch()` — a plain re-fetch consumers call after an override write/delete, same convention as `useQuartermaster`'s own re-fetch-after-mutate. Extracted rather than duplicating `useCharacterData.ts`'s player-side fetch+useMemo pattern 4 times. |
| `IconPicker.tsx` (`src/components/shared/`) | GM-only campaign-scoped icon override picker (Prompt 3 Phase B), backing `item_icon_overrides` (migration 123). Portalled to `document.body` (works from any surface regardless of ancestor `backdrop-filter`). Shows the current resolution in plain language via a `IconRung → label` map ("Using its own illustration" / "Borrowed image" / "Pinned by GM" / "No illustration available"), plus which donor item it borrowed from when applicable (reverse-looked-up from `manifest.json` by matching the resolved path back to a key). Browses every image-bearing key in the table (via `manifest.json`, `photographic/` excluded by construction — the manifest never lists those paths) with a name/key search box and category filter chips, matching `AdversaryLibrary.tsx`'s established search+chip idiom. `onSelect`/`onReset` are pure callbacks — the picker does no DB I/O itself; callers decide whether to write immediately (editing an existing ref row) or defer (new custom item, no `item_key` yet until insert succeeds). Reused verbatim in two places: `ItemDatabaseTab.tsx`'s View popup (writes/deletes the override immediately, then calls `useItemIconContext`'s `refetch()` so the change is visible without a page reload) and `ItemEditor.tsx`'s create flow (defers to a `pendingIconKey` local state; `handleSave` writes the override once the new row's deterministic `key` exists, right after the ref-table insert succeeds). No second picker was built for item creation. |
| `dutyObligationUtils.ts` | Duty/Obligation roll + trigger helpers |
| `lightsaberUtils.ts` | Lightsaber crystal bonuses |
| `gmScreenData.ts` | Static GM screen reference tables |
| `styles.ts` | Backward-compat shim re-exporting `tokens.ts` |
| `marketSnapshot.ts` | Pure serialise/parse for a Market merchant snapshot (Prompt 1) — the only persistence for a merchant beyond the single `market_merchants` live row. `MARKET_SNAPSHOT_VERSION` (currently `1`); `serializeMerchantSnapshot(merchant)` maps a `market_merchants` row shape to a plain JSON-safe object. `parseMarketSnapshot(raw, knownRefKeys?)` treats input as untrusted (loaded from a user-supplied file picker): rejects invalid JSON, a version mismatch, or any missing/malformed field per stock line (bad `itemTable`/`tier` enum, wrong types) with a specific error string rather than partially loading; never `eval`s or spreads unvalidated objects into state. When `knownRefKeys` (a per-table `Set` of catalogue keys) is supplied, ref keys in the snapshot absent from it are returned in `unresolvedRefKeys` rather than silently dropped — retired/renamed catalogue keys are a real, already-observed case (see migration 126). No React/Supabase/DOM — unit-testable in isolation. Wired to the UI in Prompt 2 (`GmMarketPanel.tsx`'s Snapshot ↓/Restore ↑ footer buttons via `useMarketMerchant.ts`). |
| `marketGenerator.ts` | Pure stock-rolling logic for the Market feature (Prompt 2) — no React/Supabase/DOM, unit-testable alone. Reproduces the approved `marketplace-rail-panel.html` mockup's own `roll()`/`weight()`/tier-assignment algorithm exactly (not "inspired by"). `generateStock({catalogue, locationModifier, archetype, scale, legality, seed})` → `GeneratedStockLine[]`. Determinism: `mulberry32` (pure-integer PRNG, no `Math.random`/`Date`/`crypto`) — same seed+catalogue+params always produces identical output on any machine; the catalogue is re-sorted by `(table,key)` internally so caller fetch-order (e.g. Supabase's unordered scan) can never perturb the draw sequence — Playwright/tsx-verified: same seed → byte-identical output; different seed → differs. Selection weight `0.62^(rarity-1)`, hard-zeroed above the scale's rarity ceiling (`SCALES`: stall 7 lines/cap 5, shop 14/cap 7, emporium 24/cap 9), so common goods dominate; `junk` archetype re-biases toward rarity ≤4 (×1.6) and away from higher (×0.4); `black` archetype's `restrictedBias` rejects 35% of non-restricted candidate draws, biasing selection toward restricted goods. `ARCHETYPES`: general/weapons/armor (weapon/armor/gear mix ratios) and medical/tech (gear-only, category-filtered) and black/junk (mix + bias flags). Tier assignment: restricted items go back_room/under_counter per `legality` (grey: 65/35 under/back; black: 30% open, else 50/50 back/under; legit excludes restricted from the candidate pool entirely, never assigns it a tier); non-restricted items go open unless `modifiedRarity >= 6` (back_room), with `black` archetype additionally pushing 25% of would-be-open non-restricted lines to back_room. `rarityToDifficultyLabel(rarity)` returns a `DifficultyLabel` (`'simple'..'formidable'`) for the canonical `RichText` `:label:` colon-shortcode path (`parseSymbols.ts`'s `TIER_BASE_DIFFICULTY`) — never a hardcoded pip count. Composition verified via tsx script: shop-scale rolls for every archetype show average base rarity 2.07–2.50 against rarity ceiling 7 (common goods clearly dominating); legit-legality rolls show zero restricted lines; black-market rolls show restricted lines landing in the open tier across a 30-seed sample (69 total). |
