Idempotent — deletes `dataset_source='respec'` rows before reinserting (no unique constraint on `ref_sig_ability_nodes` to `ON CONFLICT` against, since the same `node_key` can legitimately appear at two non-adjacent columns in one row). 37 abilities, 333 nodes. One data anomaly: "Unmatched Teamwork" references career key `CLONE`, which doesn't exist in `ref_careers` (respec) — seeded as-is (no FK to block it), effectively unreachable until/unless that career exists. |
| 090 | Adds `character_sig_ability_nodes.spec_slot TEXT` — found missing while implementing `useCharacterSigAbilities`; migration 088's version had no column to record which specialization a locked-in signature ability belongs to, and a character can lock a different one per in-career spec. |
| 094 | reSpec Force Rating rule change: neither careers nor specializations auto-grant Force Rating anymore — `ref_careers.force_rating = 0` for all `dataset_source = 'respec'` rows (oggdude rollback rows untouched; previously 1 for the six F&D careers via migration 080). Adds `characters.force_rating_purchased BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false` — set when a player deliberately spends 10 XP to gain Force Rating 1 via `handlePurchaseForceRating` (`useCharacterData.ts`). Owning a Force-sensitive specialization (`ref_specializations.is_force_sensitive`) only makes a character *eligible* for that purchase (see `isEligibleForForceRating` in `src/lib/forceEligibility.ts`) — it no longer grants any Force Rating on its own, and the purchase can be deferred indefinitely. |
| 117 | `pending_actions` — durable pending-action queue for GM/system actions awaiting a player decision. Built because every player decision popup is delivered purely by Realtime broadcast (`usePlayerBroadcast.ts`) with no persistence and no catch-up query: a socket that is asleep or reconnecting when the GM fires the action silently drops it and the player sees nothing (the suspected cause of the macOS Safari reports). Popups continue to fire alongside the queue and both resolve as one. Full column/index/RLS detail in the Data Layer section above. Infrastructure only — no GM panel writes to it yet and no player UI reads it yet. |
| 118 | `character_hand_state` — schema-only foundation for the player-facing "hand of cards" (H1; talents/Force powers as physical cards over the HUD). `character_id uuid PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES characters(id)`, `card_order jsonb`, `discarded_keys jsonb`, `is_tucked boolean`, `updated_at`. RLS: permissive `USING (true)` on SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE, matching `pending_actions`' rationale (no GM/player auth split in this app — session ownership lives in `character_sessions.session_key`, not Postgres RLS). No DELETE policy. A character with no row is valid default state (`[]`/`[]`/`false`) — the future read hook must treat a missing row that way, not as an error; verified via anon-key REST that a nonexistent row returns `[]`, not an error. **No hook, component, or read/write logic yet** — schema only, H2 onward builds on it. |
| 120 | `character_hand_state.hand_placed_keys TEXT[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` — passive talents voluntarily placed into the hand fan, disjoint from `discarded_keys`. |

A follow-up pass added `ARMOR_KEY_ALIASES` (8 DB keys whose spelling diverges from the XML `<Key>` for the same item, e.g. `SITHPAIN` -> `SITHPAINHARNESS`, found by cross-checking `Name` on the rows each side reported as unmatched) and picked up 10 more armor rows (2 DB keys alias to the same XML row: `KATARNCOMM`/`MK1KATARN` -> `KATARNCLASSARMOR`, `ARC`/`PHASEIARC` -> `PHASEIARCTROOPERARMOR`) — armor now 60/97 matched, 0 unmatched source entries. Remaining nulls: `ref_armor` 57 rows (respec's own `<Categories/>` is empty for these, or the item has no respec equivalent at all — verified case by case, not a matching bug); `ref_gear` 44 rows + 9 unmatched source entries (droid/tool items with no DB row, not yet alias-mapped). Verified idempotent (repeat runs: 0 updated) and reachable via anon-key REST. |

Stores an image identifier, not a path — path construction stays in application code. | 125 | The Archive (item detail panel) Prompt 2: `ref_armor`/`ref_gear.worn_anchor` (`TEXT`, nullable). Populated ONLY for the 18 `encumbrance_bonus > 0` rows (8 armor + 10 gear — corrects Prompt 1's own audit, which mislabeled the gear section "11 rows" when it only ever listed 10; re-verified against the live DB with zero drift immediately before this migration). Never inferred from item name. Values: `body` (MK1KATARN, PHASEIARC, PIONEER, SURVIVALARMOR, BODYSUIT, PITCREWCOV — full-torso garments occupying the same space as a worn armor suit; keeping coveralls here closes the loophole where stacking coveralls with a flak vest would collect both bonuses), `rig` (HAULHARN, UTILITYVEST — carry rigs worn OVER other equipment), `back` (BACKPACK, MODPACK, MODPACK3, PACKMIL, SPDUFFEL — bulk packs; MODPACK3 has no justifying sentence in its own description, anchored here by product-line parity with MODPACK as an explicit judgement call, not a sourced fact), `shoulder` (SURVEYBAG, WIZPOUCH — strap bags competing with neither back nor waist), `waist` (JEDIUTILBELT, LOADBEAR, UTILBELT). Drives the two worn-rules exclusivity checks in `computeEncumbranceStats()` — see the `useCharacterData` entry above. Verified anon-key REST readable on both tables. Distinct from and unrelated to `character_*.equip_slot` (migration 113) — that field stays dormant (0 non-null rows, confirmed again before this migration) and deliberately non-exclusive per its own doc comment in `types.ts:548-550`, which this migration does not touch. |
| 124 | The Archive (item detail panel) Prompt 1: `ref_weapons`/`ref_armor`/`ref_gear.effect_text`, `.lore_text` (`TEXT`, nullable) — additive split of the existing `description` column into mechanical text (effect_text) and flavour text (lore_text), for the item detail panel rebuild. `description` itself is never modified/renamed/cleared; every existing surface reading it (`WeaponsCard.tsx`, `QuartermasterModal.tsx`, `ItemDatabaseTab.tsx`) keeps rendering unchanged. Backfilled by `scripts/backfill-item-descriptions.ts` (ref rows only, `is_custom IS NOT TRUE`; skips custom items). Split rule: strip the leading `[H3]...[h3]` title (duplicates the item name); text before the first `[P]` → `lore_text`; text after → `effect_text`, further split into paragraphs on subsequent `[P]` markers; any paragraph starting with `[B]Models Include[b]` (optionally preceded by `[BR]`, case-insensitive) is moved to the end of `lore_text` instead — flavour, not mechanics. Rows with no `[P]` at all: `lore_text` = full description, `effect_text` stays `NULL` (a valid "no effect statement" state, never guessed). Live counts: `ref_weapons` 319/491 split + 172 lore-only, `ref_armor` 103/112 split + 9 lore-only, `ref_gear` 496/610 split + 114 lore-only (90 Models-Include paragraphs rerouted). Verified anon-key REST readable on all three tables; verified `description` row counts unchanged after the run. `ItemEditor.tsx` now has two textareas — "Effect" (mechanical, optional, writes `effect_text`) and "Description" (flavour, writes `lore_text`) — each with its own RichText preview toggle; on save it also composes the legacy `description` column as `loreText + '[P]' + effectText` (or just one side if the other is empty) via `composeDescription()`, so unmigrated read surfaces keep working for new/edited custom items. Loading an existing item populates the two fields from `effect_text`/`lore_text` when either is present; an old custom item with neither (never edited since the split) loads its whole `description` into the Description field with Effect left empty. |

| 119 | `talent_skill_relevance` + `talent_skill_relevance_authored` — talent→skill relevance mapping, authored lazily at spec-purchase time (H1) rather than all at once, feeding the hand-of-cards check-time glow (H2+). **A different, partial version of this mapping already existed and is still live**: `ref_talents.modifiers->'relevant_skills'` (jsonb array), populated for 83/1170 active respec talents and 87/632 oggdude talents, already read by `HudModalsOverlay.tsx:235,241` for `SkillRollPopover`'s talent-hint feature — every skill key in it resolves against `ref_skills` (verified: zero unresolvable keys across both datasets). Rather than starting the new table empty and diverging from that authoring work, migration 119 backfills from it; `modifiers.relevant_skills` itself is untouched and `HudModalsOverlay`'s existing read path is unaffected — the new table is additive here, not a replacement. `talent_skill_relevance (talent_key, dataset_source, skill_key)`, composite PK, `skill_key REFERENCES ref_skills(key)`, composite FK `(talent_key, dataset_source) REFERENCES ref_talents(key, dataset_source)` (matches `ref_talents`' actual PK shape, confirmed via `pg_constraint`). **Unmapped-vs-deliberately-empty distinction**: a bare junction table can't express "reviewed, zero relevant skills" — a row requires a `skill_key`, so zero rows is overloaded to mean both "never reviewed" and "reviewed, found none," and a sentinel `skill_key` would either violate the FK to `ref_skills` or need a nullable column inside a composite PK (not legal in Postgres). Fixed with a second table, `talent_skill_relevance_authored (talent_key, dataset_source, authored_at)` — presence of a row means "reviewed," independent of how many rows (0, 1, or several) that talent has in `talent_skill_relevance`. Live-verified: marking `GRIT` reviewed-with-zero-skills via the anon-key REST path (matching the real hook's write path) correctly dropped Scout's unmapped-talent count from 16 to 15, while `DEDI` (genuinely untouched) kept reading `is_authored=false`; the test row was reverted after. Same permissive RLS convention as 118, on both tables, with an added DELETE policy. **The authoring hook** — `buySpecialization` (`src/hooks/useCharacterData.ts`), after the purchase fully completes (after `toast.success`, never awaited, wrapped in try/catch so it cannot affect purchase success or failure): computes the distinct talent-key set from the purchased spec's `talent_tree.rows[].talents`, resolves the active dataset via `fetchActiveDataset`, and diffs against `talent_skill_relevance_authored` for that dataset. Any unreviewed keys are `console.warn`'d by spec name — GM-facing, informational only. `pending_actions` was considered and rejected as the surface: it models durable actions awaiting a **player** decision with a resolution lifecycle, and this is a passive GM/authoring notice with no player-facing decision at all — reusing it would have been the wrong shape forced to fit. Live-verified against a real spec via direct SQL reproduction of the hook's exact query (not a live purchase — both real characters in this campaign are live PCs with real XP; spending one's XP to verify a console message wasn't proportionate): Scout resolves to 16 of its 18 distinct talents unreviewed (`OUTDOOR` and `KEENEYED` are the two backfilled-authored exceptions). Also confirmed unrelated and left untouched: `wireframe/TalentsPanel.tsx`'s 4-entry `PASSIVE_STAT_BONUSES` map (Toughened/Enduring/Grit/Dedication → stat-block bonus, e.g. Wound Threshold/Soak) — a hardcoded name-keyed stat-bonus lookup with zero skill relationship, not a parallel structure to this mapping. |
